1. **`kickoff NAME` refused (32.6).** A queued kickoff is kept out of the new kickoff's `{inbox}` block, and dropped (logged `dropped` via `kickoff`) only once the new kickoff was sent or unconfirmed. A `kickoff NAME` that is refused leaves the queued one in place, so the kickoff is not lost. `up`, `restart` and `swap` replace it as specified: the old one is dropped and the new one queued.
2. **Pull paths and `--now` (32.6).** `inbox --read`/`--wait` and the Stop hook deliver a queued kickoff through the same `messages.settle`, so they do the bookkeeping of a sent kickoff. The kickoff's `kickoff` log entry is written before the batch's `delivered` entries, so 20.8 settles only debts older than the kickoff, and the kickoff message also gets its own `delivered` entry. `send --now` and `deliver NAME --now` leave a queued kickoff in the mailbox. `last_kickoff_at` and `blocked_at_startup` are written by `state.mark_kicked_off`, a field-only update under the `.herdr` lock, and mirrored into a state the command already loaded, so the watcher never writes back a stale state file. Every kickoff log entry, sent directly or queued, now carries `reason`.
3. **Report level (32.6).** `NAME: kickoff queued #id: delivered when NAME is next idle or done` is partial (exit 3), like `not briefed`. The status label `(kickoff queued #id)` shows whenever one is queued and the seat is live; a seat still blocked at startup keeps `(blocked at startup)`. `status --json` adds `"kickoff_queued": "<short id>"` to that seat only.
4. **Handover success (32.5).** Success means the seat has a `started` result and no `blocked`, `refused` or `failed` result. A kickoff queued only because the guard held still counts as success, and blocked at startup stops. A swap that finds no drift any more when the helper runs ends like a success, with the notification `Fleet: NAME unchanged`.
5. **Handover options (32.5).** `--wait MS` sets `wait_ms` (default 3600000), counted from when the helper starts. `restart --handover --drop-inbox` is carried. `HERDR_FLEET_HANDOVER` is one JSON object: `{"op", "seat", "wait_ms", "note_file"?, "drop_inbox"?}`. `note_file` must be `.herdr/handover/<seat>-note.md`, which is removed when the helper ends; `handover/` joins the `.herdr/.gitignore` lines.
6. **Ending (32.5).** A stopped helper drops its record and closes its own recorded, created pane after the owner's Enter, as on success. A second handover is a `refused` report line (exit 3): `a handover is already waiting in PANE (OP SEAT); one at a time`.
7. **Failed starts (32.1).** The suffix `the pane is at a shell; run herdr-fleet up NAME` is added after a quit only when the failed start left the pane at a shell, and not when the detail already names `run herdr-fleet up NAME` (the lost-twice case). Cases 2 and 4 keep their own words. Every `start_seat` failure from up, restart and swap is notified; quit failures are not, apart from 32.2's held quit.
8. **A fresh `waiting.json` (32.4).** Fresh means the pid is alive and, when `timeout_ms` is set, `since + timeout_ms` has not passed. The file is removed only when it names the waiting process's own pid. `inbox_wait` appears in `status --json` only while a seat waits.
9. **The second start (32.1).** The retry goes through the normal start path: a fresh `process-info` read, then `agent start` only if the pane is still at its bare shell. When that read shows a process of the kind instead (the first command ran after all), the slow-start poll applies; anything else fails at once with `not retried`, without the 10 s busy wait. Case 4 reads `herdr error timeout: timed out waiting for agent startup; foreground in PANE: NAMES; not retried`. A late blocked agent reports `blocked at startup in PANE (not ready when the start timed out); ...`. Dry-run plans print the `agent start` command without `--timeout`, as before.
10. **Naming a Codex menu (32.2).** `seats.CHOICE_RE` also accepts Codex's `›` as the highlight mark. Without it, the example `("Approaching rate limits")` cannot be named on a Codex seat. The change makes `interrupt`'s dialog refusal and the board's blocked-dialog lines see Codex menus too; it only adds detections. In the quit window, a guard that passes runs `safe_prompt` again (state, guard, prompt); a dialog held again continues the window, and text in the input refuses at once.
11. **`peek` (32.3).** `op_peek` returns `(code, text, note)`; the CLI writes the note to stderr before the text, and the board shows it in the footer after `p`. `--source` takes `visible` or `recent`.
12. **The helper command (32.5).** `herdr-fleet handover` is a CLI command whose help says the pane runs it. It requires `HERDR_FLEET_HANDOVER` and runs only in the recorded pane for the recorded seat and operation, else exit 1 (`not the recorded handover pane`). An error in the helper is notified and waits for Enter, so the pane stays readable. After each wait it reads the state and fleet files again. When `agent wait` times out or fails, the helper stops without looping. The status line reads `handover: OP SEAT waiting in PANE since HH:MM`; `prune_state` drops a record whose pane is gone. `up` never chooses the helper pane, like the strip, as the pane to split from in a workspace the caller is not in (`bringup._first_pane`).
13. **Composing a queued kickoff (32.6).** The prompt is the kickoff's text with trailing newlines removed, a blank line, then the rest of the batch as usual. Above 16 KiB the kickoff still goes, moved and logged, with the pointer standing for the rest, which stays queued. The Stop hook composes the same way and gives a pointer for everything when over its caps.
14. **32.8.** `presence.read_screen` falls back to a plain `pane read` when herdr rejects `--format ansi` with a usage error (exit 2), so the guard still answers on a herdr without the option, without faint detection. `prompt_line` finds the line without escapes; for a faint-placeholder kind, if the glyph itself was drawn faint, what is left after dropping faint text is the line. `interrupt`'s Ctrl-U check reads `agent read NAME --lines 12 --format ansi`. `fake_herdr` decodes SGR 2/22/0 with its own code, so the harness judges a suggestion independently of the plugin.
15. **Harness (32.9).** `tests/support.py` takes the caller pane from the command's own marker when the helper ran (its pane, not the seat's). It counts `agent start` calls per pane per command, ignoring busy answers. It allows the board's second, `--source visible` read only right after the failed read of the same `p`. Restart and swap may open only the `handover` tab of a `--handover` command. Existing tests whose expectations this section changes were updated: the `agent start` argv now includes `--timeout 15000`; the guard's and interrupt's reads include `--format ansi`; the `.gitignore` lists include `handover/`; the version is 0.17.0; the manifest pane set includes `handover`; and a blocked or refused kickoff is now queued (`test_inbox`, `test_up`).

Second round, after the first review (FIX; findings 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 7 and non-blocking N1, N4, N5, N6 and N7 accepted by the designer, plus the designer's own wording from the live smoke):

16. **`--timeout` on an older herdr (finding 6).** `Herdr.agent_start` sends `--timeout 15000`. When herdr answers exit 2 (`usage`: it rejected the command line and started nothing), it sends the same start once more without `--timeout` and leaves the option off for the rest of that command. A later `timeout` is classified as before. `min_herdr_version` stays 0.9.4. The harness counts neither a busy answer nor a usage rejection as a start.
17. **Wording (N4).** The first retry reports `(second attempt; the first start left a bare shell)`. The lost-twice line is `the start command did not run in PANE twice, or the agent exited at once; a quit agent may have left mouse tracking on there (README: troubleshooting); run herdr-fleet up NAME`. A second attempt whose fresh read finds the first start's agent coming up reports a plain `started - in PANE`: no second start was issued.
18. **A slow start past its deadline (N5).** It is still `failed` and still notified, but its kickoff is queued as 32.6 queues a blocked one, and `blocked_at_startup` is recorded. The failed line then ends `look at the pane; its kickoff is queued (#id) and goes when it is idle`, and a `kickoff queued` line follows.
19. **Blocked at startup (the designer's live smoke).** Once its kickoff is queued, the blocked line ends `answer the prompt there; its kickoff is queued (#id) and goes when it is idle` instead of `then run herdr-fleet kickoff NAME`. With no kickoff queued (`up --no-kickoff`) the old words stay. `bringup._queue` rewrites the start's report line. The notifications sent at the start (`is waiting for you`, `did not start`) keep the words they were sent with.
20. **The mailbox during a quit (N1).** `ops._quit_live` holds the seat's mailbox lock from the quit's `safe_prompt` until the agent is gone or the quit fails, in restart, swap, down and so in the handover helper and the popup. The lock is released before the start and the kickoff, which take it again. Nothing in the quit takes a timer lock. A watcher, `deliver`, hook or pull delivery that waits on it then finds the agent gone and leaves the messages in `new/`, and the kickoff carries them.
21. **The helper's record (N7).** `op_handover` re-reads the state every 250 ms for up to 2 s until the handover record names its pane, seat and operation, before giving up with exit 1. When the wait times out or fails, the helper now prints its report line before it stops (a gap the new tests found).
22. **Harness (N6).** `assert_helper` also checks every `pane rename` the helper makes: never `--clear`, only a pane hosting a seat, and only to that seat's wanted name.
23. **Tests (findings 1, 2, 3 and 7).** Added both second-attempt branches (an agent coming up after all: slow poll, one start; `less`: failed, not retried, one start); `presence.read_screen`'s fallback (a usage error is read once as plain text and the prompt goes; any other error sends nothing, `presence check: …; nothing sent`); the helper's stop paths (the wait timing out, the wait failing, a held quit, a failed start), each keeping its pane until Enter; the `--timeout` usage fallback twice; the quit lock (a delivery from another thread during the quit is refused and its message rides the kickoff); the record retry; the blocked and slow wordings; and `up --no-kickoff` keeping the old remedy. The copied suggestion text is replaced by synthetic words with the live escape shape. In scratch copies of the build, I removed the quit lock, the `--timeout` fallback, the `--format` fallback, the record retry and each second-attempt branch in turn; each removal failed the tests that cover it.
24. **docs/testing.md (finding 5).** The coverage table has a `test_section32.py` row, and the fixtures list names section 32's fake behaviours and harness rules. A marked HTML comment holds the place for the designer's scratch acceptance steps.
25. **Not changed.** N2 (Claude Code's cursor cell over a suggestion) fails closed and needs a live check. N3 (the guard's `pane read` uses the default `recent` source) predates this section. N8: `ESCAPE_RE` does not strip nF/Fp escapes, so such a line holds; `ops._input_line` has no `--format` fallback, so on an older herdr `interrupt` presses no Ctrl-U. Both fail safe.

### 32.11 Verification and limits (designer, 31 Oct 2026)

- **Automated evidence.** After the review round, 1,075 tests pass on Python 3.9.6 and on 3.12.13, with compilation clean; 69 are new in `tests/test_section32.py`. Of the first 56, 42 failed against 0.16.0; the second round's fixes were shown to be guarded by mutation checks rather than red-first runs. Every scenario still runs through the section 7 call-log checks, which now also cover the second start, the handover helper's reach and the visible-source read.
- **Live setup.** herdr 0.9.5, Claude Code seats on Haiku 5.5 in a disposable fleet with its own workspace, in a scratch directory the owner had trusted before. The candidate ran through `HERDR_FLEET_BIN`, and the handover pane came from a copy of the tree registered for the test under a separate plugin id and unregistered afterwards. The released plugin, the launcher and every production fleet were untouched.
- **32.1.** After a clean `up` (4.7 s), the seat was taken down and synthetic junk (`10M35`, the shape of a mouse report) was left on its shell line. `up` then reported `started - in PANE (second attempt; the first start command did not run)` and briefed the seat, 19.3 s in all: one 15 s timeout, then a start detected at once. A real mouse-mode pollution and the README's shell remedy were not reproduced live.
- **32.2.** With Claude Code's own `/model` menu open, herdr reported the seat `done`. `restart` held for the 5 s window, then refused with `a dialog or a menu is on screen in PANE ("2.  Opus 5.5 …"); answer or close it there, then run the command again` (0.16.0: the same refusal without a name). Limit: the named line is the first non-blank line of the last 16 lines, so a menu taller than that is named by its first visible entry, not its title.
- **32.3.** On a busy alternate-screen Claude seat, 0.16.0's `peek` failed with `agent_not_idle`; the candidate printed its 22 visible lines with `(working: the visible screen only)` on stderr. The guard's own `pane read` was accepted on every working seat checked; only `agent read` is refused, so `send --now` is not affected.
- **32.4.** While the seat was working and an `inbox --wait` ran for it, `status` showed `working (inbox wait <1m)` and the JSON carried `inbox_wait`; the waiting file was gone after the wait timed out.
- **32.5.** Armed from the seat's own pane while it was working, `swap NAME --handover` returned at once and opened the helper in a new tab of the seat's workspace without moving the owner's focus. The helper's environment carried `HERDR_PANE_ID`, `HERDR_PLUGIN_ENTRYPOINT_ID=handover`, `HERDR_FLEET_FILE` and the handover JSON. It waited for the turn to end, swapped the seat to the new arguments, briefed it, dropped its state record and closed its own pane, 12 s after arming. The stop paths (blocked seat, held quit, failed start) and the second-handover refusal are automated evidence only.
- **32.6.** A seat started in an untrusted directory stopped at Claude Code's folder-trust prompt. `up` reported it blocked and queued its kickoff (`kickoff queued #id`, `queued_by: kickoff`), and `deliver` left it queued while the seat was blocked. The prompt was never answered: the pane was closed. Delivery once the seat is idle is automated evidence only.
- **32.8.** Read-only, on idle Claude seats across the owner's fleets: 0.16.0's guard held two of them (`text in its input …`) whose prompt line was a faint suggestion (`❯\xa0\e[2m…`), one of them this fleet's own builder; the candidate let both through. The four other idle Claude seats passed in both versions.
- **Second round.** On the reviewed code, a fresh scratch seat repeated 32.1 (19.2 s, with the new wording), a plain `restart` through the quit lock (6.2 s), and the 32.5 handover swap end to end. The handover pane found its own record, so the helper's preflight inside a tab plugin pane resolves that pane (review 5a).
- **A pre-existing window, not new.** One `down` sent about 3 s after a kickoff found the seat still reported idle, and Claude Code dropped the `/exit` it received mid-turn; the plugin reported `did not exit within 20000 ms after /exit; … seat left alone`, as section 6 requires. 0.16.0 has the same window. docs/testing.md warns about it.
- **Quit lock and the Stop hook.** The quit holds the seat's mailbox lock for up to the 5 s window plus `quit_timeout_ms`. The opt-in Claude Code Stop hook (18.7) waits on the same lock, so if a seat's hook is still running when its quit starts, the `/exit` waits in Claude Code's input until the hook gives up at its 10 s timeout, having moved nothing; the quit then succeeds within the default 20 s. A fleet with `quit_timeout_ms` under about 11 s and hooks installed could see that quit fail. Not changed in this release (second review).
- **Not verified live.** A real Codex menu (the `›` highlight is synthetic in the fake), herdr 0.9.4's handling of `--format` and `--timeout` (both have usage fallbacks, simulated in the fake only), a real mouse-mode pollution and the shell remedy, the faint suggestion on a focused pane (the guard may read a cursor cell as typed and hold, which fails closed), Python 3.8, and the stop paths named above.

## 33. Queues that read true: FYI expiry, split counts, unread mailboxes, old debts (0.18.0)

An audit of running fleets found that most queued messages were FYIs several days old, a few were requests to seats that were working at the time, and two requests sat in a mailbox named for no seat or contact, left over from before 31's routing. 18.3 lets an FYI travel only with the next real delivery to its mailbox, and FYIs carry no ttl by default, so a quiet seat keeps them indefinitely. `status` and the board print one count per mailbox, so a quiet fleet looked blocked. The same audit found fulfilled requests still listed as owed, one of them misattributed to `owner`, and an unread hint that drops `--fleet`. Nothing in this section prompts a seat or presses a key; it changes what is counted, what is printed, and when an FYI is moved to `cur/` undelivered.

### 33.1 FYIs expire after `fyi_ttl`

A new top-level fleet file key, `fyi_ttl`, takes the `--ttl` grammar (`30m`, `2h`, `1d`) or `"off"`; the default is `"24h"`. An FYI (`fyi: true`) without a `ttl` of its own is due once the fleet's `fyi_ttl` has passed since the later of its `time` and its file's modification time. A message's own `ttl` always wins, in either direction (`send --fyi --ttl 3d` keeps it for three days). A message that is not an FYI never gets the default, and neither does a queued kickoff (32.6) or timer mail.

The rule is evaluated where 18.4's ttl is evaluated today (`inbox.is_due` and its callers), against the fleet that holds the mailbox, so it applies at once to FYIs queued before 0.18.0: they carry `time`, so no migration is needed. Read-only paths (`status`, the board, `doctor`, the unread note) stop counting a due FYI; nothing moves there.

Moving it to `cur/`, logged `expired` with `via: "fyi_ttl"` (`delivery: "none"`, the shape of 18.4's ttl expiry, including whatever 31 mirrors for a routed message's terminal record), happens on the paths that already run `expire_due` under the mailbox lock (delivery, kickoff, `inbox`, `deliver`, the hooks). It also happens in a new watcher sweep: at most once every `EXPIRE_SWEEP_S` (300 s), between passes, for every mailbox under `.herdr/inbox/` that has files in `new/` (seats, contacts, `owner` and any other name). The sweep takes each mailbox's lock without waiting and skips a busy mailbox until the next sweep. It takes no timer lock, runs no herdr command and creates no directory.

An expired FYI creates no debt and settles none, as today. Its first line stays in `log`, and its body in `cur/<id>.md` and on the board's message page (`inbox --history` lists delivered messages only, 20.1). `inbox --redeliver ID` queues it again with a fresh window: `--redeliver` sets the file's modification time to now. That is metadata only; message files are still never rewritten. A message past its own `ttl` keeps that ttl when redelivered, and the confirmation says it will expire again.

### 33.2 Counts tell FYIs from requests

Everywhere a queue is counted, FYIs are counted apart from the rest. A mailbox's actionable count is its queued messages that are not FYIs; a queued kickoff counts as actionable.

- **`status`:** `queued: sample-dev 2 (oldest 9m, +1 fyi), sample-reviewer 1 fyi`. The age is that of the oldest actionable message, or of the oldest FYI in an FYI-only mailbox. JSON keeps `queued` (every queued message, for compatibility) and `queued_oldest`, and adds `queued_fyi` ({mailbox: count}).
- **The board:** the queue cell counts actionable messages and names the next actionable one (`2 queued, oldest 3m  #id "…"`), followed by ` +1 fyi` in the dim style. An FYI-only row shows `1 fyi`, dim, and none of the styling that marks waiting work. The footer's `queued:` line uses the status form.
- **The unread note:** the 23.1 note counts both, as `2 unread (+1 fyi) from …`.

### 33.3 A mailbox nobody reads is named, and can be dropped

A mailbox is **unread** when it has queued messages that are not due, and its name is no seat, no contact and not `owner` of the fleet.

- **`status`:** its queued entry ends `(no seat or contact)`.
- **`doctor`:** it warns, read-only: `inbox: NAME holds N queued (oldest AGE); it is no seat or contact here, so the watcher does not deliver it. Read it with herdr-fleet inbox --fleet PATH --as NAME --read, deliver it with herdr-fleet deliver NAME if NAME is a live agent, or (NAME or the owner) drop it with herdr-fleet inbox --fleet PATH --as NAME --drop`. Such a mailbox can be a live queue: 31 keeps a message for a live agent that has no fleet of its own in the sender's fleet, under the agent's name.

`inbox --drop` is new and exclusive with `--read`, `--count`, `--history`, `--redeliver`, `--hook` and `--wait`. Under the mailbox lock, it moves every queued message of the selected mailbox to `cur/`, each logged `dropped` with `via: "inbox --drop"`. It sends nothing and prints one line per message: `dropped #id from SENDER (AGE): first line`, with ` (queued kickoff)` added for a 32.6 kickoff. `inbox --redeliver ID` brings one back.

- `--older-than DURATION` keeps messages queued less than DURATION ago.
- `--fyi` limits the drop to FYIs.
- `--dry-run` lists what would go and moves nothing.

The mailbox is selected as for `--read`: the caller, or `--as NAME`. `--drop` with `--as NAME` also runs the caller lookup, ignoring `--as`, for every mailbox, unread ones included. It drops a mailbox only when the caller resolves to that name, or to `owner` (outside Herdr or a verified shell pane, as `send` resolves it). Otherwise it is a usage error (exit 2, nothing moved): `only NAME or the owner drops NAME's mailbox`. `drop_queued` (`--drop-inbox`) and `inbox --drop` share one helper.

### 33.4 Old debts are summarised, and one command closes them

`ack --older-than DURATION --reason TEXT`, with neither a NAME nor an id, closes every debt in this fleet's log whose request was delivered more than DURATION ago, whoever owes it. Each is logged as 23.4's `ack #id --reason` is, with `by` set to the caller. `--dry-run` lists them first. A NAME in that form is a usage error (exit 2, nothing written). So is a caller that does not resolve to `owner` (outside Herdr or a verified shell pane): `closing others' debts in bulk is the owner's; ack #id --reason TEXT closes one`. `ack --older-than DURATION` without `--reason` keeps its present meaning (the debts you owe).

`status` lists debts whose request is older than `STALE_DEBT_S` (3 days) not one per line but in one closing line: `awaiting: N older than 3d (oldest AGE), not listed; see herdr-fleet log --owed; the owner closes them with herdr-fleet ack --older-than 3d --reason TEXT (--dry-run first)`. JSON lists every debt, as now, with `stale: true` on those. The board keeps its present debt display.

### 33.5 A printed `herdr-fleet inbox` command keeps the fleet

Every `herdr-fleet inbox …` command line the CLI prints for its caller to run gains `--fleet PATH` only when it is needed: when discovery from the caller's cwd, ignoring `--fleet`, `--file` and `HERDR_FLEET_FILE`, would select another fleet file or none. This covers the 23.1 unread note, the owner's history hint, and 33.3's doctor lines. One helper builds the flag. Prompts typed to seats are unchanged: a seat runs them from its own cwd.

### 33.6 Code and tests

- `inbox.is_due` takes the fleet's FYI window, and every caller passes it.
- `fleetfile` parses `fyi_ttl`.
- The sweep goes in `watch.py`, before or after each pass, never inside a mailbox lock it already holds.
- The counts go in `checks.queued_summary`/`queued_line`, `board` (`Row`, `queue_text`, the footer) and `messages.inbox_note`.
- Unread mailboxes go in `checks` (status and doctor).
- `inbox --drop` goes in `mailroom.py`, `ack --older-than --reason` in `messages.py`, and the fleet flag helper beside `inbox_note`.
- Section 7 is amended where it lists what moves a queued message undelivered: ttl, `--drop-inbox`, and now `fyi_ttl` and `inbox --drop`.
- README: the fleet file reference (`fyi_ttl`), `inbox --drop`, the `ack` form, and troubleshooting ("my fleet shows queued messages"). AGENTS.md architecture is updated in the same change. Version 0.18.0 in both declarations.

Tests go in `tests/test_section33.py`. Each of these fails on 0.17.0:

1. An FYI older than 24 h without a ttl is not counted by `status` or the board, and the watcher's sweep expires it and logs it with `via: "fyi_ttl"`.
2. A younger FYI is kept.
3. `fyi_ttl = "off"` keeps every FYI.
4. An FYI's own `--ttl` wins both ways.
5. A non-FYI request and a queued kickoff never expire by `fyi_ttl`.
6. The sweep skips a locked mailbox, runs no herdr command and makes no directory in a fleet without an inbox.
7. The split counts appear in status, JSON, the board cell, the footer and the unread note.
8. An unread mailbox is flagged in status and doctor, and doctor writes nothing.
9. `inbox --drop`: all, `--fyi`, `--older-than`, `--dry-run`, a queued kickoff named, and `--redeliver` afterwards.
10. `ack --older-than --reason`: closes others' old debts, keeps young ones, gives a usage error with a NAME, and `--dry-run` writes nothing.
11. Status collapses stale debts into the one line, and JSON flags them.
12. The unread note and doctor lines carry `--fleet` from another fleet's cwd, and not when discovery agrees.

`tests/support.py` checks every scenario, as before; no new herdr call is allowed from the sweep.

### 33.7 Amendments from the build

Built into the working tree on 1 November 2026 (version 0.18.0).

1. **Section 7 had no such list.** No invariant listed what moves a queued message undelivered, so invariant 11 gains the sentence: its own ttl, `fyi_ttl`, `--drop-inbox`, `inbox --drop`, a newer kickoff replacing a queued one (32.6), a timer retiring its own message (30), and the refusal of the `send --now` that wrote it.
2. **`fyi_ttl` values (33.1).** Any `--ttl` duration, or `"off"`. Anything else (`"0m"`, `"1w"`, `24`) is a fleet file error (exit 2), as for the other keys. The parsed value is `Fleet.fyi_ttl` in seconds, or None when off.
3. **`is_due` (33.1).** `inbox.is_due(message, now, fyi_ttl)` takes the window as a required argument, and so does `inbox.queued(repo, fyi_ttl, now)`. `inbox.due_by` names the reason, `ttl` or `fyi_ttl`, and `expire_due` logs it as `via`. The window counts from the `time` header. A message with timer metadata or `queued_by: kickoff` never takes it; neither is an FYI today, so this only guards the future.
4. **The sweep (33.1).** `mailroom.sweep_expired` does the sweeping and `Watcher.sweep` paces it. It runs in each pass after `timers.cycle` has released its locks and before the mailboxes are read; the first pass sweeps. A busy mailbox is skipped through `inbox.try_locked`, a non-blocking flock. The sweep creates no inbox and no mailbox directory. Like every expiry, it creates the mailbox's `.lock` file and, for a mailbox that never had one, its `cur/`. The watcher says `expired N queued: NAME n, … (ttl or fyi_ttl)`. `watch --once` and `deliver` keep `expire_all`, which waits for each lock as before and now applies `fyi_ttl` too.
5. **Redeliver, as written (33.1).** A redelivered message gets no new window. So an FYI past `fyi_ttl`, or any message past its own ttl, goes back to `cur/` at the next expiry, which includes the `inbox --read` that would have read it. The confirmation says so: `#id is queued again for NAME, but it is past its fyi_ttl, so the next delivery or read expires it again; its text is in PATH`. See the builder's report: this makes `--redeliver` useless for an expired FYI.
6. **`inbox --history` (33.1).** It lists delivered messages only (20.1), so an expired FYI is not in it. The expired message's first line stays in `log`, and its body stays in `cur/<id>.md` and on the board's message page. Not changed.
7. **Status counts (33.2).** Each part of the `queued:` line comes from `inbox.count_label`.
   - The forms are `NAME A (oldest AGE, +F fyi)`, and `NAME F fyi (oldest AGE)` when only FYIs wait. Following 20.8, the age is shown only for two or more messages of the kind counted. An unread mailbox adds ` (no seat or contact)` after that.
   - JSON `queued_fyi` lists every mailbox in `queued`, with 0 where it holds no FYI.
   - JSON also gains `queued_oldest_actionable` ({mailbox: time}, only for mailboxes with a message that is not an FYI), which the line's age needs, and `queued_unread` ([names]).
   - `queued` and `queued_oldest` keep their meaning: every message that is not due.
8. **The board (33.2).**
   - `Row.queued` counts the messages that are not FYIs, and `Row.fyi` the FYIs. `oldest` and `next_*` describe the former, and urgency ranks only by them.
   - The queue cell is `queue_spans`: `queue_text`, then ` +N fyi` in the dim style, dropped when it does not fit beside `N queued`. An FYI-only row shows `N fyi`, dim, in both the wide and the narrow cell.
   - The board's footer has no `queued:` line; the only one is on the fleet page (25.3). That line now uses the status form (`Board.queue_counts`), including other mailboxes with their `(no seat or contact)`. `Board.owner_fyi` is added.
9. **The unread note (33.2).** It reads `N unread (+F fyi) from …` when anything but FYIs waits, and `F unread fyi from …` when only FYIs do. The `#id, HH:MM` is the oldest message that is not an FYI, else the oldest FYI. The senders still come from every message, and `it`/`them` follows the total.
10. **Doctor (33.3).** The check is named `inbox`, one `warn` per unread mailbox, in status order. N counts every message that is not due, and AGE is always shown. It reads the mailboxes only.
11. **`inbox --drop` (33.3).**
    - **Exclusions.** It is exclusive (an argparse group) with `--read`, `--count`, `--history`, `--redeliver` and `--hook`, and a usage error with `--wait`, `--json` or `--full`. `--fyi`, `--older-than` and `--dry-run` are usage errors without `--drop`, and with `--hook`.
    - **Expiry.** Like every `inbox` mode, it first expires what is due (logged `expired`), then drops. `--dry-run` lists only messages that are not due.
    - **`--older-than D`.** It drops messages queued strictly more than D ago, as `ack --older-than` counts.
    - **Output.** Lines come in delivery order, AGE is from `time`, and the first line is shown without its `[from …]` prefix, as everywhere. With nothing to drop it prints `nothing to drop for NAME`.
    - **Shared helper.** `messages.drop_messages(ctx, name, via, keep)` is shared. `drop_queued` (`--drop-inbox`) keeps its count, its `via: drop-inbox` and its old behaviour of not expiring first.
12. **`ack --older-than D --reason TEXT` (33.4).**
    - **Usage.** An id in that form is a usage error, like a NAME; `--all --reason` stays one.
    - **Which debts.** They come from every open request (`unsettled`), not only the latest per pair, as `ack --older-than` does.
    - **Messages without an id.** Such a message (before 0.6.0) is closed with a name `ack` from its debtor, carrying `by` and `reason`. It is skipped when a newer message of the same pair is still owed.
    - **Output.** Each line reads `acked #id from SENDER since …, AGE ago (first line), owed by DEBTOR; reason: TEXT`; with none, `nothing owed older than D`.
    - **Spec change.** `test_section23.AckReason` asserted that `--older-than 1m --reason x` is a usage error; it now passes a NAME.
13. **Stale debts (33.4).** `checks.STALE_DEBT_S` is 3 days. `status` lists the fresh debts with their hints, then the one line. Every JSON `owed` item carries `stale`, false on fresh ones. `log --owed` and the board are unchanged.
14. **The fleet flag (33.5).**
    - **Rule.** `messages.fleet_flag(ctx)` decides it. A fleet chosen by `--fleet`, `--file` or `HERDR_FLEET_FILE` always gets the flag, as written, even where discovery would agree. Otherwise it compares discovery from the process cwd, ignoring `HERDR_FLEET_FILE`, by real path. The path is shell-quoted.
    - **Coverage.** The flag is added to the unread note, the owner's history hint and doctor's lines. It is also added to the watcher's `delivery to NAME unconfirmed` notification (`herdr-fleet inbox --redeliver ID`), which 33.5 does not name but which is a command for the owner to run.
15. **Existing tests changed by the spec.**
    - `test_section24.Build`: the dev row counts 2, plus 1 FYI, and its cell reads `2 queued, holding: owner typing +1 fyi`.
    - `test_section32.Help`: `inbox`'s help names `--drop`.
    - `test_section23.AckReason`: see item 12.
    - `test_section31`: the version.

Second round, after the designer's decisions of 1 November 2026 (A to F). Items 16, 18, 19 and 20 replace items 5, 14, 11 (who may drop) and 12 (who may close in bulk) where they differ.

16. **A redelivered FYI gets a fresh window (A).**
    - **Window start.** `inbox.due_by` counts the `fyi_ttl` window from the later of `time` and the file's modification time. A message object that was never loaded from a file counts from `time`.
    - **The touch.** Under the mailbox lock, right after the move to `new/`, `--redeliver` calls `inbox.touch`. That sets the modification time to `inbox.now_utc()` with `os.utime`: metadata only, the file is not rewritten.
    - **Own ttl.** A message's own `ttl` is never changed. A redelivered message past it gets `#id is queued again for NAME, but it is past its ttl, so the next delivery or read expires it again`; any other gets the line it always had.
17. **What reads the modification time (A).**
    - **Only `Message.sort_key`.** It breaks ties between messages whose ids share the same second. `read_box` uses that key to order `new/` (after the kickoff and urgent ranks) and `cur/`, and `all_messages` uses it too.
    - **Nothing else.** Routing (`crossfleet`), timers, the message log, owed replies and the board's caches do not read it.
    - **The one effect.** A redelivered message now sorts after the messages whose ids share its second; before, it kept its write order. Order across seconds and the kickoff and urgent ranks are unchanged. `test_a_redelivered_message_sorts_after_its_same_second_siblings` pins this.
    - **Copied files.** Files copied with fresh modification times, for example `.herdr/` restored with `cp`, get a fresh window too. That keeps FYIs longer, the safe direction.
    - **Test helper.** `test_section33`'s `put` sets the modification time to the message's `time`, as a real send leaves it. `test_inbox.put` is unchanged; its files carry the time they were written, so an FYI it backdates is not due by `fyi_ttl`.
18. **The fleet flag only when needed (D).** `messages.fleet_flag` compares discovery from the caller's cwd, ignoring `--fleet`, `--file` and `HERDR_FLEET_FILE`, with the fleet in use, by real path. A fleet chosen explicitly adds nothing by itself. The watcher's unconfirmed-delivery notification keeps the flag.
19. **Who drops with `--as` (E).**
    - **Rule.** `mailroom.may_drop` runs for `inbox --drop --as NAME` before anything is listed or moved, `--dry-run` included.
    - **Unread mailbox.** A mailbox that is no seat, contact or `owner` of the fleet needs no lookup at all ("for anyone"), so dropping one runs no herdr command.
    - **Seat, contact or `owner`.** `lookup_caller(ctx, None)` runs, ignoring `--as`: `pane current`, `agent get`, and for a pane without an agent `pane get` and `process-info`. The drop goes ahead only when the caller is NAME or `owner`.
    - **Refusal wording.** For the owner's own mailbox the refusal reads `only the owner drops owner's mailbox`, since the general form would read "only owner or the owner".
    - **Harness.** `tests/support.py` lets an `inbox` command that has both `--drop` and `--as` run the caller lookup; every other `inbox --as` still runs no herdr command.
20. **The bulk `ack` is the owner's (F).**
    - **Lookup.** `ack --older-than D --reason TEXT` runs the preflight inside Herdr, then the caller lookup, ignoring `--as`. It goes ahead only for `owner`, outside Herdr or at a verified shell pane, and `by` is `owner`.
    - **`--as`.** `--as` is accepted and ignored in that form, so `--as owner` from a seat is refused like any other seat.
    - **Check order.** The NAME and id usage errors come first, then the owner check, and nothing is written on either.
    - **Status line.** It now ends `the owner closes them with herdr-fleet ack --older-than 3d --reason TEXT (--dry-run first)`.
21. **Tests, round 2.**
    - `test_section33.py` has 38 tests:
      - a redelivered FYI survives the next sweep and a read, then expires again after its fresh window;
      - a message past its own ttl keeps it;
      - the same-second order;
      - a seat refused for another seat's and the owner's mailbox, `--dry-run` included;
      - the seat itself, the owner outside Herdr and the owner at a verified shell allowed;
      - anyone dropping an unread mailbox, with no lookup;
      - `--drop` without `--as`;
      - a seat refused for the bulk `ack`, with and without `--as owner`, and the owner at a verified shell closing the debts;
      - no flag where discovery agrees, with `--fleet` or with `HERDR_FLEET_FILE`.
    - The round-1 tests that dropped or closed in bulk with `--as` from a seat's environment now run as the owner outside Herdr.
    - Mutation checks in scratch copies: removing the touch, the window's modification-time term, `may_drop` or the bulk owner check each fails the tests that cover it.

Third round, after the second review (FIX). Items 22 and 23 replace item 19's rule for an unread mailbox and item 10's doctor wording; item 29 replaces item 21's test of an unread mailbox dropped with no lookup.

22. **Who drops, for every mailbox.**
    - **Rule.** `mailroom.may_drop` runs the caller lookup for every `inbox --drop --as NAME`, ignoring `--as`, a mailbox that is no seat, contact or `owner` included. It drops only when the caller resolves to NAME or to `owner`. Otherwise it is exit 2, `only NAME or the owner drops NAME's mailbox`, and nothing is moved or logged, `--dry-run` included.
    - **Why.** Such a mailbox can be 31's queue for a live agent with no fleet of its own: `crossfleet.destination` finds no fleet, so the message is queued in the sender's fleet under the agent's name.
    - **Cost.** Dropping an unread mailbox now runs the lookup's herdr commands, as dropping any other mailbox with `--as` does. `tests/support.py` already allows them for `inbox --drop --as`.
23. **Doctor's wording.**
    - **The line.** It is 33.3's, with NAME, N and AGE filled in: `… so the watcher does not deliver it. Read it with herdr-fleet inbox --as NAME --read, deliver it with herdr-fleet deliver NAME if NAME is a live agent, or (NAME or the owner) drop it with herdr-fleet inbox --as NAME --drop`.
    - **"if NAME is a live agent" is printed as written.** Doctor does not check whether NAME is live. It reads the mailboxes only (item 10) and runs no `agent list` for this check.
    - **Deviation: `deliver` keeps the fleet too.** 33.3 shows `herdr-fleet deliver NAME` without `--fleet PATH`. The build gives it the same `fleet_flag` as the two `inbox` commands. Without the flag, doctor run with `--fleet` from another cwd would print a `deliver` command that acts on another fleet or finds none.
    - **Unchanged and docs.** The status suffix stays `(no seat or contact)`. The README's status, doctor, `inbox --drop` and troubleshooting text say the same. A seat that tries to close others' debts in bulk, or to drop any mailbox but its own, is refused.
24. **A routed FYI (reviewer's note).** A test covers it: an FYI routed to another fleet expires through that fleet's sweep, and both logs gain the `expired` entry with `via: "fyi_ttl"` and `delivery: "none"`. No code changed. `expire_due` records through `messages.record`, which already mirrors terminal records (31).
25. **The fleet flag (reviewer's note).** A test covers it: the same fleet file reached through a symlinked cwd, or named by a symlinked `--fleet` path, gets no flag; `--fleet PATH` from a sibling directory with no `.herdr` gets it. No code changed.
26. **Timer mail and the sweep (reviewer's note).**
    - **Rule.** `messages.expire_due` takes `timer_mail` (default true). The sweep passes false, so a message with timer metadata stays in `new/` until `timers.cycle` expires it, with its journal record and its `timer_event` (30).
    - **The race it closes.** In the watcher, the cycle runs just before the sweep. A timer message whose deadline falls between the cycle's clock reading and the sweep's could otherwise be expired by the sweep. The cycle would then find it in `cur/` and record the occurrence `settled`, not `expired`.
    - **Other paths unchanged.** Delivery, kickoff, `inbox`, `deliver`, the hooks and `watch --once`'s `expire_all` still expire timer mail past its own ttl. Design 30 requires this: "Retain mailbox TTL enforcement for every delivery path".
27. **Board fields (reviewer's note).**
    - **Removed.** `Board.owner_fyi`, `owner_count` and `owner_oldest` are gone. Since round 1 only tests read them; the fleet page reads `Board.queue_counts`.
    - **Tests.** `test_section24` no longer passes or asserts `owner_count`; its build test checks `owner 1` in `queue_counts` instead.
    - **Kept.** `owner_id`, `owner_from` and `owner_line` are also read only by that test. They are kept, since the request named the three above.
28. **The sweep's line (the designer's live check).** `mailroom.sweep_expired` returns {mailbox: the reason of each message it expired}. The watcher names the reasons that applied, in the order `ttl, fyi_ttl`: `expired 1 queued: sample-dev 1 (fyi_ttl)`, `… (ttl)` or `… (ttl, fyi_ttl)`. The README gives the new form, and says the sweep leaves a timer's message to the timer pass.
29. **Tests, round 3.**
    - **`test_section33.py` has 43 tests.** "anyone dropping an unread mailbox, with no lookup" became: a seat refused for an unread mailbox, `--dry-run` included, and the owner dropping it.
    - **New tests:**
      - a live agent `helper` with no fleet, sent a message by `sample-dev` while working: a seat refused (nothing moved or logged), then `helper` itself and the owner allowed;
      - the watcher naming `(ttl)`, `(fyi_ttl)` and `(ttl, fyi_ttl)`;
      - the routed FYI;
      - the sweep leaving timer mail to the cycle;
      - the symlinked cwd and the sibling directory.
    - **Mutation checks in scratch copies.** Each of these fails the tests that cover it: restoring the unread exemption in `may_drop`, letting the sweep expire timer mail, the fixed `(ttl or fyi_ttl)` text, and comparing fleet paths without `realpath`. The routed FYI test covers behaviour that already worked and was not shown failing first.

### 33.8 Verification and limits (designer, 1 November 2026)

Live, on scratch fleets with an absent seat (so nothing could be prompted) and `fyi_ttl = "1m"`, running the reviewed working tree:

- **Sweep.**
  - After the window passed, `status` counted only the request; it moves nothing.
  - The watcher's first pass printed `expired 1 queued: sample-dev 1 (fyi_ttl)` and moved the FYI to `cur/`, logged `expired` with `via: "fyi_ttl"`.
  - The request stayed in `new/`, and the watcher reported `absent, 1 left queued`.
- **Unread mailbox.**
  - `status` printed `stray 1 (no seat or contact)`, and `doctor` printed the 33.3 warning.
  - From the fleet's own directory, the warning's commands carried no `--fleet`. From another directory, all three carried `--fleet PATH`.
- **Drop gate.** From a pane whose agent is neither the mailbox's name nor `owner`, `inbox --as stray --drop` was refused, with `--dry-run` and without it: exit 2, `only stray or the owner drops stray's mailbox`. The mailbox was unchanged and nothing was logged.

**Not verified live:**
- the owner's own drop and bulk `ack` (automated tests only);
- a routed FYI expiring at its destination;
- the board's dim FYI styling on a real terminal.

## 34. Kimi quits with `/exit` (0.18.1)

`kimi` joins the default quit table (section 6) with `/exit`. Before this, a seat with `kind = "kimi"` and no `quit` key was refused by `restart`, `swap` and `down`, and `doctor` warned about it, so every Kimi seat needed `quit = "/exit"` in its fleet entry. A seat's own `quit` key and the plugin config's `[quit]` table still take precedence, as for every kind.

Verified live on 2 November 2026 (herdr 0.9.5, Kimi Code CLI 2.4.1). With no `quit` key on a Kimi seat, `doctor` printed `ok quit …: /exit (kimi)`, and `restart` reported `quit with /exit`, started the seat in the same pane and briefed it.

## 35. Provider tiers and failover on a limit (0.19.0)

When a seat hits its provider's usage limit, a turn fails and every later delivery fails the same way. The owner asked, on 2 November 2026, for a way to move such a seat to another provider with a similar model. That is a notification and one command; nothing restarts on its own. Invariant 12 stands: the watcher never starts, restarts or quits anything.

### 35.1 Tiers in the fleet file

A top-level `[tiers.NAME]` table maps kinds to complete argument lists, so a tier is one capability level across providers. Its keys are herdr kinds; each value is an array of strings, or a table `{ args = [...], quit = "..." }`.

```toml
[tiers.strong]
claude = ["--model", "opus", "--effort", "high"]
codex  = ["-m", "gpt-6.1-sol", "-c", "model_reasoning_effort=high"]
kimi   = ["-m", "kimi-code/k3.1-pro", "-y"]
```

A seat uses either `kind` and `args`, as today, or `tier` with `prefer`, an ordered list of kinds that must all appear in the tier. The two forms are exclusive, and mixing them is a fleet file error. `prefer[0]` is the seat's primary.

Optional keys on a tier seat:
- `extra_args = { kimi = ["--add-dir", "…"] }` appends per-kind flags after the tier's arguments.
- `quit` overrides the quit command for every kind.

Everything that reads a seat's kind and args uses the effective pair. `up`, `restart` and the drift check use the primary. Once a seat has failed over, they use the kind it was swapped to (35.3).

### 35.2 Detecting a limit

The watcher detects limits in two ways.

**The screen, when a turn ends.** When a seat's wait returns `idle` or `done`, the watcher reads `agent read NAME --lines 12` once. It matches the lines against `seats.LIMIT_PATTERNS`, a table of regular expressions per kind, and it keeps the lines nowhere.
- The patterns start from messages seen live:
  - Claude Code: `usage limit` and `Credit balance too low`.
  - Codex: `hit your usage limit`.
  - Kimi Code CLI 2.4.1: `[provider.api_error] 429 You've reached your 5-hour usage limit` (seen 2 November 2026, in full below).

  A kind with no patterns is not read.
- **Kimi's limit leaves the seat `working`.** Kimi printed the error and returned to an empty input box, but herdr kept reporting the seat `working`, because its integration reports no turn end after an API error. So the turn-end read alone never sees a Kimi limit. The screen check therefore also runs for a seat that has been `working` for more than 5 minutes with mail queued for it, at most once every 5 minutes. With such a seat stuck `working`, `swap` refuses it: bring it down by hand (`/exit` in its pane), then `up NAME`. The full message was:

  ```
  Error: [provider.api_error] 429 You've reached your 5-hour usage limit. Your quota will reset when the current 5-hour
  window ends. To continue now, purchase extra usage or upgrade your plan: https://www.kimi.com/membership/subscription?tab=quota
  ```

  It names no reset time. Kimi's window is 5 hours, so the limited record carries `resets_hint: "5h window"`, and the reset notification fires 5 hours after the first match.
- A match marks the seat **limited** in the state file: kind, the matched line, the time, and a reset time when the line names one.
- The watcher then holds the seat's queued mail and does not deliver to it, so no further turn fails. The hold ends when the seat is swapped, when its reset time passes, or on `deliver NAME`.

**Codex's allowance, before the cap.** For each Codex seat, the watcher reads the latest `rate_limits` record in that seat's Codex rollout, found through the session lookup `context_size` already does: `primary.used_percent` and `resets_at`. It does this at most every 10 minutes, and at most once per pass.
- At or above `failover_at_percent` (fleet file, default 95), it raises one warning per reset window.
- Codex's allowance belongs to the account, so the warning names every Codex seat in the fleet.

Both raise one herdr notification per episode, `Fleet: NAME hit its <kind> limit` or `Fleet: Codex at 95% (resets <day HH:MM>)`. The body names the seats and the exact command for each tier seat: `herdr-fleet swap NAME --fallback`. A seat without a tier gets "no fallback defined".

`status` and the board show `limited (<kind>, since HH:MM)` for a limited seat. `status` adds `codex: NN% of the weekly allowance (resets …)` when any seat runs Codex.

### 35.3 `swap NAME --fallback`, and back

`swap NAME --fallback` moves a tier seat to the next kind in `prefer` after the kind it runs, skipping any kind with a limit still recorded. It applies only to that one named seat, and only when it is `idle` or `done`, under the same rules as `swap`.

The new session's `{note}` begins with a note the plugin writes, followed by any `--note`:
- the seat's name and the kind it replaces, and why: the limit line, or Codex's percentage;
- the last request delivered to it, from the log: its id, sender and first line;
- `Run git status: work in progress from the previous session may be in the working tree`.

The state file records `failover: {from, to, at, reason}`. `status` shows `failed over from <kind>` in DRIFT instead of an args drift. A plain `swap` with no names skips failed-over seats, saying so. `swap NAME` without `--fallback` returns the seat to its primary.

When a recorded reset time passes, the watcher sends one notification: `Fleet: <kind> limit reset; swap NAME returns it`. `--fallback` is a usage error, exit 2, in these cases:
- the seat is adopted, or uses kind/args with no tier;
- every other kind in `prefer` is limited.

### 35.4 Code, invariants and tests

- Invariant 22 gains: the watcher reads `agent read NAME --lines 12` of a seat whose wait just returned idle or done, when its kind has limit patterns, for 35.2 alone. It also reads the Codex rollout's `rate_limits` record, numbers only.
- No new key path and no new prompt path.
- The hold on a limited seat goes through the existing hold mechanism of 21.2.
- `fleetfile` parses tiers, `prefer`, `extra_args` and `failover_at_percent`, with errors for unknown kinds, a missing kind in `prefer`, and both forms on one seat.
- README covers tiers, `--fallback`, the limited state and the Codex line; AGENTS.md is updated in the same change. Version 0.19.0.

Tests go in `tests/test_section35.py`, and `fake_herdr` gains a screen showing a limit line after a turn:
- tier parsing and its errors;
- effective kind and args for `up`, `restart` and drift;
- each kind's limit pattern, and a non-matching screen;
- the hold, ended by a swap, a reset and `deliver`;
- one notification per episode;
- the Codex percentage threshold and its reset, from a fixture rollout;
- `--fallback`: order, skipping a limited kind, the generated note, the state record, refusal for an adopted seat, a seat without a tier, and all kinds limited;
- a plain `swap` skipping failed-over seats, and `swap NAME` returning one.

### 35.5 Amendments from the build

One build makes 35.1 to 35.4 as version 0.19.0. New module `limits.py` (pure: patterns, reset times, the `limited` record's rules, the fallback order); `seats.LIMIT_PATTERNS` and its constants; `fleetfile` (tiers), `state` (`patch_entry`, `patch_top`, `read_agents`), `context_size` (`seat_allowance`), `ops` (`_swap_fallback`), `watch` (the reads, the hold, the notifications), `mailroom`, `checks`, `board`, `cli`, `messages.last_request`. Tests are `tests/test_section35.py` (101 after round 4). What the section left open, and where the build differs from it:

1. **One effective pair, applied once.** A tier seat's `kind`, `args` and `quit` are set when the fleet is loaded to the primary's, and `Ctx.apply_failover` (also after `reload_fleet`) switches a seat whose state entry records `failover.to` to that kind (`Seat.use`). So no reader changed; `up`, `restart`, the drift check, kickoffs and `status` read the effective pair. A `failover` record naming a kind the seat no longer lists is ignored. `Seat.quit_for(kind)` gives a tier seat's quit for any kind, so a quit of the old kind during `swap --fallback` uses the old kind's command, not the new one's. `Fleet.kinds()` lists every kind of every `prefer`, so `doctor`, the integration rows and `check_kinds` cover the fallbacks (`check_kinds` also reports a tier entry no seat uses, when herdr lacks the kind). The hooks check and the permissions check count a seat that may run Claude.
2. **The `limited` record.** Fields: `kind`, `line` (the matched line, at most 200 characters), `at`, `resets_at` and `resets_hint` (35.2), `notified`, and later `released` (set by `deliver NAME`) and `reset_notified`. It holds a seat's queue while it counts (not released, reset time not passed) and the seat's recorded `kind` is the limited kind, so a swap to another kind ends the hold with no further write. The record then stays: it still counts for `swap --fallback`'s skipping, which is how a second seat does not choose a kind that is limited. When a different kind hits its limit on the same seat the new record becomes `limited` and the old one that still counts moves to `limited_also`. `released` ends both the hold and the counting; the next limit line is a new episode and notifies again. `limited` and `limited_also` are written field-only under the `.herdr/` lock (`patch_entry`) and kept by `save_state` like `state_seen`; the top-level `codex_allowance` is kept the same way.
3. **Patterns.** Case-insensitive, one line at a time, the last match of the twelve lines: Claude `usage limit` and `credit balance (is )?too low`; Codex `hit your usage limit`; Kimi `[provider.api_error] 429` and `reached your … usage limit`. A line that starts with the kind's prompt glyph (an echoed prompt) or with `⏺`, `●` or `•` (an assistant message) is skipped, because a seat discussing limits, as this one does, quotes the message. The kinds with patterns are exactly those three.
4. **Reset times.** `limits.parse_reset` reads `resets 3pm`, `will reset at 3:05pm (Australia/Melbourne)` and `try again at Nov 5th, 2026 8:44 PM`: a clock time with no date is the next such time; a bracketed zone is used when `zoneinfo` knows it (Python 3.9 and later), else the machine's zone. A line with no time gets `resets_at` of 5 hours later for Kimi (`resets_hint: "5h window"`) and none for the others: their hold lasts until a swap or `deliver NAME`.
5. **The working-seat read (the gap in 35.4).** Invariant 22 as 35.4 words it names only the read after a wait returns idle or done; the stuck-`working` read of 35.2 needs a line of its own. Herdr refuses `agent read` of a working seat in the alternate screen with `agent_not_idle` (32.3), so the scan tries `agent read NAME --lines 12` and, on that code only, reads `pane read PANE --lines 12`, which the presence guard already runs on every seat. `tests/support.py` (`limit_read_allowed`) allows the watcher's `agent read` after an `agent wait` of that seat returned idle or done, or for a seat `working` at its last `agent list`, with exactly `--lines 12` and no `--source`; the board's blocked-seat read and the `p` key are unchanged. The first scan comes `LIMIT_SCAN_S` (5 minutes) after the watcher starts, so a quiet watcher stays quiet; a seat's age is `state_seen` (a lower bound, 20.5). The scan lists agents once, only when a seat has non-FYI mail queued.
6. **The allowance read.** At most one rollout is read per pass: the first live Codex seat, in file order, whose rollout holds a record answers for the account. The first read comes 60 s after the watcher starts and then every 10 minutes. The reading (`used_percent`, `resets_at`, `window_minutes`, `at`, `read_at`, `warned_for`) is kept in the state file as `codex_allowance`; `warned_for` is the reset time of the window already warned about, so a restarted watcher does not warn again and a new window does. The window is named from `window_minutes` (`weekly` for 10080, `5-hour` for 300), not assumed weekly. Without `status --context`, `status` prints the watcher's recorded reading (reading no transcript, 29.2); with it, it reads the rollout now; with no reading yet it says so. The line is printed when any seat runs Codex.
7. **Hold surface.** The watcher keeps `limit_holds` beside `holds`; the board's snapshot merges them, so the queue cell reads `holding: limited (claude)` and the fleet page lists it; the row's `doing` cell and the seat page's title read `limited (claude, since 10:41)`. A held mailbox gets no waiter. `deliver NAME` (and `d` on that seat's page) releases the hold and delivers; a bare `deliver`, `watch --once` and the board's `d` leave a held seat alone and say `sample-dev: limited (claude), 1 left queued; deliver sample-dev releases it`.
8. **Notification texts.** Episode: `Fleet: NAME hit its <kind> limit`, body `<line> (resets Mon 16:05[, 5h window]). Run: herdr-fleet swap NAME --fallback` (with `--fleet PATH` when discovery from the watcher's directory would not find this fleet), `no fallback defined` for a seat with no tier, or `no fallback available: every other kind in prefer is limited`. Reset: `Fleet: <kind> limit reset; swap NAME returns it` for a seat that failed over from that kind, else `Fleet: <kind> limit reset; NAME's queue goes out again`. Codex: `Fleet: Codex at 96% (resets Thu 20:44)`, the body one `NAME: <command or reason>` per live Codex seat.
9. **`swap NAME --fallback`.** The kind it runs is the live agent's when that is in `prefer`, else the file's; the next kind is the first after it in `prefer`, then those before it (the order wraps), never one with a limit still recorded in any seat's entry. An absent tier seat is started on the next kind. `failover` is recorded only when the state entry then shows the new kind and a new `started_at`; if the quit is refused or the start fails the seat goes back to its old kind in memory and nothing is recorded. The note adds the last request by the log's account: a `send` entry that was typed at once (`sent` or `unconfirmed`) or a `delivered` entry for a `send`, not an FYI. `--fallback` with `--handover`, with no name or several, for a seat with no tier and for an adopted live seat are usage errors (exit 2).
10. **The way back.** `swap NAME` for a failed-over seat sets its primary in memory, finds the kind drift and restarts it; the `failover` record is deleted only after the entry shows the primary and a new `started_at`, and a restart that did not happen leaves the seat failed over. A seat that already runs its primary just has the record cleared. A return to a kind whose limit is still recorded says so (`note - its claude limit is still recorded …`): the hold applies again until the reset or `deliver NAME`. A plain `swap` skips failed-over seats (`skipped - failed over from claude to kimi; swap NAME returns it to claude`). `swap NAME --handover` of a failed-over seat counts as a change.
11. **`status` and `doctor`.** DRIFT reads `failed over from claude` (the `failover` record, in `--json` too, with `tier`, `prefer`, and `limited`/`limit_holds`); the STATE cell ends `, limited (claude, since 10:41)` while a record holds. `doctor` checks the quit command of every kind of a tier seat (`quit NAME/kind`), prints `info tier NAME: claude > codex > kimi (tier strong)` (and the failover and any limit), and `info limits: no limit pattern for kind X` for a fallback kind with none.

Round 2, building 35.6 (items 3 and 5 above are superseded by items 13 and 14):

12. **The input area and the six lines.** `limits.input_top` finds the seat's input area: the last line that is an input line (its first character after box borders is `❯`, `›` or `>`, followed by a blank or nothing), and the rule or box top (`─`, `━`, `╭` and nothing else but borders) directly above it, else the input line itself. A screen with no input line (a dialog, a menu) counts nothing. `limit_line` takes the last 6 non-blank lines above that, skips lines that start with the kind's prompt glyph or `⏺ ● •`, and returns the last that matches; the `⎿` of Claude Code and the `■` of Codex are dropped from the recorded line, so the notification and the failover note quote the message itself. `limits.input_empty` reads the input line the same way: its glyph alone or the kind's placeholder is empty; anything else, a Claude Code suggestion included (plain text cannot tell it from typing), is text, so the answer errs towards refusing.
13. **The quiet-seat check.** `Watcher.scan_limits`, run from `limit_cycle` every `LIMIT_SCAN_S` (the first 5 minutes after the watcher starts), reads one `agent list` and then each seat that is idle, done or working, whose kind has patterns, that is not held already and whose `state_seen` age is at least `LIMIT_QUIET_S`; blocked and unknown seats are never read. It reads once per unchanged state: `limit_read[seat]` keeps the state's `since`, in memory, so a restarted watcher reads each seat once more. The age comes from `state_seen`, which the scan itself records from its list, so a seat the watcher had not seen before is read on the second scan after it appears, 5 to 10 minutes. The turn-end read and the stuck-`working` scan are gone; a waiter that wakes only checks the record (`limits.holding`) and does not deliver to a held seat. The cost is one `agent list` every 5 minutes in a fleet with a Claude, Codex or Kimi seat, whatever its queues. A limit found with an empty queue is recorded and notified, and holds nothing until mail arrives.
14. **Harness.** `tests/support.py`: `limit_read_allowed` allows the watcher's `agent read NAME --lines 12` of a seat idle, done or working at its last `agent list` (the old wait-return allowance is gone), and a quit prompt (`/exit` or `/quit`) from `restart`, `swap`, `down` or the handover helper to a `working` seat only after a read of that pane in the same command that showed a limit line and no text in the input, judged by the fake's own `shows_limit`. `test_section20` and `test_section25` accept the 12-line reads and still catch any other (16 lines, visible, a seat that is not live). The fake's scripted screen now goes with an agent that exits, and `fake_herdr.show_limit` draws the layouts of 1 and 2 November (Claude's `⎿`, `✻ Worked for` and rule; Kimi's error, wrapped line, `/export-debug-zip` hint and input box; Codex's `■` line), with `typed` for text in the input and `prose` for an assistant sentence above.
15. **Quitting a limited working seat.** `ops.settle` asks `limited_working` first when herdr says `working` (before `--wait`, since a limited Kimi seat never settles): the record must hold for the kind the seat runs, the kind must have patterns, and a fresh read (`agent read`, then `pane read` on `agent_not_idle`) must show the limit line and an empty input. Then the seat joins `ctx.limited_quit`, `_quit_live` passes `allow=("idle", "done", "working")` to `quit_agent` for that seat alone, and `safe_prompt` and the presence guard run as always; a dry run reads and says it would. Kimi has no prompt glyph in `seats.PROMPT_GLYPHS` (an unknown placeholder would hold every Kimi delivery), so the guard cannot judge its input line; `limits.input_empty` is that judgment for this one path. For Kimi that `input_empty` read, made in `ops` just before the quit, is the only input check, and the window between it and `safe_prompt`'s own guard (which passes a kind with no glyph) is short but real; see item 19 for the other conditions. Any condition failing leaves the ordinary refusal (`agent is working; pass --wait MS`). `restart`, `swap`, `down` and `--fallback` share it, and nothing else does.
16. **`swap --fallback` with several names or none.** Names are checked first (a name with no tier, an adopted live seat, a seat with no free kind are usage errors, exit 2, before anything moves), then moved one at a time in the order given, each with the state as the one before left it. With no names it takes every tier seat whose record holds for the kind it runs; none is `fallback: nothing - no tier seat is marked limited` (exit 0), and a seat that has no free kind or is adopted is reported `refused` (exit 3) while the others go on.
17. **Section 7.** Invariants 11 and 22 are amended as 35.6 asks: invariant 11 gains the quit of a limited working seat as its one new exception, invariant 22 the quiet-seat read and the Codex numbers. README rule 21 and AGENTS.md say the same.

Round 3, answering the reviewer's FIX (#f6a0f1):

18. **Anchored patterns (F1).** `seats.LIMIT_PATTERNS` are now matched with `match` against the cleaned line, the text left once the box borders, the `⎿` and `■` markers and the leading spaces are removed, so only a line that begins with the provider's own wording counts; this replaces item 3's substring patterns. Claude: `^Credit balance (is )?too low` (seen live), and `^(Claude )?usage limit reached`, `^5-hour limit reached`, `^Weekly limit reached` (**not seen live**; taken from the documented wording, so a layout that differs is missed, not mis-matched). Codex: `^You[’']ve hit your usage limit`, and the line must have carried the `■` marker (`seats.LIMIT_MARKER_REQUIRED`); not seen live either. Kimi: `^Error: \[provider\.api_error\] 429` (seen live). The matching stays case-insensitive. The reviewer's screens a to e (a later line of an answer, a tool result quoting a grep hit, a list item, a table row, Kimi prose above the box, a Codex continuation line) and two more (a mid-line quotation, a Codex line without its marker) are tests and none matches; the test that expected a plain sentence six lines up to match is now one that expects it not to.
19. **The limited quit is tied to the working episode (F2).** `ops.limited_working` also requires that `state_seen`, read fresh from the state file, says `working` with a `since` not after the record's `at` (the seat was working without a break before the limit was recorded, which a Kimi seat stuck since before the scan satisfies and a Claude seat the owner resumed with "continue" does not), and that the record's kind equals the live agent's kind, compared there and not only through `holding`. A seat whose `state_seen` is another state or absent is refused. Tests: a stuck Kimi seat passes; a seat resumed after the record is refused in both layouts; a seat last seen idle or never seen is refused; a record of another kind than the live agent's is refused.
20. **Stale text (F4).** `docs/testing.md` describes `show_limit` for the quiet-seat check (35.6.2) and the limited quit and lists `typed` and `prose`; the comments of `limits.py` and `seats.py` say six non-blank lines above the input area; `Herdr.pane_read`'s docstring names `ops.read_screen` and the quiet-seat check among the readers. README and AGENTS.md name the anchored patterns and the working-episode condition.

Round 4, answering the reviewer's R1 (#d58dca):

21. **The quit asks for a nearer line (replaced by 37.1).** `limits.limit_line` takes an `above` count (default `ABOVE_INPUT`, 6, which the watcher's detection keeps); `ops.limited_working` passes `QUIT_ABOVE`, 3, so the limit line must be within the last 3 non-blank lines above the input area to quit a `working` seat. A turn resumed in place pushes the line up: after the Kimi error, its wrapped line and the `/export-debug-zip` hint come the owner's `continue`, a spinner line and the input box, which puts the error 5th, so the quit is refused while `state_seen` (working since before the record) cannot tell the two apart. The reviewer's screen is a test that must be refused, and the watcher still sees that line within 6. The real layouts pass: the line is 2nd for Claude (then `✻ Worked for`), 3rd for Kimi (a wrap and the hint below it) and 1st for Codex. **How Kimi draws a turn resumed in place is not verified live**: the screen is built from the reviewer's description, so a layout that keeps the error within 3 lines while working would still be taken, and one that draws the box differently would be refused.

Checks: both interpreter suites and `compileall` pass (counts in the builder's report); every file parses with the Python 3.8 grammar; an actual 3.8 interpreter was not run. Not verified live: the real limit messages beyond those in 35.2, how Claude Code words a limit with a reset time (the parser handles the forms above), whether Kimi's `agent read` of a working seat is refused, and a real Codex rollout through the watcher (the fixture follows the format of a local rollout, numbers only).

### 35.6 Designer decisions after the first build (2 November 2026)

These replace 35.2 to 35.4 where they differ. They answer the builder's points 1 to 7 and a field report from another fleet the same day: four Kimi seats limited at once, each stuck `working`, so swap refused them, and they were quit by hand.

1. **Where a match counts.** A limit line counts only within the last 6 non-blank lines above the seat's input area: Claude's prompt rule, or Kimi's input box, `╭`…`│ >`. This replaces matching anywhere in the last 12 lines, and the bullet skip stays. The real layouts seen on 1 and 2 November:
   - Claude: `  ⎿  Credit balance too low · Add funds: …`, then `✻ Worked for 3s · done …`, then the rule and `❯`.
   - Kimi: `  Error: [provider.api_error] 429 You've reached your 5-hour usage limit. …`, a wrapped line, `  If this persists, run /export-debug-zip …`, then the input box.
2. **No dependence on queued mail.** On the 2 November builder, the limit hit while it was working on a request already delivered, so its queue was empty. The watcher therefore checks, at most every 5 minutes, every seat whose kind has patterns and whose state has not changed for 5 minutes, whatever its queue. It reads each seat at most once per unchanged state, so it is read again only after its state changes. This replaces both the turn-end read and the stuck-working scan, and it needs no new waits; the watcher already reads `agent list` every pass.
3. **Invariant 22's wording** names that check. It is one `agent read NAME --lines 12` per seat per unchanged state, falling back to `pane read` only on `agent_not_idle`, plus the Codex `rate_limits` numbers.
4. **A limited seat can be quit.** `swap`, `restart`, `down` and `--fallback` may quit a seat the state marks `limited` even when herdr reports it `working`, under three conditions:
   - a fresh read still shows the limit line within the lines of point 1;
   - the presence guard finds its input line empty;
   - the quit goes through `safe_prompt` with the kind's quit command, with only the state rule relaxed, and only for that seat.

   This is invariant 11's one new exception. Anything else on screen, or text in the input, still refuses.
5. **Several seats at once.** `swap --fallback` takes several names, processed one at a time. With no names, it covers every tier seat currently marked limited. The kickoff already carries each seat's queued mail through `{inbox}`.
6. **Builder points 4 to 7 are accepted as built:** the 5-hour hold, time zones on 3.8, the Codex `primary` label, and the warning when `swap NAME` returns a seat to a kind still limited.

### 35.7 Verification and limits (designer, 2 November 2026)

Live against a real Kimi usage limit (herdr 0.9.5, Kimi Code CLI 2.4.1, Claude Code), on a scratch fleet in its own workspace, with the reviewed working tree. The scratch fleet had one tier seat with `prefer = ["kimi", "claude"]`, Kimi K3.1 Pro and Claude Haiku.

- **The stuck state.** The Kimi seat's first turn ended at once with `Error: [provider.api_error] 429 You've reached your 5-hour usage limit …`, above its empty input box. herdr kept reporting it `working`.
- **Detection.** The watcher, started in log mode, marked the seat on its second quiet-seat check, 10 minutes after it began. It printed and notified `Fleet: sample-dev hit its kimi limit (resets Mon 16:53, 5h window); holding its queue`.
  - `status` showed `working, limited (kimi, since 11:53)`.
  - The state record held the anchored error line, `resets_hint: "5h window"`, and a `state_seen` of `working` from before the record.
- **Failover.** `swap sample-dev --fallback` reported `fallback - kimi -> claude (limit recorded)`, then `working, but its kimi limit is on screen and its input is empty; it is quit`, then `quit with /exit`, `started`, `briefed` and `failed over - from kimi to claude`.
  - The new Claude session's kickoff began with the plugin's note: `Failover: sample-dev was running kimi; this session runs claude because kimi hit its usage limit (…)` and `Run git status: …`.
  - `status` showed `failed over from kimi`, and `down --close-panes` cleaned up.

**Not verified live:**
- Claude's and Codex's limit wording, other than Claude's `Credit balance too low`, which 1 November showed;
- the Codex percentage warning against a live watcher;
- a Kimi turn resumed in place (35.5 item 21);
- the reset notification (5 h).

The estimated reset is 5 h after detection, which is later than Kimi's real window, as 35.5 records.

## 36. Screen reads that fit a short pane (0.19.1)

A field report on 2 November 2026: `swap` on a Claude seat with two background shells open sent `/exit`, and Claude Code showed its exit confirmation (`Background work is running` … `❯ 1. Exit and stop tasks`). The plugin did not press its one Enter (23.7). The quit timed out with the generic `did not exit within 20000 ms after /exit; not retried, no keys sent, seat left alone`, and the coordinator answered the dialog by hand. The same seat's board row showed no dialog lines.

### 36.1 The herdr fact

herdr 0.9.5 answers `agent read NAME --lines N` (default source `recent`) from the pane's viewport when N is at most the viewport's rows (`pane get` → `scroll.viewport_rows`), whatever the agent's state. For more rows than that, it must collect the app's own history, which for an app in the alternate screen it does only while the agent is `idle` or `done`. Otherwise it refuses: `agent_not_idle: cannot read N lines while NAME is blocked: its alternate-screen history can only be captured by …` (and the same for `working` and `unknown`). `agent read --source visible` and `pane read` return the viewport rows in every state. Claude Code with `tui = "fullscreen"` draws in the alternate screen.

Verified live on 2 November 2026 (herdr 0.9.5, Claude Code 2.1.316):
- A grid of seven seats gave panes of 13 rows. `--lines 12` read a working seat; `--lines 16` was refused `agent_not_idle`; `--lines 16 --source visible` and `pane read --lines 16` returned the 13 rows.
- On a 60-row pane with the exit confirmation up (`blocked`), `--lines 16` read it and `--lines 70` was refused.
- herdr's log showed every `agent read` of the reported seat failing from 1 s after `/exit` until it exited.

So on any pane shorter than 16 rows, every read below fails while the seat is not settled, and 32.3's decision ("`quit_agent`, `interrupt` and the board's blocked-dialog read keep their reads as they are") left them unable to see the dialog they exist to see.

### 36.2 The rule

`seats.read_screen(herdr, target, lines)` is the one helper for a read that decides from what is on screen now. It runs `agent read TARGET --lines N`. When herdr refuses that with `agent_not_idle`, and only then, it runs `agent read TARGET --lines N --source visible` once and returns that. Any other error is raised as today. herdr refuses only for an app in the alternate screen, which has no host scrollback, so the viewport it returns is the bottom of what the app draws now: the dialog, prompt or menu the caller looks for.

Its callers, with their line counts unchanged:
1. `quit_agent`'s exit-confirmation read 3 s after the quit command (23.7). With the fallback it sees the dialog on a short pane and presses its one Enter, exactly as on a tall one.
2. The dialog naming of a held quit (`_settle_held`, 32.2) and of a timed-out one (`_no_exit`, 23.7).
3. `interrupt`'s read before its keys (`ops.op_interrupt`, 23.7): a dialog in the viewport still refuses the keys (`refused NAME: a dialog is on screen (…)`). Before this, the refusal of the read failed the command (`failed NAME: reading its screen: …; no keys sent`), so `interrupt` could not interrupt a full-screen seat in a short pane.
4. The board's blocked-dialog lines (`board._dialog`, 24.2).

Unchanged:
- `peek` and the board's `p` (32.3), which already fall back and say so.
- The limit reads (35.2, 35.6), which already fall back to `pane read`.
- `interrupt`'s Ctrl-U check, which runs only after the seat is `idle` or `done`.
- Report wording. A fallback read is not mentioned in reports: what it shows is the current screen, which is what the caller asked for. A read that still fails keeps today's handling at each caller.

No new key path, no new prompt path, and no new herdr command. The fallback is one more read-only `agent read`, at most once for each read it replaces.

### 36.3 Code, invariants and tests

- `seats.read_screen` is the helper. The five call sites in `seats.py`, `ops.py` and `board.py` use it. Section 32.3's sentence on these reads gains "(amended by 36)".
- Invariants 19 to 22 stand. An `agent read --source visible` from restart, swap, down, interrupt, the `handover` helper (which runs restart or swap, 32.5), the `manage` menu (whose held or timed-out quit names the dialog, and which still presses no key) or the watcher is allowed only as the fallback: right after an `agent read` of the same target, with the same `--lines` and no `--source`, that the fake answered `agent_not_idle`.
  - Only a read that succeeded counts as the screen a key decision was made from. A refused or failed read never authorizes the Enter of 23.7 or `interrupt`'s keys; after a refusal, only the visible read that follows it can.
  - The Enter of 23.7 may then follow the visible read when that read showed the highlighted exit confirmation.
  - `interrupt`'s keys may then follow it when it showed no dialog.
  - The watcher's visible read is allowed only for a seat that was `blocked` at the last `agent list`, as for the read it replaces.
  - `peek` and the board's `p` keep their own rule.
- `tests/fake_herdr.py` models the herdr fact: `viewport_rows(world, pane, rows)` (default: no limit) makes an `agent read` without `--source visible` and with `--lines` above `rows` answer `agent_not_idle` for an agent that is not `idle` or `done`. A visible read returns the last `rows` lines. The existing `agent_not_idle` switch stays.
- `tests/test_section36.py`:
  - On a 13-row pane, a `swap` and a `restart` on a seat whose quit shows the exit confirmation answer it with one Enter after the visible read.
  - With the dialog not highlighted, no Enter is pressed.
  - A held quit and a timed-out one name the dialog.
