  - `interrupt` on a working seat in a 13-row pane presses its keys when no dialog shows, and is refused naming the dialog when one does.
  - The board shows a blocked seat's dialog lines.
  - Each of these on a tall pane makes exactly the reads it makes today, with no visible read.
  - The harness fails a visible read from these commands that does not follow a refused read.
- Version 0.19.1 in `herdr-plugin.toml` and `herdr_fleet/__init__.py`. AGENTS.md's architecture paragraph names the fallback where it describes `quit_agent`, `interrupt` and the board. `docs/testing.md` lists `test_section36.py`.

### 36.4 Verification (designer, 2 November 2026)

Live with the 0.19.1 working tree (herdr 0.9.5, Claude Code 2.1.316, `tui = "fullscreen"`). The scratch fleet ran one Claude Haiku seat in its own workspace. A plain shell pane split below it left the seat's pane 13 rows tall, the height of the reported grid.

- **Exit confirmation.** The seat had one background shell open. `restart` sent `/exit`, and Claude Code showed `Background work is running` … `❯ 1. Exit and stop tasks`; herdr reported the seat `blocked`.
  - A sampler running alongside saw every `agent read --lines 16` refused `agent_not_idle` while the dialog was up.
  - `restart` reported `quit - answered its exit confirmation (exit and stop tasks)`, then `started` and `briefed` in the same pane.
  - On the same seat in a 60-row pane, 0.19.0 had answered the same dialog, since its 16-line read fitted.
- **`interrupt`.** With the seat `working` in a foreground command on the 13-row pane:
  - The installed 0.19.0 answered `failed …: reading its screen: herdr error agent_not_idle: cannot read 16 lines while … is working …; no keys sent`.
  - The working tree answered `interrupted … (done)`, and the screen showed `Interrupted · What should Claude do instead?` above an empty input.

**Not verified live:**
- the board's blocked-dialog lines on a short pane (the fake covers them);
- a held quit's and a timed-out quit's dialog naming on a short pane.

## 37. Failover on real screens, and what an FYI is for (0.19.2)

Three field reports between 2 and 5 November 2026. Two came on the day 0.19.0 shipped:
- a limited Kimi seat stuck `working` could not be quit by `swap --fallback`;
- a fallback kind that failed to start was tried again on every run, so the seat never reached the next kind.

In both, coordinators worked around the plugin by hand; two fleets hit both. The third: a fleet sat idle because a seat reported finished work with `--fyi`, as the plugin's own kickoff text tells it to.

### 37.1 The limited quit reads the error block, not a line count

Kimi Code CLI wraps its 429 message itself, at the pane's width; `recent-unwrapped` does not join the lines. Below the message it prints `If this persists, run /export-debug-zip and share the file with us for diagnosis. …`. On a pane of ordinary width the message took three lines, so with the hint the limit line was the 4th non-blank line above the input area:

```
   Error: [provider.api_error] 429 You've reached your 5-hour usage limit. Your quota will reset when the curr…
 window ends. To continue now, purchase extra usage or upgrade your plan:
 https://www.kimi.com/membership/subscription?tab=quota
   If this persists, run `/export-debug-zip` and share the file with us for diagnosis. Please don't share it p…

 ╭──────────────────────────────────────────────╮
 │ >                                            │
 ╰──────────────────────────────────────────────╯
```

Kimi draws the whole screen one column in from the pane's edge, so the plugin's read shows the error at column 3, its wrap at column 1 and the hint at column 3.

`QUIT_ABOVE` (3, 35.5 item 21) refused it, and `swap --fallback` answered `refused - agent is working`, while the watcher's detection, which counts 6, had marked the seat. Item 21 counted one wrapped line, but the count changes with the pane's width, so no fixed count is right.

The rule: `ops.limited_working` accepts the screen when `limits.limit_block(kind, screen)` holds, and `QUIT_ABOVE` goes. `limit_block` holds when the screen shows, in this order:
1. the limit line, found as today within the last `ABOVE_INPUT` (6) non-blank lines above the input area;
2. zero or more **continuation lines**, the message's own wrap. Each one meets three conditions:
   - it directly follows the limit line or another continuation, with no blank line between;
   - it is indented less than the limit line once box borders are removed (on the screen above, column 1 against column 3), so the rule holds whatever margin the kind draws;
   - its first character after that indentation is a letter or a digit, so a spinner (`⠋`, `✢`, `✽`, `·`), a bullet, a prompt glyph or `✻` is never one.

   (Amended after the first build and its review. The first text said column 0, which the real screen's margin fails, and it accepted any line at that column.) Unindented prose directly under the error, with no blank line, still reads as a wrap. Telling the two apart would need the provider's wrap rule. The quit's other conditions still apply: working since before the record, and an empty input.
3. zero or more lines that match one of the kind's `seats.LIMIT_TRAILERS`, matched like `LIMIT_PATTERNS` against the cleaned line (Kimi: `^If this persists, run \`/export-debug-zip\``; Claude: `^✻ \S+ for \d`, the `✻ Worked for …` line item 21 saw 2nd);
4. then the input area, empty (`limits.input_empty`, unchanged).

Anything else between the limit line and the input refuses: an echoed prompt, a spinner, assistant output, a continuation line after a trailer. The other conditions of 35.6 item 4 and 35.5 item 19 stand: the record's kind is the live kind, `state_seen` shows working since before the record, and the read is fresh. The resumed-in-place screen of item 21 (error, wrap, hint, then the owner's `continue`, a spinner and the input box) is still refused, because its `continue` comes after the trailer. Codex's layout (the line alone above the input) passes as before.

Tests: the screen above, with its one-column margin and with none, accepted at widths that wrap the message to 1, 2 and 3 lines; the same screen with a wrapped line indented as deep as the error refused; the review's screens refused: a spinner between the wrap and the hint, a blank line and then text with no hint, and Claude's spinner between the error and its trailer; Claude's error with `✻ Worked for 2m 3s` below it; item 21's resumed screen refused; a spinner, a prompt line or assistant text between the block and the input refused; text in the input refused; the watcher's detection unchanged (its tests stand).

### 37.2 `--fallback` moves past a kind that does not start

In one repository Codex failed at startup (`Error: account/read failed during TUI bootstrap: … invalid data (code -32603)`; the cause was a `-c` override in the seat's arguments); in another it exited at once on an MCP configuration error. `swap --fallback` started Codex, 32.1's retry started it again, and the seat was left absent. Every later `--fallback` planned Codex again (a dry run too), so the seat never reached Claude. In both fleets the coordinator started Claude by hand.

The rule: in `ops._swap_fallback`, when the start of candidate K fails, the plugin reports `fallback - K did not start (<the start's reason>); trying L` and starts the next candidate L in the same pane, in the same command. A failed start means 32.1's two attempts both left a bare shell (the command never ran, or the agent exited at once), or the start failed in any other way that leaves the pane at its shell. Candidates are tried once each, in `limits.fallback_candidates` order, which skips limited kinds as today.
- A slow start (32.1: a process of the kind still coming up) is not a failure; it is waited for as today. One still not ready at the deadline (`its process runs in PANE but herdr has not seen it ready after 45 s`) is reported and notified as today. The fallback stops there with that kind recorded, since its process is running. (Amended after review: the first build dropped that notification.)
- A step line gives only the cause: `fallback - codex did not start (the start command did not run in PANE twice, or the agent exited at once); trying kimi`. 32.1's remedy (`run herdr-fleet up NAME`, the mouse-tracking hint) is printed only when the seat is left absent.
- A start that ends `blocked` (a dialog at startup) stops there, as today: only the owner answers it.
- When every candidate fails, each failure is reported, the seat is left absent, and one notification names the seat and the kinds tried (`request` sound). Exit 1.
- A failed start is not a limit. It records no `limited` entry and is not remembered across commands, so the next `--fallback` tries the same order again: the cause is a configuration the owner fixes.
- `failover.to` and the failover note name the kind that started. The note adds one line per kind that did not start (`codex did not start: <reason>`), so the new agent knows why it is not the first fallback.

Invariants: 32.1's start budget becomes per candidate kind. A pane gets at most two `agent start` calls for each kind it tries, the second only after a `process-info` read showing the bare shell; the first start of the next kind also follows a `process-info` read showing the bare shell. `tests/support.py`'s start counting counts per (pane, kind) within one `swap --fallback` command and keeps today's per-pane count everywhere else.

Tests: Codex scripted to exit at once on both attempts, then Claude started and briefed with the failover note naming Codex's failure; every candidate failing (each reported, one notification, the seat absent, no `limited` record, exit 1); a `blocked` first candidate stopping without trying the next; a slow first candidate waited for and kept; several names, where the first seat's fallback skips a failed kind and the second seat's run tries it again.

### 37.3 An FYI is for what nobody has to act on

The report, 5 November. A coordinator gave its builder one fix round, said "when the dev reports back, I'll run my full gate", and went idle. The builder finished nine minutes later and reported with `--fyi`. An FYI never wakes an idle seat (20.8), so both seats sat idle until the owner noticed. The same had happened that morning to the builder's "built, every check passes" report, which waited 52 minutes until the coordinator happened to read its inbox. Until that day the builder had reported finished work as a plain message, and the coordinator had answered with a `Thanks (#id)` FYI, which settles the obligation without waking anyone.

The cause is the plugin's own wording. Every kickoff's `{roster}` sentence says "When you have acted on a request, reply with `--fyi` or `ack #id`". `send --help` describes `--fyi` as "a report that awaits no reply". Both define an FYI from the sender's side (no reply wanted), while what the plugin does with one concerns the recipient (it is never woken). Finished work is where the two differ: the builder wants no reply, but the coordinator acts on it.

**Not a wake rule.** An FYI that settles the target's open request looks, in the log, exactly like the coordinator's `Thanks (#id)` FYI that settles a reviewer's verdict. A rule that woke the one would wake the other, and such thanks are most of a busy fleet's FYIs. So 20.8's delivery rules stay as they are; the fix is the wording, plus one hint at the moment of sending.

1. **Wording.** An FYI is "nothing for the recipient to act on".
   - `kickoff.ROSTER_SENTENCE` begins: ``Message a seat with `herdr-fleet send <name> "..."`. Finished work, a verdict or an answer someone is waiting for goes as a plain message, even when you want no reply; `--fyi` is only for what nobody has to act on, because an FYI never wakes an idle seat. When you have acted on a request, report it, or close it with `ack #id` when there is nothing to report, since the plugin cannot see what you did.`` From "To control order" on, the sentence is unchanged.
   - `send --help` for `--fyi`: `nothing for the recipient to act on: it never wakes an idle seat and goes with its next delivery, or expires after fyi_ttl; finished work or a verdict someone waits for is not an FYI (text starting with "FYI:" or "FYI " counts too)`.
   - README: the `{roster}` row, the `send` row and the FYI section say the same.
2. **The hint.** When `send` queues an FYI for a local seat or contact (not a routed target) that it found idle or done and whose queue no limit record holds (35.2; `deliver NAME` would release that hold), and that FYI settles a request the target sent to the sender (one that `messages.unsettled` lists from the target to the sender just before this send is logged), the output gains one line after the outcome:
   `note: sample-orchestrator is idle and an FYI does not wake it; this one answers its #3f2a9c. If it is waiting for this, run: herdr-fleet deliver sample-orchestrator; otherwise leave it.`
   The line says `done` for a done seat and adds `--fleet PATH` under 33.5's rule. A dry run prints it too. Nothing else changes: the FYI stays queued, the watcher does not wake the seat for it, and `deliver NAME` is the existing guarded path.

There is no new prompt path, key path or log field. The hint reads only the log.

Tests:
- the roster sentence's new text, with no "reply with `--fyi`";
- the `--fyi` help text;
- the hint for an FYI to an idle seat and to a done seat whose request to the sender is open;
- no hint when the target is working, when a limit record holds its queue, when its request was already settled (an `ack`, or an earlier reply), when it never asked, for a plain send, for a routed target, or for `--fyi --now` (which delivers);
- the dry-run hint;
- the FYI still queued and no waiter started for it.

### 37.4 Version and documents

0.19.2 in `herdr-plugin.toml` and `herdr_fleet/__init__.py`.
- AGENTS.md's paragraph on design 35 names `limits.limit_block` in place of `QUIT_ABOVE`, and the per-kind start budget of `--fallback`. Its harness paragraph gains the per-(pane, kind) start count.
- README's failover section says that `--fallback` tries the next kind when one does not start; the messaging sections take 37.3's wording.
- `docs/testing.md` lists `tests/test_section37.py`.
- 35.5 item 21 gains "(replaced by 37.1)".

### 37.5 Amendments from the build

- A request old enough to have no id still gets the FYI hint, but its line says `this one answers its open request` rather than inventing an id. Requests with ids use 37.3's exact line.

### 37.6 Verification (designer, 5 November 2026)

These checks ran live on the working tree (herdr 0.9.5). The scratch fleet had two seats in their own workspace. One was a tier seat preferring Claude Haiku, then Codex with an argument Codex rejects, then Kimi K3.1 Pro; the other was a plain Claude Haiku seat.

- **37.1, on a real screen.** `limits.limit_block` was run on a Kimi screen saved when the 429 hit, read through the plugin's own read on 2 November.
  - The first build refused it: Kimi draws every line one column in, and that build looked for the wrap at column 0. The rule was amended to relative indentation.
  - After the fix round the screen is accepted.
  - The same screen is refused with a spinner line inserted before the hint, and with an echoed `continue` after it.
  - A live 429 was not provoked; the fake covers the layouts.
- **37.2.** `swap NAME --fallback` from Claude tried Codex, which exited at once twice. In the same command the plugin reported `codex did not start (the start command did not run in … twice, or the agent exited at once); trying kimi`, started Kimi, briefed it, and recorded `failover.to` kimi with no `limited` record, in 36 s. After the fix round the step line gives only the cause.
  - `swap NAME` returned the seat to Claude.
  - A second `--fallback` tried Codex again, so the failure was not remembered.
- **37.3.** The plain seat sent a request to `owner`. `owner`'s answer, sent as an FYI while the seat was done, printed `note: … is done and an FYI does not wake it; this one answers its #…`.

  - A second FYI, after the request was settled, printed none.
  - Both FYIs stayed queued.

## 38. The caller of a command run by Codex (0.19.3)

A field report on 3 November 2026: a coordinator running Codex saw `HERDR_PANE_ID` name another seat's pane, a seat of its own fleet, so commands that find the caller by its pane could act as that seat. The coordinator had worked around it with `--as` on every send.

### 38.1 The facts (designer, 5 November 2026)

- Codex 0.166 can run a TUI against a shared background app-server: `codex resume` with `--remote ADDR`, or the daemon that herdr's restore of several resumed Codex seats came up with after a machine restart. Such a TUI's own process carries the right `HERDR_PANE_ID`. But Codex runs its tools in the app-server, and the app-server's environment is that of the process that started it: another pane, sometimes in another workspace, sometimes one that now hosts a Claude seat. On the owner's machine one daemon served six seats in three fleets, all of whose commands saw one pane of a fourth seat.
- A TUI started fresh, or resumed without `--remote`, runs its own app-server (a `codex-code-mode-host` child), and its commands see its own pane.
- Every command Codex runs carries `CODEX_THREAD_ID` (and `CODEX_SESSION_ID`, the same value): the thread's id, right in both modes. herdr's Codex integration reports that id as the agent's session, so `agent list` shows it as `agent_session` `{source: "herdr:codex", value: <id>}` on the right pane. For a TUI attached to the daemon after it started, herdr may have no session recorded at all (the integration's report is made with the daemon's pane and is dropped).
- `pane current --current`, which `context.resolve_caller` uses to refresh a stale pane after a move, answers for the pane `HERDR_PANE_ID` names, so it repeats the wrong pane.

The pane decides more than the sender. Invariant 6 (the caller's own pane is never started into), the refusal to restart, swap or quit the caller's own seat (32.5), `up --me`, `--join`, the workspace `up` uses when the fleet file names none, `inbox` and `ack` without `--as`, and the handover request all read it. From such a seat, `restart` of its own seat would be allowed and the restart of the seat whose pane it inherited refused.

### 38.2 The rule

In CLI mode (not a plugin action or pane: their environment is herdr's), when the environment has a non-empty `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, the caller's pane is found by its thread and never from `HERDR_PANE_ID`, `HERDR_WORKSPACE_ID` or `pane current`:
1. The caller is the live agent whose `agent_session` has source `herdr:codex` and value `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, from one `agent list` (the one preflight already reads). Its `pane_id` and `workspace_id` become the invocation's.
2. With exactly one such agent, everything that reads the caller's pane uses it, as today. When it differs from `HERDR_PANE_ID`, the identity reason says so: `named caller in w2:p4 (by its Codex thread; HERDR_PANE_ID w2:p1 is another pane)`.
3. With none or several, the caller's pane is unknown (`inv.pane_id` and `workspace_id` None) and the reason is `a Codex command whose thread herdr does not know` (or `… that herdr records on several panes`). Then:
   - commands that need the caller's identity fail closed as 31.2 says, with the `--as NAME` guidance and that reason;
   - `up` without a `workspace` key in the fleet file stops with a usage error that names the reason and says to set `workspace` or run from the fleet's own pane;
   - `--me` and `--join` stop the same way, and so do `tile` and every other command that needs the caller's workspace when the fleet file sets none (not with "this was not run from a herdr pane");
   - `ack --older-than` fails closed too, but its error says to run it from the owner's shell, since bulk `ack` ignores `--as` (33.4);
   - the own-seat guards cannot compare against an unknown pane, so they treat every live agent that **could be the caller** as the caller. With none or several matches, every live Codex agent could be the caller. A pane whose `herdr:codex` record names another thread is not ruled out. A TUI on the shared app-server that starts a new thread (`/new`, or a resume) reports it from the daemon's pane, so herdr may keep the pane's old record. A Codex seat herdr cannot place therefore cannot restart, swap, down or interrupt any Codex seat; it does those from another pane. `restart`, `swap` (with or without `--fallback`), `down` and `interrupt` refuse a seat that could be the caller, whatever its state and with `--wait` too: `refused - SEAT may be the caller's own agent (<reason>); run this from another pane`. `up` never starts into such a pane, which invariant 5 already ensures, since the pane hosts a live agent.
   Nothing falls back to `HERDR_PANE_ID`.

   Amended after review (#2811b9). The first text said "the own-pane guards hold no pane, which is safe: nothing compares equal". That was wrong for `interrupt`. A caller herdr cannot place is `working` while its command runs, which is the state `interrupt` requires. So `interrupt SELF --as SELF` pressed the interrupt keys into the caller's own pane. `restart SELF --wait MS` would also have quit the caller if the command outlived its turn. Round 2 (#0c6835, D1) dropped the first fix's exemption for a pane whose record names another thread, because that record may be stale.

Known limit: the rule is only as current as herdr's records. A stale record can still match a thread that has since moved to another pane on the shared app-server. Then the command is taken for the seat whose pane holds the stale record.
4. Without `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, nothing changes.

`--as NAME` still settles the sender as before. It does not give the command a pane, so the guards follow item 3. `doctor` adds `info caller: by Codex thread …` when the rule applied.

### 38.3 Code, invariants and tests

`context.detect_invocation` notes the thread; `context.preflight`/`resolve_caller` applies the rule from the `agent list` it reads and skips `pane current` when the rule applied; `messages.caller_record` and `mailroom`'s caller lookups take the invocation's pane, no longer `env["HERDR_PANE_ID"]` directly, so they cannot bypass it. No new herdr command (one `agent list` the command already runs, or one more where none ran yet), no key, no prompt path. Invariant 6 and 23.3 gain a sentence each: under Codex the caller's pane is the pane of its thread. The board's `z` (`tui.py`) ignores `HERDR_PANE_ID` under the thread and zooms the recorded watch pane. Under a thread, the invariant 6 oracle in `tests/support.py` holds a set of panes: the matching one, or else every pane that could be the caller's (item 3). The harness fails any start, key or quit prompt that reaches one of them.

Tests, fake herdr (agent records with `agent_session`, an environment with `CODEX_THREAD_ID` and a `HERDR_PANE_ID` naming another seat of the same fleet):
- `send` without `--as` uses the thread's seat as the sender, the reason names both panes, and no `pane current` runs;
- an unknown thread fails closed (usage error, the reason, nothing written);
- the same for a thread on several panes;
- `--as` still names the sender;
- `restart` of the caller's own seat is refused, and `restart` of the seat whose pane `HERDR_PANE_ID` named goes ahead;
- `up --me NAME` adopts the thread's pane;
- `up` with no `workspace` key uses the thread's workspace, and an unknown thread stops it;
- `inbox` and `ack` without `--as` act for the thread's seat;
- a handover request is accepted for the thread's own seat;
- a plugin action with `CODEX_THREAD_ID` in its environment ignores it;
- with an unknown thread: `interrupt SELF --as SELF` on a working seat is refused and sends no key; `restart SELF --wait 1000` on an idle seat is refused; `restart`, `swap`, `swap --fallback` and `down` of a Codex seat without a session record are refused, and so is a Codex seat whose record names another thread; with several matches, every live Codex seat is refused; a Claude seat stays reachable in both cases;
- `ack --older-than` and `tile` give item 3's wording;
- no `CODEX_THREAD_ID` gives today's behaviour, and the existing tests stand.

### 38.4 Version and documents

0.19.3 in `herdr-plugin.toml` and `herdr_fleet/__init__.py`. AGENTS.md's architecture paragraph says where the caller's pane comes from under Codex. README's Messages section, where it says who the sender is, says the same, with the `--as` advice. `docs/testing.md` lists `tests/test_section38.py`.

### 38.5 Amendments from the build

- **Invariant and sender clarification.** The designer added section 38's caller-pane sentences to invariant 6 and section 23.3.
- **Claude hooks under a Codex command.** `inbox --hook` returns silently without a Herdr lookup or mailbox access when the CLI Codex rule applies. A hook is Claude Code's path; resolving the inherited pane there could consume another seat's mail. Other hook invocations retain their existing path.
- **Agent-list reuse.** `preflight` returns the list it read, and `send` uses it for recipient resolution as well as caller resolution. Pull inbox lookup reads one list only when the CLI Codex rule applies; explicit `inbox --as` remains offline. Doctor reuses its existing server-check list.
- **Clock-relative test fixtures.** The Codex limit line is generated two days after its test clock in the provider's dated format, still exercising `parse_reset`; Claude's daily reset is six hours ahead. Allowance reset epochs, rollout timestamps and state-age fixtures follow the clock, and worktree age uses a commit two weeks earlier. Fixed dates remain for frozen-clock timer, TTL, stale-debt, calendar and parsing cases, and for non-expiring serialization or metadata fixtures; those do not expire against the wall clock.

### 38.6 Verification (designer, 5 November 2026)

These checks ran live on the working tree (herdr 0.9.5, Codex 0.166). The working-tree CLI was run with a simulated environment: `CODEX_THREAD_ID` set, and `HERDR_PANE_ID` naming another seat's pane, as a command run in the shared app-server inherits. Every command was read-only or a dry run, and neither message log changed. One scratch fleet had two seats in their own workspace: a Codex seat on the shared app-server with no herdr session record, and a Claude Haiku seat. The development fleet was used too.

- **Session records.** Codex seats started fresh, or restored by herdr, carry an `agent_session` record with source `herdr:codex`. Only a TUI attached to the app-server afterwards has none.
- **Known thread.** The thread was a real seat's recorded thread, and `HERDR_PANE_ID` named a second seat's pane.
  - `doctor` printed `info caller: by Codex thread …: <thread's pane> (by its Codex thread; HERDR_PANE_ID <other pane> is another pane)`.
  - `send --dry-run` named the thread's seat as the sender. 0.19.2, with the same environment, named the other seat.
  - `inbox --drop --dry-run --as <other seat>` was refused. 0.19.2 allowed it.
  - `restart <thread's seat> --dry-run` was refused as the caller's own agent. `restart <other seat> --dry-run` was refused only because that seat was working.
- **Unknown thread.**
  - `doctor` printed the 38.2 reason.
  - `send`, `send --dry-run`, `inbox` and `ack` stopped with exit 2, the `--as` guidance and the reason, and wrote nothing.
  - `up --me` stopped with the item 3 wording.
  - After the fix rounds, `restart` and `swap` were refused with `may be the caller's own agent (…)` for the record-less Codex seat, which was blocked, so the refusal came before the state check. They were refused too for a Codex seat whose record names another thread. `restart` of the Claude seat went ahead (dry run). That seat's pane was the one `HERDR_PANE_ID` named, so 0.19.2 would have refused it as the caller's own.
  - `send --as owner --dry-run` went ahead.
- **A failover along the way.** Mid-release, the Kimi builder hit its 5-hour limit on its first turn of the brief. `swap NAME --fallback` moved it to Codex in one command, and Codex built the section.

**Not verified live:**
- `down` and `interrupt` of a possible caller: neither has a dry run, and a real run would quit the seat or press keys into it;
- `--wait` and the no-names forms;
- a command run from inside the app-server by a real seat: the scratch seat's command stopped at Codex's own sandbox prompt, which was not answered.

The fake covers each of these.

## 39. Mail that reaches the live fleet, Kimi's input box, and a truer failover (0.19.4)

Five gaps seen between 5 and 7 November 2026, each from a live fleet: a report sent from a Git worktree that never arrived, a brief typed into a Kimi menu, a Kimi limit the watcher missed, a failover onto a nearly used-up Codex, and a failover note that quoted an old limit.

### 39.1 A linked worktree uses the main checkout's fleet

**The fact.** A field report on 7 November: a builder ran `herdr-fleet send <coordinator>` from a linked Git worktree under `/tmp`. The repository commits `.herdr/fleet.toml`, so the worktree has its own copy, and discovery (section 2) stopped there. The worktree's directory became the fleet's root, the coordinator was working, and the message was queued in the worktree's `.herdr/inbox/`, where no watcher looks. It waited there four days, until the system's `/tmp` cleanup deleted the worktree. A scan of the owner's fleets found one more message stranded in another fleet's worktree since 24 October. Cross-fleet routing (section 31) has the same gap: `crossfleet.fleet_root` walks up from a destination agent's directory, so an agent working in a worktree is routed to that worktree's copy.

**The rule.** Discovery that walks up (not `--fleet`, `--file` or `HERDR_FLEET_FILE`, which are used as given) checks the directory `D` where it found `.herdr/fleet.toml`:
1. `D/.git` is a file `gitdir: X` (a linked worktree; `X` relative to `D` unless absolute), and `X/commondir` names the shared Git directory `C` (relative to `X` unless absolute). Two small file reads, no `git` command.
2. When `C`'s last component is `.git`, the main checkout `M` is its parent. If `M` is not `D`, `M/.herdr/fleet.toml` is a file, and `D/.herdr/fleet.state.json` does not exist, the fleet is `M`'s, and its root is `M`. A state file means a fleet was brought up in the worktree itself (20.5: nothing else creates one), and that fleet keeps its own.
3. Anything else keeps today's answer: no `.git` file, no `commondir` (a submodule), a common directory not named `.git` (a bare repository such as `proj.git`; a bare repository cloned into a directory literally named `.git` does switch, to the fleet file beside it, if there is one), a pointer that does not resolve, or no fleet file in the main checkout.

Each command that switched says so once on stderr: `herdr-fleet: <D> is a linked worktree; using the main checkout's fleet <M>/.herdr/fleet.toml (--fleet <D>/.herdr/fleet.toml selects the worktree's own)`. `inbox --hook` stays silent, as on any error. `send`'s `selected fleet: … (discovered)` reads `(discovered from a linked worktree)`.

The same rule runs where the plugin walks up on someone else's behalf:
- `crossfleet.fleet_root`, so a destination agent working in a worktree of its fleet's repository is routed to that fleet's mailbox; an agent of this fleet working in its own worktree is then the same root, so it stays local;
- `messages.fleet_flag` (33.5), so a printed `herdr-fleet inbox` command run from a worktree needs no `--fleet`;
- the hook's fleet lookup (`mailroom._hook_seat`).

**Mail already stranded.** `doctor` lists the repository's linked worktrees (`git worktree list`, read-only as invariant 8 says). For each worktree whose `.herdr/` has no state file but has queued messages (`inbox/*/new/*.md`), it warns: `warn stranded mail: 2 queued in <worktree> (sample-dev 1, sample-reviewer 1); nothing delivers them: herdr-fleet inbox --fleet <worktree>/.herdr/fleet.toml --as NAME --read`. It moves nothing.

Section 2's discovery paragraph gains: "In a linked Git worktree, discovery uses the main checkout's fleet file (39.1)."

### 39.2 Kimi's input box is checked

**The facts.**
- Kimi Code draws its input as a box: `╭───╮`, then `│ > ` with the cursor (a reverse-video blank) and blanks to a closing `│`, then `╰───╯`, then its mode and model line and `context: …`. Read live on 7 November. With text typed, the text follows `> `. No placeholder was seen in an empty box.
- After a long idle, Kimi replaces the box with a menu (5 November): a rule, `This session has been idle for 43m and is ~300k tokens.`, `↑↓ navigate · Enter select · Esc cancel`, `Cache expired — …`, then `❯ Compact and continue`, `Start a new session`, `Continue as-is` and `Don't ask me again`, then a rule. herdr reports the seat `idle` the whole time. A brief sent then was typed into the menu, and the seat sat there for half an hour until the owner chose an option.
- Kimi has had no entry in `seats.PROMPT_GLYPHS` since 0.18.0, so the presence guard (21.2) passed every Kimi seat unread (35.5 item 15).

**The rule.**
1. `seats.PROMPT_GLYPHS["kimi"] = (">",)`, and the new `seats.PROMPT_BOXED = ("kimi",)`: for a boxed kind, a line counts as the prompt line only when its first non-blank character is a box vertical (`│` or `┃`) followed, after blanks, by the glyph. So a quoted `> ` line in Kimi's answer is not mistaken for its input, and the menu's `❯` lines are not either.
2. An empty box passes. Text in the box holds, as for any kind: `owner typing in <pane>` or `text in its input …`. No box on screen holds as a dialog or menu: `a dialog or a menu is on screen in <pane>; answer or close it there`. Kimi becomes `checkable`, and `doctor` stops listing it as unchecked.
3. Everything that already runs the guard now covers Kimi: `send`, `deliver`, the watcher, the kickoff, and every quit. A plain `send` to an idle Kimi seat with the menu up queues the message with that detail (21.2). The limited quit (35.5 item 15) keeps its `limits.input_empty` read, and `safe_prompt`'s own guard now agrees with it rather than passing blind. One thing stays out: `interrupt`'s one Ctrl-U after its keys (23.11) was verified on Claude Code alone. A boxed kind gets no Ctrl-U until Kimi's interrupt is checked live. When the interrupted prompt comes back into a Kimi box, `interrupt` ends as it does today for text it cannot clear: `its input still holds text; press Ctrl-U there before sending it anything`. (Amended after review #42d9ce, F1: the first build pressed Ctrl-U for any kind the guard can check, which now includes Kimi.)
4. **A held dialog is notified.** When the watcher has held a seat's mail for a dialog or menu (`presence.is_dialog`) for 5 minutes (`DIALOG_NOTIFY_S`, or the fleet's `[watch] notify_blocked_after` when set), it raises one notification per hold: title `NAME has a dialog or menu open (<pane>): N queued wait`, body `answer or close it there; herdr-fleet sends it nothing until its input is back`. This applies to every kind. A hold for typed text is not notified, since the owner is at that pane.

No new key path and no new prompt path: the guard only reads, as before.

### 39.3 The limit check follows deliveries

**The fact.** On 5 November a Kimi builder took a brief by a plain `send` while `idle`. Its turn ended at once with Kimi's 429 error, and it was `idle` again before the watcher next looked. `state_seen` still read `idle` with the old `since`, a state the watcher had already read once (35.5 item 13), so it never read the screen again. The designer found the limit by hand.

**The rule.** The quiet-seat check (35.6 item 2) keys each read on the state's `since` and on the seat's **last prompt**: the time of the newest log entry that put text into the seat. Those are a `send` typed at once (`sent` or `unconfirmed`), a `delivered` entry, or a `kickoff` entry. A seat is read when:
- its state is idle, done or working, its kind has patterns, and it is not held;
- its state is at least `LIMIT_QUIET_S` old, and so is its last prompt;
- the pair (`since`, last prompt) differs from the pair of its last read.

So a seat is read once per unchanged state, and once more after each prompt the log records, 5 to 10 minutes after it. The watcher reads the log only when its size or modification time has changed, and only from where it left off. Blocked and unknown seats are never read.

**Known limit.** A turn the owner starts by typing into the pane is not in the log. If it hits a limit and ends between two of the watcher's looks, it is still missed.

Invariant 22's sentence becomes "… at most every 5 minutes, once per unchanged state and once more after each prompt the message log records for that seat …".

### 39.4 `--fallback` puts a nearly used-up Codex last

**The fact.** On 5 November, `swap NAME --fallback` moved the Kimi builder to Codex when the account's Codex allowance was at 97% of its week, with the reset an hour away. The fleet ran no Codex seat, so its recorded reading was days old, and `limits.fallback_candidates` does not look at the allowance anyway. The build fitted in what was left only by luck.

**The rule.**
1. When `codex` is among a seat's candidates, `--fallback` takes the account's reading. That is the state's `codex_allowance` when it was read within `ALLOWANCE_READ_S` (10 minutes). Otherwise it is the latest rollout's `rate_limits`, from the new `context_size.latest_allowance(env)`: the most recently modified `rollout-*.jsonl` in the newest two date directories of the Codex sessions root, read backwards and bounded as 29.2 reads, numbers only.
2. No reading, or a reading whose `resets_at` has passed, counts as unknown and changes nothing.
3. At or above `failover_at_percent` before its reset, `codex` moves to the end of the candidates. It is never dropped: a nearly used-up Codex is still a fallback when nothing else starts.
4. The step says so in both cases:
   - `fallback - kimi -> claude (limit recorded; codex at 97% of the weekly allowance until Thu 20:44, tried last)`;
   - when Codex is still the one taken, `fallback - kimi -> codex (codex at 97% of the weekly allowance until Thu 20:44)`.
5. `--fallback` writes no reading to the state file; that record stays the watcher's. A dry run reads it too, since the read is read-only.

Invariant 8 gains `--fallback`'s read of the Codex rollout numbers, under the same terms as `--context`.

### 39.5 The failover note quotes only a limit that still counts

**The fact.** On 5 November, the failover note and the state's `failover.reason` quoted the seat's 2 November Kimi record, whose reset had passed three days earlier. The 5 November limit itself was never recorded (39.3).

**The rule.**
- `fallback_note` and `failover.reason` use the `limited` record only while it counts (`limits.counts`) and is of the kind being left. Otherwise the note gives the owner's request (`the owner asked for the fallback of kimi with swap NAME --fallback`), and `failover.reason` is `swap --fallback`.
- When the kind being left is Codex, the note cites the allowance only for a reading at or above `failover_at_percent` before its reset (39.4's reading).
- The step's `(limit recorded)` already uses `counts` and is unchanged.

### 39.6 Code, invariants and tests

Code:
- `fleetfile`: the linked-worktree check, pure file reads, which `find_fleet_file`'s walk and `crossfleet.fleet_root` share; the stderr note goes through `Ctx`.
- `checks`: `stranded mail` in `doctor`.
- `seats` and `presence`: the boxed prompt line.
- `watch`: the dialog-hold notification, and the limit read keyed on the last prompt.
- `context_size`: `latest_allowance`.
- `ops`: candidate order, the step line, `fallback_note` and `failover.reason`.

Invariants:
- 22 and 8 as above.
- 19 is unchanged; it now covers Kimi.
- No new herdr command, key or prompt.

`tests/support.py`: the invariant 19 oracle (`owner_typing`) learns Kimi's box, so a prompt to a Kimi pane with text in its box, or with no box on screen, fails the test. `fake_herdr` draws Kimi's box (empty, with `typed`) and its idle-cache menu (`show_dialog`).

Tests go in `tests/test_section39.py`. Their fixtures are relative to the test clock (38.5).
- Worktrees, against real repositories (`git init`, `git worktree add`):
  - discovery from a linked worktree takes the main checkout's fleet and prints the note once;
  - `--fleet`, `--file` and `HERDR_FLEET_FILE` keep the worktree's;
  - a worktree with its own state file keeps its own;
  - so do a main checkout without a fleet file, a `.git` file without `commondir`, and a pointer that does not resolve;
  - `send` from a worktree queues into the main checkout's mailbox;
  - a cross-fleet destination agent whose directory is a worktree of its fleet routes to that fleet;
  - `fleet_flag` from a worktree adds no `--fleet`;
  - `inbox --hook` from a worktree is silent;
  - `doctor`'s `stranded mail` appears for queued files, and not for an empty or brought-up worktree.
- Kimi:
  - an empty box passes;
  - text in the box holds;
  - a quoted `> ` line above an empty box passes;
  - the menu holds as a dialog;
  - a plain `send` to an idle Kimi seat with the menu up queues;
  - the watcher holds, notifies once after 5 minutes, and delivers when the box is back;
  - existing Kimi scenarios that assumed an unchecked kind draw the box.
- The limit read:
  - a short turn (state unchanged, a `delivered`, `sent` or `kickoff` entry after the last read) is read once after `LIMIT_QUIET_S`;
  - no second read without a new prompt;
  - the log is not reread when unchanged.
- Fallback:
  - Codex demoted at or above the threshold, from a fresh state reading and from a rollout fixture when the state's is stale;
  - unchanged below the threshold, after the reset, and with no reading;
  - Codex as the only candidate is still taken, with the line;
  - a dry run writes nothing.
- The note:
  - a passed or released record is not quoted, and `failover.reason` is `swap --fallback`;
  - a counting record is quoted;
  - a Codex reading below the threshold is not cited.

### 39.7 Version and documents

0.19.4 in `herdr-plugin.toml` and `herdr_fleet/__init__.py`. README:
- the fleet-file discovery paragraph gives the worktree rule and its note;
- the presence guard lists Kimi's box;
- `--fallback` says how Codex's allowance orders the candidates.

AGENTS.md's architecture paragraph says the same in its own places: discovery, `seats.PROMPT_GLYPHS`, the quiet-seat read and `_swap_fallback`. `docs/testing.md` lists `tests/test_section39.py` and the fake's Kimi screens.

### 39.8 Amendments from the build

- **Fresh state readings.** The state's `codex_allowance` is fresh only when its `read_at` is no more than `ALLOWANCE_READ_S` old; a record without a usable `read_at` falls back to `context_size.latest_allowance`.
- **Leaving Codex.** `--fallback` takes the 39.4 reading when the kind being left is Codex as well as when Codex is a candidate, so 39.5 can cite a current above-threshold reading. It is still never written to the state file by this command.
- **Review round 1.** F1 takes option (a): `_clear_input` reads a boxed kind's restored text and returns `held` without Ctrl-U; `KeysCheck` rejects that key for a boxed kind. `timers` uses `Ctx.find_fleet`, which notes the switch without invoking the caller lookup. The prompt-log signature includes the inode and a changed inode resets the offset to zero. N1 needs no code change: the existing last-component `.git` test matches the amended rule. The no-`commondir` fixture now uses an existing Git directory, typed-text holds are tested not to notify, and the real Git fixtures use an isolated environment.

### 39.9 Verification (designer, 7 November 2026)

These checks ran live on the working tree (herdr 0.9.5) after review round 1, and again where round 2 touched them. Every command was read-only or a dry run, except one real delivery to a Kimi seat. No owner fleet's files changed.

- **Worktree discovery.** The scratch repository committed `.herdr/fleet.toml` and had one linked worktree, both reached through the symlinked `/tmp`.
  - Run from the worktree, `status` and `send --dry-run --as owner` printed the 39.1 note once, used the main checkout's fleet, and named its mailbox.
  - `send` said `(discovered from a linked worktree)`.
  - `status --fleet <worktree>/.herdr/fleet.toml` kept the worktree's own fleet.
  - A message file placed in the worktree's mailbox made `doctor`, run from the main checkout, print `warn stranded mail: 1 queued in <worktree> (sample-dev 1); …`.
- **Kimi's box.** The guard ran read-only against a live Kimi Code seat (K3.1 Pro), using `--format ansi`, the seat's real empty box with its reverse-video cursor.
  - `prompt_line` gave the empty string and `typing` gave None: mail may go.
  - The idle-cache menu of 39.2, as text, gave no prompt line: it holds as a dialog.
  - A quoted `> ` line above an empty box passed.
  - A real `send` through the working-tree CLI to the idle Kimi seat was delivered at once, and the seat took the turn.
- **The Codex allowance.** `context_size.latest_allowance` read the account's live reading from the newest rollout: 4% of the weekly window.
  - On a scratch fleet with `failover_at_percent = 3` and `prefer = ["kimi", "codex", "claude"]`, `swap sample-dev --fallback --dry-run` printed `fallback - kimi -> claude (codex at 4% of the weekly allowance until Thu 20:44, tried last)`. It created no state file.
  - On the development fleet, below its threshold, `swap NAME --fallback --dry-run` chose Codex with no note and left the state file byte-identical.

**Not verified live**, each covered by the fake:
- text typed into a Kimi box;
- Kimi's idle-cache menu on a running seat, since it appears only after a long idle, and the dialog-hold notification;
- Kimi's shell mode (review N3), which holds as a dialog if its line is not `│ >`;
- the watcher's read after a logged prompt (39.3), since it needs a live limit;
- `interrupt` of a Kimi seat.

Open for 23.11, not 39: `interrupt`'s Ctrl-U still reaches Codex and Antigravity seats, as it has since 0.12.0. Its facts were taken on Claude Code.

## 40. Inspecting one message, reliable envelopes and safe state updates (0.20.0)

The owner accepted five areas after a fleet-feedback survey: selective inbox reading, a reliable sender/id envelope, state-section retrieval and append, clearer labels/help, and quiet FYI-expiry visibility. This section defines that scope. Native resume policy, provider configuration, tier adoption, automatic debt closure and new interrupt behavior are outside it. Existing delivery, routing, TTL, FYI wake, debt and key rules stand.

### 40.1 Inspect or read one message

`inbox --show ID` prints one stored message without consuming it. `inbox --read [ID]` keeps the existing bulk read when no ID is supplied and consumes only the selected message when one is supplied.

- Both accept a full id, a six-character short id or `#short`, resolved within the selected caller/`--as` mailbox of the selected fleet, not across other mailboxes or fleets. Unknown or ambiguous ids are usage errors naming that mailbox. `--show` searches its `new/` and `cur/`, including expired/dropped messages. `cur/` means stored history, not proof of delivery.
- `--show` is a pure file inspection: no expiry, moves, logs, waiting marker, directory/lock creation, state update or Herdr command with explicit `--as`. Implicit caller discovery retains its existing read-only identity lookup, but inspection records no `state_seen`. Print the normal identity heading, a line identifying `new/` or `cur/` and that inspection does not mark it read, then the existing message header and body. Apply `inbox_print_max`, the full-file pointer and `--full` as for bulk reads. `--show --json` returns one object with the existing message/body fields and `box` (`new` or `cur`), keeping diagnostics on stderr.
- `--read ID` requires that the selected message is in `new/`. A message already in `cur/` gives exit 3 and `already in cur/; use inbox --show ID to inspect it`, without logging or moving anything. Resolve and validate before creating any lock; re-read and revalidate the selection under the existing mailbox lock before settling it. An unknown/ambiguous reference writes nothing. If another reader delivered it first, return the same already-in-history result rather than another receipt.
- Selected reading expires only the selected message if it is due, using the existing terminal-receipt/mirroring rules, then returns exit 3 with an expiry explanation and the show command. Otherwise it settles that one message through the same `messages.settle` as bulk reading, including queued-kickoff bookkeeping and cross-fleet receipts. Other queued messages, even due ones, are untouched. Reading records delivery, not task completion or acknowledgement; kickoff debt boundaries remain as in 32.6.
- `--read ID --json` returns a one-element array on successful reading, preserving bulk read's array shape. `--read` with no ID, including `--wait`'s bulk read, keeps its existing behavior and output. `--show` is exclusive with the other action modes and with `--wait`; a selected read is incompatible with `--wait`. `--full` supports show and selected reading. Existing invalid-mode checks still apply; no `inbox --ack` alias is added.
- Explain show, selected/bulk reading, and `herdr-fleet ack` in inbox help. State plainly that a reply or acknowledgement settles a request; merely reading it does not.

### 40.2 Every ordinary direct message has its own envelope

`inbox.direct_text` always prefixes an ordinary message with `[from SENDER #short] ` using its actual metadata, even if its body already begins with `[from`. The body, including any hand-written prefix, is appended verbatim and is never rewritten on disk. This deliberately supersedes 16.1/18.11's pre-prefixed-body exemption. An ordinary direct send is thus attributable and acknowledgeable without hunting through status.

Batch headers already contain the actual sender/id and stay unchanged. Timer headers keep their timer metadata. Queued kickoffs keep 32.6's special verbatim composition and are not ordinary messages. No replay or migration of historical native prompts occurs; no additional prompt is sent. Document the compatibility change and recommend letting the tool supply the envelope.

### 40.3 State-section retrieval and serialized append

- `state get KEY` prints only the selected section's body, without its heading/update stamp, with one trailing newline when nonempty. A missing section or duplicate matching headings is an actionable usage error, not empty successful output. It creates nothing and runs no Herdr command. Section boundaries remain 16.5's level-1/level-2 headings; nested level-3 headings are part of the body.
- `state append KEY TEXT` and `state append KEY --file PATH` append to that section, creating it if absent. Existing body text is retained; trim only trailing newlines from the old body and outer newlines from the added text, joining nonempty bodies with one newline. Preserve introduction, other sections and order as `set_section` does. Stamp the updated heading as `set` does. Reject whitespace-only added text and duplicate matching sections before writing. KEY is nonempty and single-line; TEXT/--file are exclusive and use the same UTF-8 handling as set.
- Both `state set` and `state append` take the same strict exclusive `flock` on the state note's parent directory, from reading the current note through atomic replacement. Create a missing parent only for a validated write. Failure to acquire the lock fails visibly and writes nothing; do not use the state-file helper's fail-open fallback. Separate CLI processes must serialize so concurrent appends retain both additions, and set cannot read a stale copy outside that lock. No lock file or external dependency is needed. No timer or mailbox lock is acquired while this lock is held.
- `state set` still replaces the section, including its existing empty-text behavior; help names replacement explicitly and points to append. Do not add heuristics rejecting legitimate short values or shell-like characters. `state show`/`keys` and the kickoff's state-note reading remain unchanged. Keep read-only operations free of new files and retain atomic writes.

### 40.4 Labels describe the operation

- Display persisted `queued_by: refusal` as `[plain send]` wherever the queue reason is shown, including inbox and board pages. Keep the stored value, mode selection, ordering, old-file compatibility and delivery policy unchanged. `[queued]`, `[fyi]` and `[kickoff]` retain their meanings.
- Cross-fleet send output retains its sender heading, selected fleet, actual owning mailbox and origin. When a requested alias differs from the canonical recipient, say `alias ALIAS -> CANONICAL`; omit that mapping when the two names are equal. Do not substitute the sender for the alias or change actual attribution/routing. Dry runs follow the same display rule.
- README, generated help and relevant examples use the new wording and distinguish reading from acknowledging. No new message-log field or delivery rule is required for these labels.

### 40.5 Quiet inspection of recent FYI expiries

Add `log --expired-fyi`, a read-only metadata view of messages that had an FYI-expiry event in the last seven days. `status` prints one concise summary when that view is nonempty, and `status --json` adds `expired_fyi` (an array, empty when none).

- Each derived item is `{id, sender, to, at, via}`: the full id, original sender, recipient, latest expiry time and reason (`ttl` or `fyi_ttl`). Use an original `send` record's FYI flag and sender, joined by full id, never message bodies. Add `sender` and `fyi` to newly written FYI `expired` records so new terminal records remain intelligible when the original send is unavailable; old records still join through existing send metadata. Preserve ordinary terminal-record `from: herdr-fleet`, routing metadata, logging and receipt validation. Never classify timer/kickoff mail as FYI expiry.
- Deduplicate by full id, taking its latest valid expiry event. Include events with `now - 7 days <= at <= now`; ignore invalid/future times and entries that cannot be identified as an FYI from metadata. Mirrored duplicate receipts do not increase the count. This is expiry history: later redelivery/delivery does not erase that event, and the UI says so rather than implying permanent loss. No outstanding debt is inferred from it.
- Human log lines show time, original sender, recipient, short id and expiry reason. `--json` returns the derived objects. Existing `--seat` filters sender or recipient and `--last` applies after filtering (the existing nonpositive-last convention remains). `--expired-fyi` with `--owed` is a usage error. Normal log modes stay unchanged.
- Status says `expired fyi: N messages in the last 7d; some may since have been redelivered; see herdr-fleet log --expired-fyi`, with the selected fleet flag when discovery from the caller's cwd would choose another fleet. The summary includes all senders in this selected fleet, without an extra caller lookup; the detailed view names them. Reuse status's existing log read and a shared pure derivation rather than scanning bodies or repeatedly reading the log for each seat.
- This creates no counter, acknowledgement obligation, notification, prompt, new queue or watcher action. It does not change expiry, wake behavior, historical records, source-owned legacy queues or redelivery. New terminal metadata naturally comes from existing expiry paths, so watcher code updates still require a new process at rollout.

### 40.6 Implementation and required evidence

Version 0.20.0 in both declarations. Builder implements the section in the shared working tree and appends build amendments here, without editing prior design text. Update README, CLI help, AGENTS.md architecture and docs/testing.md; preserve the first three AGENTS rules. Public fixtures and prose use synthetic `examples/team` identities, never survey/deployment names, paths or copied reports. Python 3.8+ and no third-party dependencies remain required.

Add meaningful regressions in `tests/test_section40.py`, with red-before-change evidence for the new paths and compatibility assertions for existing bulk operations:
- scoped full/short/# ids; new/cur show, due/expired/dropped inspection, no-write/no-Herdr explicit show, JSON/caps/full and invalid combinations;
- reading one of several messages, selected due expiry only, unknown/ambiguous/other-mailbox zero writes, repeated/concurrent reading without duplicate receipts, selected queued kickoff and cross-fleet receipt/debt propagation;
- prefixed/unprefixed ordinary bodies with actual sender/id and unchanged stored bytes, timer/batch/kickoff compatibility;
- get section boundaries and missing/duplicate cases, append existing/missing/file/invalid bodies, preserving other sections, concurrent appends from separate processes, strict-lock failure without note replacement and set/append sharing the lock;
- clear queue/board/alias/help text, equal-name and real-alias cross-fleet sends including dry run;
- new/legacy FYI expiry metadata, seven-day/time-validation boundaries, repeated/mirrored expiry, later redelivery, non-FYI/timer exclusions, status text/JSON and filtered log text/JSON, zero writes and no extra Herdr calls.

Run focused coverage then the full suite under the available Python below 3.11 and at/above 3.11, compileall and diff checks. Keep the safety oracle active; show/read with explicit --as add no Herdr calls, and no new key or prompt path is authorized. Independent reviewer checks the final diff against this section and the public-content boundary. The designer live-smokes synthetic messages on a cheap Claude seat in a scratch fleet: non-consuming/selected reads, one generated direct envelope, state get/append, labels and an expiry summary, while preserving input holds. Record observed outcomes and untested paths here before commit. Publish/install only after the gates pass and the owner authorizes release; refresh watcher strips while retaining coding-agent sessions and fleet-file bytes.

### 40.7 Verification and limits

- **Automated evidence.** The complete suite increased from 1,370 to 1,427 methods, including 57 section-40 methods, with the existing safety oracle active. The designer's final serial runs passed all 1,427 tests on Python 3.9.6 in 81.766 s and Python 3.12.13 in 77.191 s, with no failures, errors or skips. The builder independently passed both complete suites after the fix round, and the reviewer independently passed all 57 focused cases on both interpreters. Compilation and diff checks passed. All 91 Python/launcher files parsed with Python 3.8 grammar; an actual Python 3.8 runtime was not available. README/testing-guide validation passed 21 shell blocks under each of bash and zsh, six TOML blocks and five JSON blocks.
- **Regression evidence.** Recorded red-before-change groups cover selective inbox operations, get/append and strict locking, the direct envelope and expiry view, file-only discovery, malformed typed metadata, an explicitly empty selected id, and selected header/path validation. Independent review caught two further errors: leaked-action failure reporting invoked a notification for file operations, and a move between the new/cur scans made one full id appear ambiguous. The correction's red cases reproduced both, including all six show/read and full/short/#short move interleavings; positive lifecycle-notification and genuine-ambiguity controls remain green. The other new cases were exercised green rather than each independently demonstrated red. Final independent review returned MERGE with no remaining actionable finding.
- **Scratch file acceptance.** Real CLI subprocesses against a disposable fleet confirmed non-consuming full/short-id inspection with unchanged bytes/mtimes, unknown-id zero writes, one selected read leaving the rest queued, a repeated read without another receipt, and selected expiry after a real one-minute TTL. The expiry history retained the original sender and appeared in log and status text/JSON. Two separate append processes retained both additions and the original section while another section stayed unchanged. Plain-send queue labels and missing-section diagnostics matched the design. After the fixes, file-only failure commands under a leaked action environment returned their usage errors without executing a rejecting Herdr sentinel or changing the scratch tree.
- **Native acceptance.** Under Herdr 0.9.5 and Claude Code 2.1.319, a disposable Haiku 5.5 seat with tools and MCP servers disabled received a synthetic pre-prefixed body inside the actual owner/id envelope and produced the expected standalone acknowledgement. This was checked initially and repeated on the reviewed source after the fixes. Both created scratch workspaces were cleaned up through the fleet lifecycle commands. No production job, owner-fleet configuration, agent key, dialog answer or focus request was used for acceptance.
- **Public boundary and limits.** The 110-file candidate passed the checked private-inventory/home-path and credential-pattern scans with zero findings; local evidence and deployment inventory stay under ignored files. The first three AGENTS rules, section 7 invariants and pre-40 design text remain unchanged. This is a current-tree check, not a Git-history audit. Cross-fleet receipts, the move-between-scans race, lock failures, input holds and other provider layouts have automated coverage rather than a live production experiment; owner-typed drafts and dialogs were not manufactured for this release. No new prompt/key/lifecycle/provider policy was introduced. Publication, installation and watcher/session comparisons are separate deployment checks recorded privately.
## 41. A local fleet dashboard with a watch-only terminal (0.21.0)

The owner selected the Signal Box visual direction, its Deep-field Console treatment and animated Live line study, with an expandable terminal and collapsible fleet column, then authorised implementation. The first release includes monitoring, messages/replies and live terminal **Watch**. Writable terminal control follows separately. Existing agent-input, identity, delivery, ownership and privacy rules remain binding; no permission dialog is answered by this feature.

### 41.1 Entry point, roster and lifetime

- Add `herdr-fleet dashboard [--fleet PATH ...] [--all-fleets] [--port N] [--open] [--context] [--demo]`. It is a foreground, owner-started process, prints its local launch address, and stops with Ctrl-C. It binds only `127.0.0.1`; default port is zero (an available port). `--open` deliberately opens the launch address in the default browser. Do not create a daemon, watcher, coding seat, plugin pane or external service. No normal command starts it implicitly.
- Repeated `--fleet` paths are explicit registrations, validated and canonicalised using the existing fleet loader/discovery rules. Without paths, normal cwd/environment discovery supplies the first fleet. `--all-fleets` additionally discovers conventional fleet files from the cwd of live agents in this one Herdr session, deduplicating canonical roots; never recursively scan the user's filesystem. The dashboard can monitor every registered/discovered fleet; it never edits those files. Warn about invalid/disappearing registrations and isolate their failures. Stable opaque fleet ids map to server-owned paths; HTTP clients cannot supply filesystem paths or add registrations.
- Live mode requires the existing inside-Herdr/reachable/version preconditions, without status collection, pruning or caller-side writes. Bind discovery to that Herdr binary/session, not whatever desktop pane has focus. Retain unreachable fleets as unavailable rather than substituting another session. Roster additions under `--all-fleets` use the same bounded discovery; removal of a fleet/seat invalidates its streams and pending action view.
- `--demo` serves only synthetic fixtures and the authored sample run, works without Herdr/fleet files and performs no Herdr call or fleet write. It cannot enable live APIs. Live and Demo are conspicuously distinct. No request from Demo submits a real reply.
- Use standard-library Python for the service and ship static browser assets. There is no frontend build or Node runtime requirement for users. Retain Python 3.8 compatibility. Package version becomes 0.21.0; existing CLI/manifest action behavior stays intact.

### 41.2 The selected interface

Use the accepted command-console design as the visual reference, with two primary desktop columns: 25% fleet selector and 75% selected fleet. The selector carries state lamps, freshness and attention; the selected fleet contains owner questions, agent seats and Terminal / Fleet log / Messages. There is no extra feature-navigation rail.

- The fleet column collapses to a compact, labelled-accessible rail with a persistent restore button. Fleet switching remains available. Terminal expansion/restoration gives more space within the page without invoking Herdr zoom, resize or focus. Both layout operations preserve the selected target, drafts, output and logical read-only Watch selection, and do not restart Demo playback or general data collection. If viewport dimensions change, one debounced observer replacement is allowed with visible reconnect and a fresh full frame; it never resizes/focuses/zooms the agent pane. Mobile reflows without horizontal page overflow. Preserve keyboard focus and reduced-motion support.
- All live fields come from observations or recorded events. Idle is not accepted work; reply settlement is not acceptance. Distinguish agent state, queue/receipt state and an agent's reported result. Unknown readings remain unknown. Context usage/allowance is not money or task completion. Show the observation time and disconnected/stale state; a stopped watcher must not look like a fresh active one. A permission-denied PID liveness check means unknown, not stopped; distinguish it from a confirmed missing process.
- Plain owner messages remain plain text with a reply field. Do not infer options, urgency, dependencies, stages, expected duration or acceptance from prose. The six-stage authored sequence belongs only to Demo. Live mode must not manufacture its progress rail or replay unrecorded pane history.
- Demo includes several synthetic fleets and an authored event sequence with an owner hold, matching terminal/log/message updates and per-stage evidence ids. Authenticated `POST /api/demo/replay` and `POST /api/demo/reply` accept only `{}` in Demo, restart or release that local authored hold, and return `{"demo": true}`. Repeated replies outside a hold are harmless. No typed sample reply leaves the browser, no route exists in Live, and neither endpoint reaches owner operations, Herdr or files. Extra Demo fields (`hold`, `stage_evidence`, seat `waiting`) remain synthetic; Live draws a waiting marker only from an actual waiting record.
- Animate newly observed state changes and message events using their actual identities and routing. The first historical snapshot is not replayed as new activity. Deduplicate mirrored cross-fleet events. Cosmetic travel/reveal duration never postpones the displayed confirmed receipt or determines a work state. Working activity is indeterminate; no guessed percentage/time remaining. Motion can be paused/disabled independently of live data, and reduced-motion preference is respected. Hidden-page resume reconciles a fresh snapshot, without inventing missed events.
- Ship public synthetic examples using `examples/team` names. Do not copy private fleet names, pane/session identifiers, paths, schedules, report text or screenshots into tracked assets, tests or docs. The designer's private studies are references, not files to publish verbatim.

### 41.3 Pure observation and bounded data

Create a deliberately read-only observation adapter. Do not call `status --json`, `collect_status` or `board.build` unchanged: those paths update seen state, prune/save ids or label panes. Factor/share pure readers where useful while retaining existing command behavior.

- Share one bounded current `agent list` snapshot per refresh across fleets; poll about every two seconds, with file-signature caches for slower state/log reads. Read fleet/state files, mailbox metadata and the existing append-only message/watch logs without creating locks/directories, expiring mail, marking delivery, renaming panes or rewriting state. Never call permission/config setup helpers that create directories during observation. `--context` opts into the existing numbers-only transcript reader; no transcript content or source path is returned.
- Summary payloads contain seat identities, state/freshness, queue counts with FYIs separate, owed/owner requests, watcher/limit metadata and available context numbers. Read message bodies only for explicit listings/history/selected-message inspection; do not analyse them. Fetch bounded recent log/history pages on demand, with explicit limits/truncation, not the entire history per poll. Render untrusted bodies/log lines as inert text.
- Preserve the last successful observation on transient failure and mark it stale. A bad fleet does not erase healthy fleets. Bound retained history and file reads; no provider transcript, terminal output or message-body cache is written to new dashboard files. The dashboard is not an independent scheduler or mailbox delivery worker.

### 41.4 Local browser authority

Loopback binding alone is not authentication. The page can expose private messages and cause guarded sends, so the service must establish an explicit per-launch owner session.

- Generate a cryptographically random launch capability (at least 256 bits), held in memory. Place it in the printed launch URL's fragment, never a query string. The local bootstrap exchanges it through a same-origin JSON POST, removes the fragment promptly and establishes an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict session cookie plus a per-session anti-forgery value for API requests. Process a later launch fragment from a same-page hash change identically; pasting the full address into an already open bare page must consume/remove it rather than leave it unused. Apart from the scoped HttpOnly session cookie, no capability/session secret is written to disk or included in request logs, error traces, telemetry, HTML assets, localStorage or sessionStorage. A server restart invalidates sessions. Do not claim `--as owner` is authentication.
- The capability-exchange POST is the sole unauthenticated session API and requires exact present, non-null bound Origin and JSON. Validate the exact bound Host/port; require present, non-null, matching Origin on every mutation. All subsequent data/stream/session APIs require both the session cookie and an independently secret anti-forgery header; cookie possession alone cannot mint/recover that header. Page reload reauthenticates through the original launch-capability URL; do not add an unspecified cookie-only recovery endpoint. Use the session's anti-forgery header for API access; the browser can consume streaming responses using `fetch`, without putting secrets in URLs. No permissive CORS, JSONP or arbitrary proxy endpoint. Reject foreign fetch contexts where supplied. Use no-store responses and deny framing. No third-party network requests or remotely loaded executable/font assets at runtime; only the explicitly allowlisted locally vendored xterm bundles are permitted.
- Serve only a fixed allowlist of packaged assets, with correct content types, no directory listing/path traversal/symlink escape. Use a restrictive Content-Security-Policy with same-origin external scripts/styles and no eval/inline executable handlers, plus nosniff and no-referrer. The trusted terminal renderer's generated styles may use a fresh per-response style nonce; do not broadly enable inline styles or nonce untrusted content. This style nonce is distinct from owner authentication secrets. Dynamic data uses safe DOM construction/text, never body interpolation into HTML or scripts. Limits cover request bodies, connections, stream buffers and timeouts. HTTP errors return actionable bounded messages without exposing secrets.
- Keep owner sessions bounded without locking a valid launch capability out after repeated reloads: a valid new capability exchange may invalidate/replace the oldest session at capacity, or reuse an existing valid session only after validating that full capability. Scope cookie identities to this server launch, and preserve other active tabs' authority when another tab exchanges the capability; separate loopback dashboard ports must not overwrite each other's cookie. This grants no cookie-only recovery endpoint. Only a successfully authenticated action handler may invoke the narrow owner-operation adapter below. Browser-provided author names, command arrays, paths or extra flags are not accepted. Existing CLI caller resolution is not weakened or bypassed for other commands.

### 41.5 Messages and replies through the existing engine

The first release permits explicit owner sends/replies and selected owner-mailbox reading. It does not expose raw terminal input, `--now`, arbitrary commands, lifecycle actions, bulk delivery/drop/ack, agent focus, provider approvals or other-user mailbox consumption.

- A send/reply is a validated JSON POST naming a registered source fleet id, a registered destination seat, text, a unique intent id and, for replies, the exact original message id. Resolve paths and routing on the server. Each actionable seat/watch view carries an opaque server-issued generation bound to canonical fleet, seat, pane, kind and available native/terminal identity. POST/watch must present it; freshly reload/revalidate configuration and recipient identity, reject missing/stale/removed/rebound identity and ask the browser to refresh. Revalidate the actual engine-resolved destination and receipt roots against the approved roster; never silently fall back to another root or target. Contacts outside the approved roster are viewable as metadata but cannot be used to route a write outside it.
- Dispatch as explicit owner through existing `messages.op_send`/delivery helpers with ordinary send semantics and full reply ids. Do not add a prompt path or reinterpret content. The inbox-first write, cross-fleet ownership/receipts, FYI policy, presence guard, blocked/unknown/working holds and unconfirmed-prompt handling all remain. A working seat keeps the reply queued; absence of a watcher is visible and does not start one. No polling request delivers mail.
- Viewing an owner question is pure inspection. An explicit Read action uses existing selected owner-inbox reading. Reply may include that explicit selected read before send; if the request is expired/invalid, show that result and do not send. Resolve the original full id only in the selected fleet's owner mailbox, verify `to=owner`, and bind the reply destination to its validated original sender. Validate every routing/receipt root against the roster before mutation, retaining normal cross-fleet propagation. Already-read history is replyable only with matching delivery evidence; `cur/` alone is insufficient. Expired/dropped and history-only undelivered records are ineligible. Check current expiry and terminal receipts; uncertain history requires refresh or an explicit separate plain send. Never consume another seat's queued mail to display it. Clearly show queued, delivered, expired, refused and unconfirmed results without treating HTTP success as delivery or work acceptance.
- Guard duplicate clicks and retries with a durable, metadata-only per-source-fleet intent journal under `.herdr/`. Reserve the intent under a strict interprocess lock before any selected read/send, release that lock before mailbox work, and record the terminal outcome afterward. Fingerprint the action type, canonical source/destination identities, view generation, reply id, read-before-send choice and body hash. Store ids, identity and finite result metadata, never message/terminal bodies or raw engine output. Journal corruption, locking/reservation/durability failure fails closed before effects. No automatic deletion/pruning may make an old intent executable again. Identical completed intent retries return the prior result; changed payload with the same id conflicts. After reservation, record any completed selected read and known outgoing id independently of send/HTTP success. Later validation/I/O/response failures retain the reservation and never imply nothing happened. An interrupted/in-progress intent, including a crash after reading or destination enqueue, is unconfirmed and is never automatically reissued after a crash. A lost HTTP response must not cause a fresh send. Preserve drafts on errors and make any deliberate new attempt explicit.
- An optional internal per-target outcome callback on `messages.op_send` may expose the actual message id, canonical source/destination/recipient, finite delivery certainty/status and exit code to the dashboard adapter. With no callback, existing CLI behavior, output and Herdr calls are unchanged. Derive certainty from the actual engine outcome/receipts, not merely `new/cur` location or exit zero; never expose/store bodies or raw engine output through it. A callback/journal failure after a send remains potentially delivered and must never trigger another send.
- Use fresh request-local operation contexts/output buffers, not a shared mutable `Ctx` across HTTP threads. The authenticated adapter's use of explicit owner attribution grants no generic owner execution surface. Tests must prove authentication/validation failure creates no mailbox, intent journal, message, notification or prompt.

### 41.6 Read-only live terminal

These are the Watch-only rules introduced in 0.21.0. Watch remains passive. Section 45 separately permits the authenticated human terminal-control seam and its named safety amendments; it supersedes only this section's blanket exclusion of that separate Control interface, not the observer's closed stdin, automatic-input prohibitions or automated key guards.

The live terminal is an actual renderer of Herdr output, not plain repeated screen scraping. Vendor the MIT-licensed production browser bundles/styles for `@xterm/xterm` 6.0.0 and its first-party `@xterm/addon-fit` 0.11.0, retaining licences, upstream attribution, versions and integrity metadata. A documented local patch may propagate the per-response style nonce to xterm's four trusted dynamic stylesheet-creation sites; retain upstream integrity and record the patched asset's integrity separately. These are packaged static assets, not a Node/Python runtime dependency or runtime CDN download. Disable input and unnecessary clipboard/link behavior; no attach addon, onData forwarding, executable links or custom escape handler that writes to the host.

- Only an explicit authenticated selection opens a watch. Resolve an approved live seat to its authoritative terminal identity; never accept an arbitrary terminal/pane id or shell command from the browser. Require the server-issued view generation, revalidate the selected name/pane/kind/session against fresh Herdr data, use immutable terminal targeting where available, and stop on removal/rebinding. Reconnect revalidates identity; unavailable identity stops Watch. Do not promise atomic session targeting beyond Herdr's actual guarantees.
