A stale/absent target cannot silently switch to another agent. Contacts have no terminal access until their fleet/seat is registered.
- The only new Herdr streaming command is `herdr terminal session observe TARGET --cols N --rows N`, through `herdr.py` with stdin closed. Keep all Herdr execution in that module. Extend its forbidden-command guard to reject `terminal session control`, writable/direct attach and any new terminal-input path. Add no `agent send-keys`, pane input, focus, resize or zoom call. Watch may show a blocked seat's screen because the owner explicitly selected it; it cannot answer it. Label native dialogs with the instruction to handle them in Herdr.
- Validate bounded viewport dimensions and bounded newline-delimited `terminal.frame` data (`encoding: "ansi"`, base64 string `bytes`, `full`, `seq`, `width` and `height`), decode its ANSI bytes and handle full/delta/closed records. Render only the current chosen stream. No raw output is persisted or injected into HTML. Terminal controls are copy/select, expand/restore and Watch connection state; Take control and input fields are absent or clearly disabled as unavailable in this release.
- A viewport change can restart only that observer at the new read-only viewport dimensions; it never resizes the actual agent pane. Debounce resize and make reconnect/gaps visible. Do not replay old buffered frames onto a replacement. When the selected seat/fleet changes, clear the old screen or retain an explicit disconnected provenance caption naming its actual former source; never display old output as the newly selected seat's screen. Multiple browser viewers are passive observers, not controllers.
- Bound concurrent observers, buffers and output records; release obsolete/disconnected streams promptly. A healthy idle terminal may remain silent: retain the explicitly selected passive Watch while its target, authenticated owner session and client remain valid, without an arbitrary stream-age or silent-output cutoff. Revalidate session authority as well as identity during stream ticks; authentication expiry/revocation stops the stream. Bound request setup and native reads/checks, and stop/close/reap child observers on deselection, authenticated stream close, terminal removal or dashboard shutdown. Herdr 0.9.4/unsupported observe reports Watch unavailable without failing the rest of the dashboard. Missing/malformed/oversized records fail that stream visibly and send no input. Stream transport failure is not proof the agent exited, and malformed/rebound/error streams are not automatically retried.

### 41.7 Safety and public boundary

This adds a visible foreground service, not a background survivor: all its workers and observers belong to its lifetime. Preserve section 7's current text and first-three AGENTS rules, extending the safety oracle for the dashboard's pure observation, explicit guarded messages and selected read-only observer. HTTP sampling is passive browser observation; dashboard Watch does not expand the watcher board's invariant-22 permissions. Existing watcher/key/own-seat tests remain intact. No owner fleet configuration is changed for implementation or acceptance testing. Do not install an unreviewed candidate or refresh production strips for this work. Publication/push remains the owner's separate decision.

### 41.8 Required verification and documentation

Automated coverage must exercise the CLI/demo entrypoint; canonical repeated/discovered rosters; zero-write observation under valid/invalid/missing state and partial log changes; bounded updates and stale recovery; body escaping and no transcript leakage; exact host/origin/session/anti-forgery checks; traversal and wrong-target rejection; authentication/schema/initial roster failures with zero side effects, and later failures retaining reservations/known partial outcomes; intent deduplication/concurrency/crash uncertainty; correct owner read/reply/cross-fleet/working/blocked/input-held semantics; forbidden input/controller/attach paths; fake terminal frames, malformed/oversized/unsupported/closed/replaced targets and observer cleanup. Run the safety oracle for Herdr call scenarios. Unit tests use fake processes/Herdr, never live agents.

Verify browser interactions and rendering at desktop/mobile widths: roster/seat changes, logs/messages, real-data versus Demo distinction, motion controls, input absence, terminal expansion, fleet collapse, retained drafts/selection/scroll and stream cleanup. Run new security and backend checks below and above Python 3.11, then the full suite on both, compileall, diff check and public-content/licence audit. Update README/help, testing instructions and AGENTS architecture with the actual interface and limits; use synthetic examples. Do not claim a browser, Python version or platform was exercised without evidence.

The designer then uses a disposable scratch fleet with a cheap Claude seat for live acceptance: authenticated fresh observations and no-write checks, one real owner message/reply with existing receipt/holds, read-only ANSI watch and viewport/selection cleanup, invalid-origin/input rejection with no keys, and clean shutdown. No production job or owner fleet is a test target. Record actual live results/limits in 41.9 before committing.

### 41.9 Verification

- **Automated evidence.** The complete suite increased from 1,427 to 1,483 methods, including 56 dashboard methods with the safety oracle active. The designer's final serial runs passed all 1,483 tests on Python 3.9.6 in 81.825 s and Python 3.12.13 in 70.552 s, with no failures, errors or skips. Six shipped-script JavaScript regressions also passed; they cover duplicate clicks/new attempts, deferred outcomes across draft/target/fleet/reply switches and edits, obsolete Watch errors and capability recovery/convergence. The reviewer independently passed those six plus two additional source-switch/retry probes. The initial full Python run exposed three old exact-ignore-list assertions; their expected entries were corrected without changing the runtime or weakening the safety oracle. Compilation, JavaScript syntax and diff checks passed. All 97 Python/entrypoint files parsed with Python 3.8 grammar; an actual 3.8 runtime was unavailable. Documentation validation passed 23 shell blocks under both bash and zsh, six TOML blocks and five JSON blocks. Independent final review of the corrected code and evidence returned MERGE with no remaining actionable finding.
- **Browser evidence.** An independent visual pass against the integrated checkout passed 65 checks on headless Chrome 157 under macOS, with Python 3.9.6 serving Demo and unchanged source pins during the pass. It covered the accepted event choreography, evidence-linked stages, authored owner hold/reply/replay, expanding terminal and compact fleet rail, retained drafts/selection, keyboard controls, motion preference and layouts at 1440, 1024 and 375 pixels. A quiet Demo Watch stayed open for 130 seconds and then received its next frame without reconnecting. Runtime resources stayed local; no console error or warning was reported. After the frontend corrections, a separate Chromium 151.0.7922.34 pass ran 28 checks against the real engine with fake Herdr, including one selected-read/reply, duplicate-click suppression and an explicit new attempt, inert hostile-looking body text, rendered fake ANSI, viewport/selection/rebinding/mobile behavior and four reaped observer children, with the safety oracle active and no browser/CSP/external-request errors. Seventeen additional browser checks exercised actual session expiry, revocation and authority replacement on the same port: a pasted valid launch capability exchanged once, removed its fragment and restored polling with one timer while closing the old Watch. These are synthetic browser/fake-native checks; the separate native evidence follows.
- **Native scratch acceptance.** The designer used two disposable fleet roots and one tools-disabled Haiku 5.5 seat under Herdr 0.9.5 and Claude Code 2.1.322. Authenticated observations/listings and invalid authority/input requests left scratch bytes/mtimes unchanged. Real ANSI full frames arrived at two read-only viewport dimensions, with the native pane's row count unchanged. One cross-fleet owner reply produced a confirmed receipt, read only its selected original and left another owner request queued. Retrying the identical intent returned its prior id without new writes or another send. The actual standalone model acknowledgement was observed. A separate synthetic turn was explicitly observed working before a second send; that message remained queued after completion with no watcher running. The candidate services stopped, both observed native children were reaped, and the released lifecycle command removed the created seat/workspace. No production job, owner-fleet edit, agent key, dialog answer or focus request was used.
- **Acceptance limits.** The initial harness checked for the model acknowledgement before it was visible; a later read recovered the existing acknowledgement without resending. One later harness startup read did not find a launch address and retained no diagnostic, so its cause cannot be attributed; a retry before any message submission succeeded. Native full frames and pane-row preservation were checked; delta/closed/malformed/auth-loss paths and other pane dimensions have fake-process/HTTP coverage, not equivalent native experiments. Private native terminal output was not loaded into the browser for these checks. No screen reader, real phone, other browser or actual Python 3.8 runtime was exercised. Capability/session secrets and terminal payloads were not written to evidence files. Publication and installation remain separate owner decisions.
- **Public and vendor boundary.** The 126-file candidate passed the checked private-deployment/home-path and credential-pattern scan with zero findings. The designer independently verified the two pinned official npm archive SHA-512 values, all five packaged SHA-256 values, unchanged upstream CSS/MIT licences and the exact documented four-site nonce patch/source-map removals. The first three AGENTS rules, section 7 and pre-41 design text remain unchanged. This is a current-tree check, not a Git-history audit. Publication, installation and watcher refresh remain separate owner decisions.

## 42. A continuous owner conversation in the dashboard (0.21.1)

The first dashboard delivered owner messages but left their acknowledgements in the log and their answers in a separate manually refreshed mailbox. The owner expected a conversation in the widget used to send the message. This correction closes that loop while preserving the selected visual design and all existing authority, receipt, delivery, privacy and terminal-input rules.

### 42.1 The conversation experience

- Turn the selected agent's owner-message composer into a conversation panel: a chronological thread immediately above the input, with the owner and agent clearly distinguished. Keep the fleet selector, terminal/log/history views and sci-fi treatment. The thread remains accessible when the terminal expands or the fleet column collapses. Mailbox names, CLI commands, intent ids and manual refresh are not steps in the normal conversation flow.
- Show the owner's outgoing message immediately as Sending, then reconcile its actual full message id and finite outcome. Preserve an exact intent correlation through trusted message metadata or the validated journal, so a pending row can reconcile even after a lost HTTP response; never deduplicate by body text or allocate a replacement intent automatically. Later recorded delivery, acknowledgement and linked replies update that same message. Display acknowledgement as a status/receipt on the original; do not fabricate an agent reply bubble from an ack, a state change or the absence of owed debt. Agent idle/done is not a reply or accepted work. Show queue holds, unconfirmed results and failures honestly.
- New incoming agent messages and replies appear in the selected conversation within the normal approximately two-second refresh cycle, without opening another tab or clicking Refresh. A response received while another agent is selected must remain in its own thread. Mark other threads as having new activity without switching the owner's target or focus. Preserve draft text, selection, scroll position and pending/uncertain actions across polling, target/fleet/reply switches, expansion and authentication recovery. Scroll to new activity only while already following the end; otherwise show a New messages affordance. Do not replace input DOM or steal keyboard focus every poll.
- Keep a bounded Recent conversations control inside the selected-fleet pane for visited source/target/seat combinations, including a target in another registered fleet after its triggering owner question leaves the pending list. Label the source and target clearly, retain only opaque registered identities and existing session-local state, and switch only on an explicit owner click. Navigation sends or reads nothing, preserves drafts and response-generation guards, and uses real metadata for activity badges. Label those counters as new activity, not unseen messages; an explicit recall clears only that thread's browser-local counter even when its last rendered row is unchanged. This adds no navigation column or write endpoint; the normal fleet/seat selection remains the default thread.
- After a confirmed queued/delivered submission, move its retained intent/result into the originating conversation record and clear the composer only if its monotonic draft generation still matches the submitted draft; text equality is insufficient after editing away and back. Retire only that submitted reply_to/read-before-reply selection when its generation still matches, so the next ordinary message is plain unless the owner explicitly selects a reply. Preserve newer text/reply/read choices. The owner can type and send the next message normally, including deliberately typing the same text again as a distinct intent. Pending duplicate clicks cannot send again, and a late response cannot clear a newer edit, another draft or another submission's intent. Refused/expired/dropped results are not this success transition. Retain uncertain attempts and their original ids; later exact association and positive receipt evidence may resolve that same submission under the same generation checks, without resending, while missing/progress-only evidence cannot. Deliberate resubmission after remaining uncertainty stays explicit and warns about possible duplicate delivery. Clear successes must not require a New attempt button for every ordinary next message.
- Preserve the separate Messages/history view for inspection, and refresh its visible content when new relevant data arrives. Owner question/reply linkage and status come from metadata, not guesses about prose. Demo remains conspicuously synthetic and cannot send live messages; its local authored reply can appear locally in the Demo conversation without transmitting typed text.

### 42.2 Pure, authenticated conversation data

- Add a bounded authenticated GET conversation endpoint with registered source-fleet id, target-fleet id and canonical seat name, plus bounded history/cursor parameters if needed. No client paths, arbitrary mailbox names or author overrides. Validate both registered fleets and the seat's declared membership; history may remain readable when that seat is absent, while sends still require section 41's fresh live generation. All section 41 Host/session/anti-forgery/no-store/CSP bounds remain.
- Build the thread from the selected source fleet's stored owner-to-target history, the approved target fleet's matching delivery files/receipts where necessary, and source-owner mailbox messages from that canonical agent. Cross-fleet messages have one canonical bubble per full id. Validate source/destination roots and paired metadata before following a counterpart; never scan arbitrary roots supplied in headers. Filter headers before loading bodies, then validate identity, participants and immutable route/provenance from the same bounded bytes used for the body, so a replacement between reads cannot expose another message's body. An unrelated owner/agent pair must not appear merely because its ids or short suffixes collide. For a different source and target root, incoming file and log-only history requires a validated route or an exact reply_to bound to a validated selected original; current roster membership alone does not prove historical origin. Local and routed history, new/cur movement and history-only mirrors must be handled deliberately.
- For older routed answers, the existing mirrored ack with via=reply may identify reply_id/reply_fleet while the body remains in the agent fleet's owner mailbox. Follow only that exact full-id reference in an already-approved target root after validating the original route, sender, owner recipient and full reply_to. Never broadly import that root's owner mailbox. Include the legacy answer once without migration or writes; invalid/missing reference evidence remains incomplete. Expose a bounded metadata-only intent-to-message-id/result association from the source journal for this validated conversation. Join optimistic rows only through that association; absent/crash-before-id evidence remains uncertain, never body/time matching or automatic resend.
- Join finite delivery and explicit acknowledgement/reply evidence by full id and validated participants, route and recording root for every kind of record, including send records. A reply's reply_to binds it to its original owner message. An explicit full-id acknowledgement remains visible when its validated message is queued or its delivery record arrives later; the acknowledgement does not promote transport state to delivered. Generic legacy acknowledgement may be represented only with the existing log's actual participant/order semantics; do not reinterpret a missing debt, kickoff or unrelated reply as an exact acknowledgement. Preserve separate queued/delivered/unconfirmed/expired/dropped state rather than flattening everything into success. Incomplete/truncated or conflicting evidence remains visibly incomplete/uncertain. Keep collection/history completeness separate from the evidence for an individual selected message: omitting an unrelated unbound item retains a thread warning without downgrading independently validated message receipts or blocking their exact intent reconciliation. A selected message's own missing body/receipt, identity/route conflict or insufficient evidence remains uncertain.
- These GETs are pure: no locks/directories, expiry, moves, delivery, acknowledgement, notifications, seen-state writes or transcript reads. Loading a conversation does not consume another seat's mailbox or silently mark all owner messages read. Reuse bounded cached metadata/log reads; fetch bodies only for the explicitly selected conversation, cap retained rows and body bytes, and indicate truncation/older history. Avoid full fleet/body rescans on every snapshot or a background delivery worker.
- Poll only the active thread/visible history on the existing refresh lifetime, with one scheduler and guarded response generations. Late requests from a previous selection/authentication epoch cannot overwrite the current conversation. Pause hidden-page work and reconcile on return without replaying fictitious activity. Disconnect/stale state is explicit and never wipes a draft or a retained uncertain action.

### 42.3 Returning the agent's answer to its conversation

- Dashboard-origin owner sends carry a versioned internal dashboard_chat object: v is integer 1, source_file is the canonical source fleet file and intent is the original bounded browser intent id. The immutable Message sender/to/full id and existing origin_fleet/destination_fleet fields bind this object to its actual conversation; do not duplicate or infer these identities from body text. Only the authenticated owner adapter supplies it through a narrow optional internal op_send argument; it is not an arbitrary browser field or a new authorisation mechanism. Persist it through message serialisation, queued/batched delivery, cross-fleet history/counterparts and selected inbox reads, and include it in paired-header receipt equality. Do not change the stored user body. Ordinary CLI sends, old messages, timers, kickoffs and other channels keep their current rendering and semantics.
- At an actual dashboard-owner message's delivery, generate concise agent-facing guidance outside the stored body: return the user-facing answer through the fleet owner inbox using the original full id and the source fleet selected in the dashboard. Ack records receipt/settlement and is insufficient for a requested conversational answer. Guidance must work for direct, batched, queued/pointer and printed inbox-read paths; JSON consumers receive the corresponding metadata. Respect existing task/dialog/safety rules; this is not permission to answer another agent's dialog or bypass a blocked/working/input hold.
- The generated reply example uses the existing send engine, explicit canonical recipient-seat attribution where needed, the full original id and a properly shell-quoted server-validated source fleet path. In a routed case, the reply must land in the owner's original source fleet, not the agent's default current fleet. Validate paired source/destination headers before emitting a return route; invalid/unapproved provenance never becomes an executable suggestion. Keep operational paths out of browser payloads and public fixtures. Do not globally change owner routing for ordinary CLI sends or invent a new prompt path.
- Validate provenance again at rendering and JSON-read time, beyond the dashboard process lifetime: require its supported schema, sender=owner, canonical recipient/full id and a source_file whose parsed fleet root equals the validated routed origin_fleet, or the local holding root when not routed. Canonicalise legitimate aliases of the holding root consistently while refusing redirects inside its metadata/mailbox tree. Local messages do not require a nonexistent counterpart. Routed messages require matching paired headers including dashboard_chat, with supported schema and strict field types checked on both counterparts; boolean true cannot stand in for integer version 1. Support custom source fleet filenames. Missing/removed/changed source files or mismatched provenance suppress executable guidance/normalised return metadata while leaving the original body inspectable; quoting alone is not validation. Reply examples always specify the canonical agent attribution.
- Mixed batches keep each dashboard message's own full-id/source tuple, including B/C-origin requests alongside ordinary mail and a queued kickoff. Count the fully rendered guidance in BATCH_CAP before selecting the existing pointer path; pointer notification is not body delivery and leaves messages queued. Do not attach an unbounded instruction per message to a capped pointer. Subsequent printed/JSON inbox reads, hook reads and truncated-body reads retain each validated return route. Kickoff and ordinary-message bytes retain their previous semantics.
- Do not scrape or mirror provider transcripts, terminal prose or this separate native assistant conversation into the owner inbox. A true conversational response is an explicitly sent agent message, and its failure/absence remains visible rather than synthesised. Existing plain-send/FYI, identity, cross-fleet receipts, strict intents and no-automatic-retry rules continue unchanged.

### 42.4 Build and verification gates

Version 0.21.1 in both declarations. Builder implements this section, with a focused public conversation module/seam if needed; maintain Python 3.8+ and no new runtime dependencies. Preserve section 7 and the first three AGENTS rules. Update README/help where relevant, the architecture note and testing guide. Public examples remain synthetic. The designer coordinates visual work and owns native acceptance; no production fleet/config/job is a test target and no installation occurs before the release is reviewed and approved.

Regressions must first reproduce the actual missing ack/reply update, then cover local/routed pure conversation joins, exact identities/ids, receipt uncertainty, alias/participant isolation, bounded/truncated reads, body escaping, wrong roots/headers/auth/schema with zero effects, absent seat history, duplicate mirrors and new/cur races. Prove return metadata survives all delivery/read forms, ordinary messages remain compatible, source-fleet reply guidance is quoted and correct, and no extra key/prompt/lifecycle path appears. The shipped-script frontend tests must cover initial/late replies and ack without manual actions, one polling lifetime, target/auth races, multiple consecutive normal sends, fast/pending duplicate clicks, editing/switching/out-of-order responses, uncertain retries and scroll/focus/draft preservation. Demo must never reach live actions.

Run focused then complete suites below/above Python 3.11, actual-script Node regressions, compile/grammar/diff/public audits and independent final review. Browser acceptance on a fake service/Herdr must start with one Send and end with acknowledgement and reply visibly in the same selected conversation, without an Owner mailbox/Refresh click; exercise mobile, switching, errors and retained drafts. The designer then runs a cheap Claude scratch seat with only the reply tool needed: send from the candidate dashboard, have the agent itself return a synthetic answer using the generated guidance, and observe it in the source conversation without manual mailbox steps. Exercise a different registered source and target fleet. No designer-authored substitute response counts as that end-to-end check. Record actual results/limits in 42.5 before local commit. Push/install still requires a new explicit owner instruction for this release.

### 42.5 Verification

The designer exercised the candidate's authenticated HTTP API with two separately registered disposable fleets and one Claude Haiku seat on Claude Code 2.1.322, Herdr 0.9.5 and Python 3.9.6. A dashboard owner request from the source fleet reached the agent in the other fleet. The agent itself used the supplied return guidance to acknowledge the original full id and send a synthetic textual answer into the source owner's conversation. The original message retained its acknowledgement receipt and the returned answer retained its full reply linkage. No designer-authored substitute answer, provider-transcript scraping, production job or owner fleet was used.

The initial helper incorrectly expected the answer to remain in the new mailbox. Ordinary owner delivery had already filed it in history through the existing notification path. Recovery inspected the same real answer without resending: three authenticated conversation reads preserved every scratch file's bytes and modification time. An exact original request fingerprint matched the retained intent; replaying that identical intent returned the previous full id with no second send or file change. Invalid session/origin requests were refused, both candidate services stopped cleanly, and the created agent and workspace were removed. This was API/native-agent acceptance; integrated browser behavior is a separate gate.

The inert view's corrected browser harness reports 101 passing checks; the same regression assertions against the pinned earlier view produced 89 passes and 12 failures. Independent review verified the pins and ten logical DOM assertion groups, including uncertainty wording, generic-ack announcements, reply-link focus identity, bounded remembered keys, overlapping history prepends, literal unknown statuses, quiet catch-up and the already-following scroll rule. Those checks do not establish real screen-reader behavior or integrated controller correctness.

The final conflict-aware source passed all 1,513 tests on Python 3.9.6 in 71.489 seconds and Python 3.12.13 in 62.922 seconds, with zero failures, errors or skips. The 30 new Python cases include generated cross-fleet reply-command execution, mixed-source dashboard mail alongside ordinary mail and a queued kickoff, hook reads, exact acknowledgements without a delivery-order assumption, and selected NEW/CUR header conflicts that must remain uncertain. All 32 shipped-script controller regressions passed, including the four inactive/returned and unchanged/changed read-choice cases, independent draft/intent retention, bounded completed history, explicit Recent activity clearing, and positive versus conflicting lost-response reconciliation. The original acknowledgement/reply symptom loop fails against the released script and passes against the candidate.

Compileall, both JavaScript syntax checks and diff checking passed. The current-tree pattern audit covered 130 public files and found no selected private-content or credential markers; all 100 Python files parsed with Python 3.8 grammar, the first three repository rules and section 7 were unchanged, and the five vendored asset/licence hashes matched the manifest. Pattern checking is not proof of absence of every private detail, and grammar parsing does not substitute for a Python 3.8 runtime run.

The integrated fake-live browser pass completed 106 checks with zero failures on the final controller and conflict-aware reader; before/after runtime hashes were identical. It used the actual Live server, page, controller, message engine and generated CLI guidance, with synthetic fleets, fake Herdr and fake agent tool replies. The headless browser was the installed Google Chrome 157.0.8103.38 binary. The designer inspected the final desktop conversation and mobile screenshots. One ordinary Send produced Sending, a recorded delivery, an acknowledgement on the original and a linked answer in that same panel, without a mailbox, manual refresh or reload. Local and routed lost-response cases first demonstrated an uncertain retained attempt after one guarded fake-agent prompt and one send record, then reconciled automatically to one confirmed row with no second send. The pass also covered independent plain/uncertain drafts and newer reply text, the read-choice matrix, bounded history, Recent recall and activity clearing without inbox effects, queued delivery, errors, quiet hidden-page return, Demo isolation and 1440/1024/375 layouts with collapse/expansion.

The ambiguous selected NEW/CUR identity case was exercised by actual-file reader regressions and independent controller probes, not constructed in the browser pass. Hidden-page behavior was simulated; no real phone, screen reader, other browser or live Watch stream was exercised in this correction. Existing terminal Watch and plain/FYI debt semantics were retained. The unbound historical question remains excluded and the collection warning remains visible, while independently validated rows keep their receipt certainty. Independent final whole-diff review returned MERGE with no remaining actionable findings after checking the final runtime pins, browser evidence, complete-suite results and this verification amendment. Publication still requires the owner's separate explicit instruction.

## 43. Chat composer keyboard send (0.21.2)

The owner requests Command+Enter to send from the dashboard chat widget. The existing composer contains a hidden Ctrl/Command+Enter hint but has no matching handler. Implement the shortcut through the existing Send button so it shares the current delivery, intent, pending, uncertain-result and Demo behavior.

### 43.1 Interaction

Listen on the message textarea only. Command+Enter or Ctrl+Enter, without Alt or Shift, prevents the textarea's newline and activates the existing Send button once when enabled. Ignore auto-repeat, composition events and the legacy composition key code 229. Ordinary Enter and Shift+Enter retain multiline editing; shortcuts outside this textarea do nothing. A disabled Send button cannot be activated through the shortcut. No browser keyboard event is forwarded to a terminal or agent.

Reveal the existing shortcut hint beside Send and describe the shortcut through `aria-keyshortcuts` on the Send button. Keep the hint accurate in Demo, whose existing Send action only records a sample reply. Update the user documentation. Version 0.21.2 in both declarations; no backend, dependency or native input-path change is needed.

### 43.2 Verification

Execute the actual shipped script in the existing frontend fixture. Verify Command and Control sends, one request under rapid repeat/pending input, disabled/empty/composing rejection, textarea scope and unchanged plain/Shift+Enter behavior. Demonstrate the positive shortcut case fails against the previous script. Run the existing frontend regressions, the required Python suites below/above 3.11, syntax/compile and diff checks, and independent review. The designer verifies the keyboard action in a browser against disposable fake fleets and performs the established cheap-Claude scratch acceptance before committing. Record actual results and limitations here. Publication requires a new explicit owner push instruction for this release.

### 43.3 Verification results

The shipped-script controller suite increased from 32 to 37 passing tests. Both independent Command and Control positive cases failed against the previous script with zero requests instead of one; the candidate passes both. The other cases cover modifier/composition/repeat exclusions, disabled and empty sends, textarea scope, visible platform wording, shortcut accessibility metadata and Demo's existing local-only reply. Independent review reproduced the two failures against the previous script and all 37 passes against the candidate. Guard cases are positive controls rather than individually mutation-tested assertions.

Both complete Python suites passed all 1,513 tests with no failures, errors or skips: Python 3.9.6 in 66.052 seconds and Python 3.12.13 in 69.216 seconds. Compilation, JavaScript syntax and diff checks passed. The public audit covered 130 files with zero selected private/credential-pattern findings; 100 Python files parsed with Python 3.8 grammar, the first three repository rules and section 7 were unchanged, and all five vendor hashes matched. Grammar parsing is not an actual Python 3.8 runtime run, and pattern checking is not proof that every possible private detail is absent.

The designer's Google Chrome 157.0.8103.38 check passed 13 assertions against the real candidate Live server and synthetic fleets with fake Herdr. Browser keyboard events for Command+Enter and Ctrl+Enter each produced one delivered message through the existing action endpoint. Plain Enter and Shift+Enter inserted newlines without sending. Extra modifiers, auto-repeat, synthetic composition events, outside-composer focus and an empty draft produced no send; rapid presses while pending produced one guarded prompt. The hint and accessibility metadata were present, no native agent keys were sent, no browser exceptions or warnings occurred, and the candidate service and browser stopped. This used Chrome on macOS with injected Control events, not a physical Windows/Linux keyboard, real input-method composition or other browser.

The designer separately ran one real cheap Claude Haiku seat in two disposable fleets. The candidate API sent a synthetic owner request across fleets, and the agent itself acknowledged it and returned an explicitly linked answer to the source conversation. Repeated reads preserved scratch file bytes and modification times; replaying the same intent returned its existing id without another send. The server stopped and the created agent/workspace were removed. This native check exercised the existing API/delivery path; the keyboard action itself was the separate browser check. No owner fleet or production job was used as a test.

Initial browser attempts produced no acceptance evidence because of private harness setup, sandboxed Chrome startup and timeout issues. After correcting the helper, stopping its exact stalled runner/server processes and using the approved browser run outside the sandbox, the completed pass above supplied the browser evidence. These issues required no product change. Independent final review returned MERGE for all seven changed paths and this verification amendment. No publication is authorised by this verification record.

## 44. Decision Desk and daily dashboard use (0.22.0)

The owner authorised the next dashboard milestone: a Decision Desk that brings observed needs for attention together, followed by separately reviewed human terminal control, plus clearer update/reconnect guidance and useful view preferences. Implement this section within the existing safety rules. Section 45 records the separate human-control proposal and its authority/protocol gates; this section creates no terminal-input, native approval or lifecycle action.

### 44.1 Attention means observed evidence

Add a bounded pure attention projection to the existing dashboard observation, rather than a task classifier or second delivery engine. It has five types:

- **Owner message:** the union of existing non-FYI, non-expired pending owner mail and existing unsettled requests addressed to owner, including delivered CUR history. Use the existing `messages.unsettled` semantics without replacing them with `owed`'s latest-per-pair summary. Join exact full ids to validated same-read NEW/CUR headers, keeping unread, delivered/awaiting reply and incomplete/unavailable evidence distinct. A request lacking safe header evidence stays a metadata-only unavailable item, never an executable reply route. For a valid routed owner message with both registered roots, show one item at the authoritative destination owner mailbox, using that root for Inspect/Reply; suppress its origin history-only mirror only after paired-header validation. Invalid, unpaired or outside-roster evidence remains unavailable metadata. Ordinary local `send owner --fleet SOURCE_FILE` keeps that selected owner mailbox as its source; no new owner routing is introduced. Use its canonical source fleet and recorded sender. A message is not automatically classified as a question by its prose. Inspect and Reply retain the existing selected-read/send rules; opening the item does not mark it read or acknowledge it. A genuine reply/ack settles only what the existing engine says it settles; reading alone must not remove an awaiting-reply item. An acknowledged still-NEW file may remain explicitly unread without being labelled awaiting reply. Queue TTL does not retroactively expire already delivered unsettled CUR history. Truncated logs cannot establish certain unresolved debt; mark that evidence incomplete. Strip log `first_line` and every other prose field from attention output.
- **Native dialog:** a registered live seat actually observed as blocked. Say that it needs attention in Herdr; offer explicit read-only Watch of that exact current seat, never guessed provider Approve/Deny buttons. No extra pane/agent screen read is performed merely to populate attention.
- **Send issue:** an explicitly recorded owner-origin send/reply outcome that is unconfirmed, refused, expired or dropped. Read only bounded message/journal metadata. Distinguish an unresolved outcome from an ordinary Queued request. Do not infer refusal from terminal prose, queue age or agent state. Reuse exact full-id and source/destination/participant validation; a newer authoritative positive outcome for that same intent/id supersedes an older uncertain one. If evidence is conflicting or cannot be paired, retain uncertainty rather than inventing a resolution. Historic negative outcomes are labelled recorded outcomes, not presumed unfinished tasks. Inspect opens the correctly scoped receipts/conversation; it never retries or reconstructs a missing body.
- **Provider hold:** the existing pure limit rules report an active hold for the live kind at the observation time. Expired, released, invalid or different-kind records do not become active holds. Show recorded/reset timing when valid; do not offer automatic failover, release or interruption.
- **Observation issue:** a fleet or service observation is stale/unavailable, or an expected watcher's liveness is confirmed stopped or unknown. A fleet with `watch = false` does not need attention merely because its intentionally disabled watcher has no live PID; expose that enabled/disabled expectation as metadata. Keep the last observation with its real timestamp, distinguish stale from stopped and permission-denied liveness from missing PID, and offer an explicit refresh/inspection action only. Do not make a failed registry/version probe a fleet outage.

Build stable bounded item ids from the type and exact server-owned fleet/seat/message/intent identity. Cross-fleet mirror copies of the same validated send issue are one item; unrelated same-short-id records stay separate. Preserve distinct causes on the same seat. Expose only opaque registered fleet ids, canonical seat names, message/intent ids, finite status/reason labels, timestamps and supported action descriptors. No paths, commands, credentials, message bodies, terminal prose or provider transcript content enter this summary. Never classify message bodies or automatically read them.

The attention summary is recomputed from current observations and existing records; it creates no attention/debt/task files and is not another source of truth. File reads use existing bounded caches and the existing metadata-only strict journal parser, without reserving intents, creating directories/locks or changing mail. Isolate corrupt/oversized journal/log inputs and mark that source incomplete while retaining healthy fleet observations and independently valid items. Use a finite per-fleet candidate bound and a 200-item response cap, with category counts, visible truncation/incomplete indicators and deterministic ordering: owner messages, native dialogs, send issues, provider holds, observation issues; within a category valid oldest time first, then stable id. Missing time remains unknown. Apply the requested registered-fleet scope/category filter before the response cap so that many owner messages cannot hide all blocked seats when Native dialog is selected. Category `counts` describe bounded candidates matching the requested fleet scope before category filtering; `matched_count` describes candidates after category filtering and before the response cap. Thus selecting one category does not falsely turn the other category badges into zero. Candidate truncation marks counts as capped/incomplete. Never imply that a truncated observation proves zero issues. A narrow authenticated read endpoint may serve filtered metadata from the shared observation; it creates no additional native poller or body read.

### 44.2 One workspace, with a clear decision surface

Extend the accepted Signal Box design, retaining the desktop 25% fleet selector / 75% selected-fleet workspace, collapsible rail, expandable terminal, adjacent conversation and responsive reflow. Do not add a feature-navigation rail. Use the current palette/type/motion system and finite state lamps. The distinctive surface is a compact **Needs attention** desk above the selected workspace, with an All fleets / Selected fleet scope and category filters. Keep the fleet roster, selected source/target, terminal, conversation and drafts accessible. Empty attention means no observed matching items, not that all work is accepted or complete.

Each item identifies its fleet, seat/sender, observed reason, age/freshness and one explicit supported action. Show actual recorded evidence and statuses rather than inferred urgency/progress. A selected item can expose a detail area without fetching bodies for other items. The existing Owner message detail/read/reply behavior is reused. An absent or ambiguous sender retains inspection but cannot be mapped to an arbitrary live seat. A failed cross-fleet attempt opens the recorded source/target conversation, preserving independent drafts/uncertain intents and original reply links. A native item selects only its exact registered fleet/seat and starts passive Watch only after the explicit Watch action. Switching attention scope/filter itself does not change the selected fleet/seat, fetch bodies, prompt, acknowledge, deliver or take control.

Retain stable item DOM/focus identity under polling; disappearing items return focus to a labelled desk heading/filter, not a different item's action. New observations never steal focus or scroll the page while the owner types/reads. Only explicit detail/Watch actions may move focus to their selected detail. Deduplicate live announcements, respect quiet hidden-tab catch-up and reduced motion, and do not replay history as new urgency. All controls work with keyboard; badges and state have text alternatives. On narrow widths the desk, terminal and composer remain usable without page overflow; terminal expansion and rail collapse preserve access to attention and Release remains a future section-45 requirement, not a fake active control.

Demo uses plainly synthetic examples of all attention types and the same filtering/navigation, with no live file/Herdr/action path. Do not simulate Take control as though it were available in Live.

### 44.3 Running service versus installed package

Capture this foreground service's running build identity once using the existing immutable build identity helper. At most once per 60 seconds, read the enabled exact-id `yarrasys.fleet` registration through a bounded Herdr plugin-list read and validate its version/resolved commit. No fetching, installing, executing installed code, reading arbitrary source paths or automatic restarts occur. A missing/failed/ambiguous registry read means installed identity unknown; it does not alter healthy fleet observations or discard the running identity. Demo performs no native registry call.

Authenticated snapshot metadata exposes only validated bounded running/installed version and commit plus a current/different/unknown comparison and last check time. A difference must say **Running a different build** or **Restart to load the installed version**, not claim that an older installed build is newer. Reuse existing comparison rules without version-string ordering guesses. Preserve the launch's original registration mode in bounded restart guidance: stop the foreground dashboard with Ctrl-C, rerun its original launch command with `--open`, and use the fresh tab. Show `herdr-fleet dashboard --all-fleets --open` only when this service was launched with `--all-fleets`; a narrow/explicit-fleet launch instead says rerun your original dashboard command without reconstructing private paths. Do not put private fleet paths or capability URLs in payloads, copy buttons or persistent browser storage. Do not restart the owner's server, clear drafts or claim that page Refresh replaces its running package.

### 44.4 Safe view preferences

Persist a small versioned allowlist of cosmetic settings in localStorage for this origin: fleet rail collapsed, motion explicitly chosen, terminal height within its existing bounds, attention scope and category filter. Do not persist the active Terminal/Fleet log/Messages tab: a new page starts on Terminal, and body-bearing views require a fresh explicit selection. Terminal expansion is transient and does not restore an input/controller session. A manual Reset view preferences control removes only this plugin's own preference key and restores cosmetic defaults, without changing the active tab/Watch, authority, drafts, messages, other sites/keys or running agents.

Never persist capability/session/CSRF values, fleet/seat/pane/session ids, source paths, message bodies, terminal output, question/reply ids, drafts or retained send intents. Existing drafts/intents remain in memory; this section does not promise draft survival across a process or page restart. Validate the full stored schema/types/enums/ranges and cap input/output at 2 KiB. Missing, malformed, oversized, unavailable or throwing storage falls back to defaults without breaking startup, authentication, polling or typing. Live and Demo cosmetic preferences use distinct keys. A saved motion choice respects the owner's explicit selection; otherwise reduced-motion remains the default. Settings load only as cosmetics and cannot start Watch, fetch a mailbox, replay Demo, send, acknowledge or enable control. Do not claim preferences survive a randomly changed loopback origin/port; explain origin-scoped persistence and fixed-port use where appropriate.

### 44.5 Implementation and gates

Set both package declarations to 0.22.0. Builder owns service/data/controller/tests and shared runtime integration. Designer coordinates Fable's private visual work and native/browser acceptance; no moving shared-source ownership. Prefer pure projection modules and reuse existing narrow adapters. Preserve Python 3.8+, no new runtime dependency, the first three repository rules and all section-7 invariants. Update README, architecture/testing notes and fake safety oracle for the new read-only plugin registry call only. This is not authority to weaken any existing input guard.

Add real before/after tests for missing cross-fleet attention and stale-build indication, pure GET zero-effect snapshots, metadata-only joins/isolation/deduplication/supersession, bounds/corruption/active-limit timing, registry failure/cache/wrong-id cases, stable item focus/actions and scope, safe storage schema/throw/reset/secret absence, retained drafts and the existing shortcut/intent/auth/Watch/Demo protections. Run relevant focused checks, complete suites below/above Python 3.11, actual-script frontend regressions, compile/syntax/3.8 grammar/diff/public/vendor audits and independent final review. Browser checks cover desktop/narrow layout, keyboard filtering/detail/Watch, update states, quiet catch-up and retained drafts; distinguish injected Control events from physical Windows/Linux coverage. The designer performs cheap-Claude scratch acceptance; no owner fleet/job is a test target. A native quota prevented a new agent answer during this build. For this observation-only feature, independent review accepted an adjusted native gate: actual Herdr-reported scratch seat state, exact registered attention projection, explicitly selected passive ANSI Watch, zero-write API/filter checks and owned-resource cleanup. The attempted genuine answer is a prevented/failed check, never a pass. Browser tests must separately exercise the actual candidate mailbox/receipt engine with fake agents, including CUR requests and exact settlement. This does not replace section 42's original genuine reply evidence or relax section 45's input gates. No quota retry or billing change is authorised by this adjustment. Record actual evidence and limits in 44.6 before commit. Publishing requires the owner's separate explicit push instruction.

### 44.6 Verification

The complete suites increased from 1,513 to 1,542 tests with 29 new Python cases. All 1,542 passed on Python 3.9.6 in 109.803 seconds and Python 3.12.13 in 96.223 seconds, serially, with no failures, errors or skips. The shipped controller/inert-view suite increased from 37 to 48 passing tests; independent review reran the 29 focused Python and all 48 Node cases. Before implementation, the released observer produced zero attention rows for the three eligible fixture messages, and both new controller positives failed against the released script. Further red-before-fix cases covered canonical owner mirror duplication, mismatched filename/header identity, phantom uncertainty from unrelated corruption, malformed/capped metadata and intentionally disabled watchers. Other negative controls were exercised green, not individually mutation-tested.

The designer completed 21 browser checks on Google Chrome 157.0.8103.39 and the same 21 on Firefox 159.0.1, each against its own disposable synthetic fleets, the actual candidate Live server, mailbox/receipt engine and CLI, with fake Herdr/agents and a fake passive ANSI stream. Both passes covered two delivered CUR owner requests from one sender, metadata without bodies, explicit inspection, Read preserving an awaiting reply, exact-id acknowledgement settling only its request, filtering a native dialog after more than 200 owner messages, exact-target passive Watch with no prompt/key, reset retaining Watch and draft, both Control/Command shortcuts, cosmetic-only storage and fresh-document restoration without content tabs or Watch, narrow launch restart guidance, 375-pixel page fit and no browser exceptions/warnings. The designer inspected desktop and narrow screenshots. All 11 captured runtime hashes still matched after acceptance.

Early browser attempts stopped after nine passing checks because the private synthetic owner CLI was missing explicit owner attribution while retaining the fake Herdr binding. The helper was corrected and independently checked before the completed passes. Full message ids are bare; the earlier private missing-prefix diagnosis was incorrect. These partial runs are not completed acceptance and required no product change. The Firefox protocol adapter had a separate blank-page keyboard mechanics check; it is not counted as candidate coverage. Both browsers ran on macOS with injected keyboard events, not physical Windows/Linux keyboards, a real input method, real phone or assistive-technology testing. Existing controller regressions cover stale auth/selection/filter generations, quiet catch-up and stable view focus; those do not imply that every such race was separately reproduced in these browser passes.

For native acceptance, the designer created one cheap Claude Haiku scratch seat in two disposable fleets on Herdr 0.9.5. One synthetic owner request was delivered, but the actual agent entered its weekly quota dialog and could not return an answer. That genuine-answer attempt failed/prevented its answer check; no reply, quota retry, dialog answer or billing change is claimed. Under the adjusted observation-only gate in 44.5, the final candidate then reported that exact Herdr-observed blocked seat as a Native dialog item with the exact current registered Watch view. Three reads retained its stable id; attention reads and the unavailable control endpoint preserved scratch file bytes/mtime. Explicit passive Watch returned a real full 80-by-24 ANSI frame, preserved the native agent identity and left its dialog blocked; the candidate and observer stopped. This supplied 13 assertions over 11 distinct checks, separately from the browser results. After validating its recorded created workspace and single original pane, the designer closed only that scratch workspace and verified the agent absent, without a quit prompt or dialog answer. No owner fleet/job was used as a test target.

Compilation, three JavaScript syntax checks and diff checking passed. The current public audit covered 134 candidate files with zero selected private/credential-pattern findings; all 103 Python files/launcher parsed with Python 3.8 grammar, the first three repository rules and section 7 remained unchanged, and all five vendor hashes matched. Grammar parsing is not a real Python 3.8 runtime run; pattern checks cannot prove the absence of every possible private detail. Independent review approved the complete source and final evidence text (MERGE). No publication is authorised by this verification record.

## 45. Explicit human terminal control (0.23.0) — implementation design

The owner explicitly approved the narrow human-only rule exception after reviewing this concrete Take control / interact / Release proposal, including personally answering native dialogs. After the source-level limitation in 45.1 was verified, the owner separately approved the terminal-level alternative in 45.2, including its shell/resize semantics and lack of atomic agent binding. These approvals authorise the required named rule amendment when implementing this feature; they do not authorise agent-generated answers or input replay. Installed Herdr help and its current persistence documentation expose `terminal session control TARGET`, newline-delimited input/frame commands and one controller; availability is not evidence that the plugin can safely bind or revoke an agent-generation-specific lease. Until the remaining coordination/implementation gates are met and the named amendments are made, existing runtime prohibitions remain enforced and no controller/input path is implemented.

The approved exception permits only the authenticated owner, after an explicit Take control action, to type/paste into the exact selected native terminal and personally answer its native approval dialogs. Agents, watchers, Decision Desk actions and automated suggestions must never choose, submit or replay those answers. Passive Watch and ordinary owner-message delivery retain their existing guards. Implementation names section 45 in AGENTS bullet 2, section 7's input/blocked/typing invariants and section 41's Watch-only exclusions. The exception grants no agent permission to operate a real native dialog, including during acceptance testing.

The control adapter stays behind herdr.py, with no raw argv/pane-run/send-text API. A same-origin authenticated POST validates exact Host/Origin/cookie/independent CSRF and current registered seat/native identity, then creates a short-lived in-memory lease bound to that tab/session/terminal and observed agent identity. No silent takeover. One controller per terminal across dashboard instances and coordination with cooperative plugin delivery/lifecycle operations are required; queued messages stay queued while the owner controls that terminal. Acquire serializes with active cooperative plugin operations. This is a submission boundary, not cancellation of work already accepted by Herdr, an agent turn already running, or input already in its terminal queue. Merely disabling browser Send or checking whether the prompt is empty does not close the empty-input race.

Only deliberate owner input while that lease is active is forwarded, in bounded sequenced frames; duplicate, stale and uncertain input is never replayed. Terminal output, device replies, focus/mouse reporting, automatic clipboard integration and page scripts must not produce writes. `isTrusted` can filter accidental events but is not server authentication or proof of a human. Release, auth/target/native-generation changes, disconnect/page switch, lease expiry and shutdown revoke/reap only owned controllers, discard buffered input and do not submit/clear/ack/flush anything. The exact target and Release remain visible. Only bounded session metadata may be recorded, never typed text/paste/terminal output or secrets.

Sections 45.1–45.2 resolve the native targeting question with the separately approved terminal-level scope. Sections 45.3–45.8 define the remaining implementation gates. Native one-controller exclusivity alone does not block `agent prompt`, hooks, restart or external native/third-party input. The plugin gate coordinates its participating versions and correctly attributed delivery/consumption paths; native clients, old plugin versions, arbitrary tools and unrelated writers remain outside it. The feature must not claim global exclusion, agent-bound input or cancellation of accepted upstream work. Extend the fake safety oracle only for the named human lease and preserve all automated blocked/key-path failures. Real native approval answers remain owner testing, never agent automation.

### 45.1 Verified upstream limitation

Independent source inspection of Herdr 0.9.5, tagged commit `2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01`, found that controller input contains text or bytes, not an expected agent/session generation. `TerminalAttach` retains a terminal id, and the server applies input to that terminal's runtime without a per-input agent identity check. Detach, disconnect, takeover, actual pane removal and handoff have guards, but they do not establish revocation when only the agent exits to its surviving terminal shell. Acquiring control also owns native terminal sizing and can resume a restored pending agent. An agent-bound guarantee therefore cannot be implemented by adding a client-side poll. This is source-level evidence, not a live unsafe-input reproduction. Primary implementation: [input protocol](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/client/terminal_sessions.rs#L165), [terminal ownership](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/server/clients.rs#L15), [input application](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/server/headless.rs#L2084).

### 45.2 Approved terminal-level alternative — implementation design follows

The feasible alternative deliberately controls the selected **terminal**, with the same terminal-level ownership semantics as native Herdr attach. It must not claim to bind every accepted byte to an agent generation. The Take control confirmation names the currently selected registered agent and terminal, explains that the terminal can become a shell if that agent exits, and explains that Control uses real terminal sizing. A fresh target check is still required before acquisition, and a subsequently observed identity change ends control promptly and discards unsent input; that is a best-effort additional guard, not atomic revocation of bytes already accepted upstream. No hidden takeover or automatic reconnect is permitted.

The owner gave the separate explicit approval acknowledging these terminal-level semantics, and subsequently approved the disclosed native restored-session effect in 45.9. All other human authority, authentication, lease sequencing, non-replay, visible Release, no output-induced writes, metadata-only recording and plugin cross-process/in-flight arbitration requirements remain binding. Controller acquisition refuses a target already observed missing, without a runtime, or pending restore rather than deliberately using attach as a substitute for a separate start/resume command. Native replacement/restore between the final check and attach may still start the saved agent; this unavoidable effect is now expressly confirmed by the owner, not represented as prevented by a live PID, `launch_pending=false`, a first frame or another poll. The earlier no-hidden-resume prerequisite is replaced only by that explicit 45.9 disclosure/confirmation. The feature must coordinate all participating plugin-generated prompt/quit/restart/interrupt paths; native Herdr clients and unrelated third-party writers are outside that plugin mutex and must not be represented as globally excluded.

### 45.3 Cross-process permits and endpoint identity

Use one small coordination module, independent of HTTP and fleet discovery, with opaque domain, automation-permit and control-permit objects. A permit is scoped to its creating process/thread and a complete semantic operation. Bind to the actual creator Thread object identity plus PID, not a recycled numeric thread identifier. A nested call reuses a covering permit explicitly; a different thread cannot borrow it and an inherited descriptor alone cannot mint input authority. The native client asserts permit coverage before every participating mutation. Generic `run`/`text` continue to reject all terminal input commands. No browser-supplied path, argv, lock file, descriptor or JSON object can stand in for a permit.

The central native-command classifier is closed: recognise exact supported argv shapes as observation/discovery, explicitly exempt nonparticipating effects, or participating mutations with exact permit coverage. Reject every unknown/malformed command before runner invocation, even with a broad permit; an unrecognised prefix is not a read. Validate supported option positions/arity and reject global dispatch/session/indirect `api call`/`command invoke` escapes. Preserve fixed discovery forms (`--version`, agent usage, `agent start --help`, integration status and plugin config-dir); `notification show` is an explicit exempt effect rather than an observation. Table-driven run/text tests prove zero calls for unclassified/alternate-dispatch shapes and missing/insufficient mutation permits. Fixed status/registry/observe seams have their own narrow validation, not a generic alternate runner.

Resolve the same canonical API endpoint in every invocation, including CLI, hooks and dashboards, without a new native lookup for explicit file-only inbox operations. Follow the pinned Herdr environment/default/session rules; resolve filesystem aliases before hashing the endpoint. Freeze that endpoint into each native child's environment so execution cannot silently select another server. Treat an ambiguous legacy client-socket override as unavailable for Control. Do not select a new namespace from socket inode, server PID, cwd, repo, dashboard port, TMPDIR or an unverified instance token. A stable endpoint namespace survives server restart; a surviving holder conservatively continues to exclude. Unsupported/ambiguous resolution is a finite refusal, never an ungated fallback. A bounded read-only server-status check may corroborate the resolved endpoint during Control acquisition but does not replace the pure hook resolver.

The pure resolver follows pinned Herdr 0.9.5 source: an explicitly present API override wins environment session selection; reject empty/unusable/nonabsolute overrides rather than guess. Without it, XDG_CONFIG_HOME is selected by presence, otherwise HOME/.config; initial support requires a nonempty absolute base. Do not use Python account-home expansion, Rust's missing-HOME temporary fallback, literal-tilde expansion or cwd-relative retargeting. Session is absent/`default` for the base directory, otherwise 1–64 ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` characters excluding `.` and `..`; invalid values are unavailable, not silently defaulted. API override shadows even an invalid environment session as native does. Release and debug native builds use `herdr` and `herdr-dev` respectively, then optional `sessions/NAME`, then `herdr.sock`; filename/version does not establish build flavour. A file-only consumer with no override conservatively acquires shared N in **both** candidate endpoint domains, deduplicated and globally ordered by domain then resource order. Retain a candidate whose socket/config directory is absent, canonicalizing existing ancestors without creating config directories. This can hold a same-named hook when an unrelated other-profile target is controlled; document that conservative availability cost. An explicit override has one domain.

For a native producer or future Control, the selected binary's bounded `herdr status server --json` under its original immutable environment corroborates its actual canonical API path against those candidates; reject remote labels, malformed/ambiguous/nonpath output or an unrelated endpoint. Do not probe candidates and choose the first reachable: both profiles may run. Parse the actual top-level socket/running status shape, then freeze the matching absolute API endpoint into native subprocess environments. Endpoint corroboration is independent of liveness: an ordinary producer can select a valid `not_running`/`running:false` endpoint and preserve its usual native absent-server outcome; a future Control additionally requires positively running/runtime evidence. Exit 0 alone establishes neither. Herdr 0.9.4 has the same relevant status/resolver source, so this dependency does not raise the minimum supported version; fixtures include its flat running/not-running/malformed/remote shapes. Pure explicit-name consumption does not run that command. No injected CLI `--session`/session-attach arguments can override the frozen endpoint. Before freezing, derive the client path from the ORIGINAL API spelling with native file-stem semantics, canonicalize it and require equality with the client derived from the canonical API spelling; a renamed socket symlink can otherwise change servers. A legacy client-only override without API override must equal the normal derived client endpoint or make Control unavailable; an API override shadows it as native does, and the frozen child environment removes the shadowed legacy value. Preserve these checks for observation/control targeting; never silently clear a mismatched override and retarget. This resolves a native endpoint, not an atomic server/agent generation. Primary rules: [configuration roots](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/config/io.rs#L22), [session precedence](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/session.rs#L31), [client socket derivation](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/server/socket_paths.rs#L23), [status shape](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/cli/status.rs#L262), [0.9.4 availability](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/7e26bad94044eba205033111a0ce8033ede7e24f/src/cli/status.rs#L33).

The concrete POSIX lock root is `/tmp/herdr-fleet-control-<effective-uid>` after canonicalizing the OS `/tmp` parent, with endpoint-hashed subdirectories. It is independent of caller HOME/XDG/TMPDIR/cwd/fleet/port and has no production per-process override. Use a checked OS-owned sticky temporary parent and a user-owned mode-0700 root; an existing foreign/symlink/insecure root is unavailable rather than repaired or followed. Test injection substitutes an isolated root only through an internal constructor, never environment/CLI/browser input. Validate directory/file ownership, private permissions, regular single-link mode-0600 lock files and no symlink traversal using descriptor-relative operations where required. Creation is allowed only for real participating mutations/acquisition, never pure GET/show/history/count/dry-run. Keep inode identity stable: no unlink, replacement, truncate/recreate or automatic lock garbage collection. Separate human-readable metadata from authoritative lock inodes; metadata contains no input, output, authentication secret or raw browser-visible endpoint path. Unavailable storage refuses the affected mutation/control; file enqueue remains available. Lock inodes deliberately accumulate with target churn; document this disk/inode cost and permit only manual offline cleanup when no participating process remains. Check descriptor/operation-size budgets before effects; an allocation/EMFILE failure closes the complete attempt and refuses.

Client-pair validation above is specifically eligibility for client-protocol Watch/Control. Separate API endpoint selection/freezing from that client eligibility: a valid native JSON API-only producer still uses its corroborated canonical API endpoint when a legacy client-only override or renamed client alias differs. Such a mismatch must not break ordinary API delivery/lifecycle commands or be described as required by Herdr 0.9.4. Record the original pair's eligibility on the domain and explicitly require it at the Watch/Control stream seam; freezing an API-only environment must not erase that original evidence and accidentally make a later stream eligible. File-only consumers remain pure API-domain name locks with no client connection.

Descriptor/growth limits belong to the original semantic operation cumulatively, including every domain, derived P/T and newly created W. Reserve a finite authorised growth plan before its first effect, then charge every derivation against that remaining plan and the 512-descriptor operation cap. Repeated nested acquisitions cannot reset either budget. An excess derivation refuses before opening more lock files or altering existing coverage, retaining the parent's current locks; add deterministic multi-derivation and refusal-preserves-coverage tests.

Resources are separate canonical **N:name**, **P:pane** and **T:terminal** keys, not a composite name/pane file. Human control takes exclusive locks on its exact N/P/T; ordinary correctly attributed producers take shared locks on their covered resources. File-only agent mailbox consumption takes shared N without adding native caller lookups. A workspace topology resource **W:workspace** uses the reverse modes: Control holds shared W; any plugin split/move/resize/close or strip operation affecting that workspace holds exclusive W. Cross-workspace operations cover both W resources. This holds geometry changes for the affected workspace while retaining ordinary delivery to other seats and other fleets; it does not pause all automation on the server.

Acquire the whole required set nonblocking in a fixed order: sorted W, sorted N, sorted P, sorted T. Failure releases the complete attempt and returns Held/Busy/Unavailable. No lock upgrade or waiting under mailbox/state/timer/root locks. Planning reads do not authorize effects: after acquisition, re-read the live name/pane/terminal/workspace mapping and compare; changed or ambiguous mapping releases/refuses before effects. Starts reserve the intended N and actual destination P even while no agent exists. A covering exclusive W permits only its owned creation/move phase to derive fresh P/T coverage for newly returned native identifiers, while retaining the already reserved N and original coverage; central assertions verify the derivation belongs to the same operation, workspace and fresh native response. A freshly created workspace is exclusively owned by its creating operation until W is acquired before any target is published or further effects run. If those conditions cannot be established, stop before the next effect and replan without publishing a Control-eligible half-created target. Other nested resource growth must be planned by the outer operation or cause a clean all-or-release replan before effects. Existing timer-before-mailbox ordering remains unchanged. Control holds no fleet mailbox/state lock for its lifetime.

### 45.4 Operation coverage and lifetime

The builder must inventory every native mutation entry point and every automatic inbox-body production path. Coverage belongs to complete semantic intervals, with central native-client assertions as a second guard:

- **Delivery:** enqueue/reservation stays available. The prompt interval includes fresh target/presence checks, batch composition, submission and receipt/settlement decision. A Control hold keeps messages/kickoff queued and reports `owner control`; `--now` does not bypass it. Acquire outside the mailbox lock, or use a strictly nonblocking attempt that unwinds without reading/settling/printing a held batch. Never wait for Control under a mailbox lock.
- **Lifecycle:** restart/swap/fallback/down retain the same covering permit from final eligibility checks through quit, its permitted confirmation Enter, completion waits after that effect, all start retries, close and immediate kickoff. A user-requested preliminary `--wait` occurs before acquisition, followed by fresh eligibility/mapping checks under the permit; waiting for a working agent must not exclude its human's Control access. Multi-seat/topology actions plan all required resources before their first effect. Refuse an affected topology operation as a whole when its workspace is held; never partially retile a controlled layout.
- **Interrupt:** hold across presence/screen checks, the entire key sequence, settled-state read and permitted Ctrl-U cleanup. Per-key acquire/release is insufficient.
- **Bring-up and layout:** cover start/adoption/rename, affected neighbour geometry, both ends of moves, workspace close, strip creation/removal and handover helper placement. A helper waiting for a turn holds no delivery permit; its later lifecycle operation uses the ordinary gates. Label/focus/zoom paths cannot resize/rebind a controlled terminal behind the lease.
- **Agent inbox and hooks:** hold N across actual read/NEW-to-CUR settlement and body/pointer/hint production. A held agent consume returns a finite hold without content or settlement; hooks stay silent. `--wait` polls without retaining a permit and acquires only for a batch, revalidating under its mailbox lock. Owner mailbox operations remain distinct. Pure inspection/history/count stays file-only and does not create gate files. Explicit `--as` retains existing native-call restrictions. Arbitrary tools/output falsely attributed to another name are outside this cooperative delivery contract.
- **Timers and observation:** timer planning/enqueue/expiry and passive observation continue unchanged. Only eventual prompt delivery uses the gate; no new timer/mailbox lock inversion or automatic native action is introduced.

The existing cross-fleet reservation path also needs a lock-order repair: sorted source/destination directory locks followed by a blocking destination mailbox can deadlock a queued-kickoff delivery that already owns that mailbox and needs the destination directory for `mark_kicked_off`. For a real routed send, use one bounded acquisition helper that attempts all sorted roots and the destination mailbox nonblocking. On contention release **every** root/mailbox descriptor before backoff and fresh validation; the entire acquisition deadline is five seconds, including root contention, with no automation permit retained across backoff. Enqueue precedes native prompt permits; a caller that cannot separate these intervals returns finite Busy instead of sleeping while holding its operation permit. Allocate/reserve IDs, paired log order and message/log writes only after the complete set is acquired and paths/configuration revalidated. Retry only typed acquisition contention before message effects, never permission/path errors, partial destination writes or authoritative-success recovery. Exhaustion reports destination busy with no source pending fallback or automatic resend.

A real send may first create validated empty destination mailbox infrastructure and its stable lock inode; absent parents are not perpetual contention. Revalidate prepared infrastructure under the final set. Keep existing pure/dry-run behavior and the watcher's noncreating `try_locked` sweep contract. Preserve sorted-root uniqueness, destination-only pending ownership, paired history/receipt semantics and partial-write recovery. A deterministic two-process barrier fixture uses an actual state file and queued kickoff: mailbox owner waits to mark kickoff while the routed sender owns roots immediately before mailbox acquisition. Old source wedges; fixed acquisition unwinds roots, kickoff completes and enqueue retries once to one authoritative destination message/history pair. Cover finite persistent contention, absent mailbox, reverse routes and post-first-write failure without retry/duplicate logs.

Agent consume coverage includes actual stdout production and flush, not merely returning a serialized hook body from inside the permit. Keep the same N permit through hook JSON/body/pointer/hint emission; release on output error with the existing silent-hook/no-replay behavior. A barrier-controlled flushing stream plus a separate exclusive N process must stay excluded until flush completes. Already emitted bytes/context remain outside recall. This extends no native lookup for explicit-name or hook consumers.
