Both shared producer and exclusive controller permits must outlive their exact native child. Intentionally pass only the relevant descriptors to that child, with unrelated descriptors closed. Parent death must leave an orphan controller excluded until the native child ends; parent-only close-on-exec locks are insufficient. Closing a permit in a normal parent must not issue an explicit unlock while a surviving child still owns the shared open-file description. Release disables input, closes/detaches the owned controller, drains/discards output, terminates/reaps that exact child within bounded cleanup, then releases the parent's lock descriptors. If teardown cannot be established, retain exclusion and show that release is incomplete rather than deleting locks or announcing availability. No process-name-wide kill or unrelated child cleanup.

An RPC timeout or child exit does not prove upstream work has stopped. The gate prevents new participating submissions/consumption while active and waits for currently held semantic wrappers; it does not recall already accepted native requests, terminal bytes, earlier hook context or an agent turn. State this limit in the confirmation/help. Do not insert a finite sleep and call it a drain barrier. Late-completion tests must demonstrate and document this exact scope. Old/native/third-party writers are not made cooperative by flock. An enabled watcher that may be live must have positively established participating launch identity: protocol metadata `control_gate_protocol: 1` in its existing PID-owned watch lock, a valid package/build identity and positive matching live process evidence. Missing, invalid, ambiguous or unreadable identity/liveness makes Control unavailable. Installed package identity is not running watcher identity. A positively stopped/absent watcher or a disabled watcher is distinct and does not block. Recheck at prepare/acquire. Pure monitoring and messaging continue when Control is unavailable. Never refresh production watchers automatically as part of a test.

### 45.5 Authenticated lease and transport

Add narrow authenticated POSTs under `/api/control/` for prepare, acquire, stream, input, resize, heartbeat and release. Each route has an exact allowlist schema, same Host/Origin/session/independent anti-forgery checks as owner actions, bounded JSON and no query-string authority. Demo has no native controller or input adapter. Prepare is metadata-only and short-lived: validate an exact registered fleet/seat/view, live terminal identity and present running process, return the confirmation target plus a random one-use nonce. No attach, resize or gate-file creation happens during prepare. Acquire requires that nonce, a per-document in-memory random tab id and a deliberately confirmed action, including the 45.9 native saved-agent resume disclosure; freshly resolve under permits before spawning one fixed `terminal session control TERMINAL --cols N --rows N` child. Never pass `--takeover`, agent attach, shell commands or a browser-provided native target. Native refusal/capacity errors release only this attempt. Observed missing-runtime/restored-pending/ambiguous process state is unavailable. Native attach alone may have the explicitly accepted restore-race effect; no separate plugin start/resume is invoked, and later failure/release is not a rollback.

The lease is random, memory-only and bound to the authenticated session, tab, canonical registered target and observed native identity. Acquisition is not successful merely because the native child spawned: require its first valid full ANSI frame within five seconds and a fresh matching registered/native identity check before exposing active input. A closed/error/unsupported/silent setup tears down the attempt without enabling input. One lease per tab and bounded server-wide capacity (at most four) prevent unattended accumulation; native exclusivity plus cross-process locks decide contention. A repeated acquire nonce cannot spawn a second child, including after uncertain response; a bounded tombstone retains the attempt as used. An acquire response lost in transit is not retried: the client checks no alternate target and waits for expiry or explicitly releases that attempt. The server expires an unattached stream within five seconds. A disconnected authenticated stream revokes its lease; a second stream for it is refused, not a reconnect.

Input and resize share one exact-next per-lease sequence, starting at 1, and one browser submission queue. No second frame is submitted before the previous response is known successful. One bounded dedicated lease worker owns the thread-bound permit, serialized native writer, deadlines and teardown; HTTP handlers submit bounded work and never borrow its permit, while a separate output reader has no write authority. Accept only a bounded UTF-8 text payload (at most 8 KiB including bracketed-paste wrappers), or an explicitly bounded viewport for resize. Reversed HTTP arrival, duplicates or a gap revoke before writing the rejected bytes; the server does not reorder or wait for a missing frame. Reserve the exact sequence before its write; partial/complete write followed by any uncertainty revokes and prevents all subsequent submissions. No replay. Nonblocking bounded stdin and independently scheduled release/deadline signals prevent a full queue, blocked write or native identity check from trapping Release/expiry. Native input uses only `terminal.input` with text encoded as JSON; arbitrary NDJSON, mouse/scroll/native-control commands or argv are unavailable. A successful HTTP write means submitted to the terminal controller, never proof that an agent consumed it. Never store or log text, paste, native output, full HTTP body or lease secrets. Strict finite buffers/backpressure release on overflow; they do not silently lose and continue keyboard input. No automatic retries of acquire/input/resize or silent reacquisition.

Heartbeat at most every two seconds checks session, current registration and fresh native identity, independently of output arrival. Polling is a best-effort stop, not agent-generation atomicity. Missed heartbeat expires after 15 seconds; absolute lease duration is ten minutes and requires a new explicit confirmation. Every input/resize also validates authority and lease state, and serializes against release. Auth loss, target/rebinding/process change observed, hidden/navigated page, stream disconnect, EOF, unsupported/malformed frames, server stop, expiry or deliberate Release disables input and tears down the owned controller. No buffered input survives any of these transitions. Releasing does not submit Enter, clear the prompt, acknowledge/read mail, flush queued delivery, focus a pane or restart anything; later normal watcher cycles follow their existing guards.

### 45.6 Human interaction and terminal output isolation

Watch remains the default, with closed native stdin and a persistent Watch label. From the exact current registered terminal, **Take control** opens a keyboard-accessible confirmation that names fleet, agent and terminal and states: this controls the terminal, it may become a shell after agent exit, sizing changes are real, already accepted work/bytes cannot be recalled, and other native/older writers are outside the plugin hold. It also states the 45.9 process effect: taking control may start/resume the saved agent process if Herdr restores this terminal between validation and attachment, even before input; failed acquisition/Release cannot undo a resume already performed. Explicit Confirm starts acquisition; cancellation has no native effects. Busy/unavailable explanations preserve the current Watch view and drafts. Native dialogs are displayed verbatim as terminal output, without generated Approve/Deny options or interpreted suggestions.

While active, show a persistent **Control** label, exact target, expiry and visible **Release** within the terminal toolbar, including collapsed rail, expanded terminal and narrow layout. Focus enters the terminal only after successful explicit acquisition. Reserve **Ctrl+Shift+X** as a visible, accessible local Release shortcut while Control is active; never forward that combination. Ordinary Escape remains a deliberate terminal key rather than a hidden release shortcut. The local shortcut plus the keyboard-accessible Release button prevents trapping the owner in terminal key handling. Fleet/seat/tab changes, closing/collapsing the terminal, hiding the document and leaving the page first disable input and release. Keep the existing conversation drafts/intents independent; chat Send can enqueue normally but explains when delivery is held by owner control. Do not auto-open or reacquire Control from Desk items, reconnect, preferences, reload or newly observed blocked state. Release has a normal accessible button and returns focus to Take control after confirmed teardown; a teardown failure retains an honest unavailable status.

Forward only intentional trusted keyboard and explicit paste/composition events from the focused controlled terminal. Do not connect `xterm.onData` blindly: terminal output can elicit device/focus replies. Use event-origin filtering on key/paste/composition handlers, suppress composition key duplication, handle committed IME text once, and leave output-generated replies, mouse reporting, focus reporting, automatic clipboard and window requests unforwarded. Browser copy/paste shortcuts stay local: an actual paste event is the sole paste submission, without also forwarding its Command/Ctrl+V key as terminal input. `isTrusted` is an accidental-event guard, not proof of human origin or a substitute for server authority. The first implementation has no terminal mouse-input path. Copy remains local and existing terminal output still renders through the ANSI frame decoder/xterm, never HTML.

Manual paste is plain text, with a total UTF-8 size of at most 8 KiB including any bracketed-paste begin/end wrappers. Reject an oversized event completely before writing, never split or partially submit it. Respect the terminal's live bracketed-paste mode without inferring commands or provider choices. Preserve normal multiline and keyboard behavior; do not let the chat Command/Ctrl+Enter handler steal terminal keys. Local fitting/Watch layout never writes native dimensions. In Control, only explicit human layout/window size changes request bounded native resize; receiving a frame or fitting its reported dimensions cannot trigger a resize feedback loop. All authority/lease/input/target data is transient, excluded from preferences, URL, storage and telemetry. Reduced motion and existing stable attention/conversation reading positions remain respected.

### 45.7 Named safety amendment and implementation sequence

AGENTS bullet 2 retains every automated prohibition and adds only this named exception: **the authenticated owner's explicit section-45 terminal-control lease may forward that owner's intentional terminal input, including their own dialog answers; agents and automation never operate that path or answer dialogs.** Its separately approved native attach effect is disclosed in 45.9 and creates no separate plugin start/resume path. The terminal adapter is a separate validated `Herdr.terminal_control` seam requiring an opaque control permit; `Herdr.agent_send_keys` retains its exact existing automated key allowlist and call paths. Invariants 1, 2, 19 and 21 explicitly scope their new exception to that human lease; invariant 5 names only the approved native pending-resume exception and invariant 23 records its non-rollback semantics. Invariant 12 and all watcher/popup/dry-run restrictions remain unchanged. Section 41's Watch path stays observe-only; its exclusion of writable control is superseded only by section 45's authenticated interface. No CLI flag or agent-callable command grants a human lease.

Implement and review in two dependent stages within 0.23.0: first the coordination module plus complete ordinary-operation/hook coverage, tested with real OS-process fixtures; then the authenticated native controller and browser interaction using those reviewed permits. Keep Control unavailable until both stages pass. The designer makes the rule/spec amendments; the builder owns runtime/test implementation and reports each inventory entry covered. Independent review examines actual coverage and failure paths, not just helper tests. No new dependency or vendor replacement is required. Existing Python 3.8+ compatibility and both Python suite gates remain binding.

### 45.8 Verification gates

Tests use only fake Herdr/native-controller processes and isolated temporary endpoint namespaces, never the owner's socket or fleets. Extend the invariant oracle for exactly the authenticated human seam and require all old automated blocked/key/path failures to remain failures. Demonstrate meaningful red-before-fix cases for duplicate input, output-induced terminal replies, cross-process delivery/lifecycle contention and orphan-controller lifetime. Required actual-process fixtures include endpoint default/env/session/alias agreement, legacy-client ambiguity, two dashboards contending, name-only explicit inbox hooks with zero extra native calls, opposite-order multi-resource operations, nested restart/quit/prompt, mailbox contention, sibling/topology refusal, child descriptor inheritance, parent crash, EOF/blocked output/kill, stuck teardown, late RPC completion, stable namespace across server restart, symlink/hardlink/permission attacks and no inode replacement. Test pure GET/show/history/count/dry-run zero gate-file/native-write behavior and ordinary noncontrolled seats continuing.

Backend/API tests cover strict schemas/auth/target/runtime guards, no hidden takeover or separate start/resume command, the explicitly confirmed 45.9 native effect, nonce reuse/lost responses, sequence replay/order/uncertainty, expiry/release races, stream and authority loss, metadata hygiene and Demo isolation. Specifically exercise known missing/pending runtime refusal with zero attach effects, and running-to-restored/pending replacement after the last preflight read but before attach. In that approved race the fake native attach may resume its saved agent before input; the later fresh identity guard must stop changed/unsupported acquisition without claiming that teardown undoes the resume. Unconfirmed/automatic/retried attachment is still forbidden. Exercise enabled old/unknown/unreadable/current watcher identities, disabled and positively stopped cases; reversed input handlers, input/resize/release races, partial/complete stdin write followed by response loss and zero continuation after uncertainty. Browser acceptance in Chrome and Firefox uses the actual candidate Live engine with fake Herdr: explicit confirmation/keyboard/paste/IME, rejected synthetic/output-induced input, held plugin delivery then ordinary guarded recovery, real resize request only in Control, Release, target/page/hidden changes, draft preservation, lost response/no retry, overlapping clicks, collapse/expansion and 1440/1024/375 layouts. Inspector screenshots and test logs state browser versions and which human/platform behavior was simulated.

Designer native acceptance uses only a disposable cheap Claude seat in a scratch fleet for fresh identity, passive Watch, runtime guards and teardown. After automated gates and independent review pass, the OWNER manually performs a real no-input acquire/release of the pinned CLI adapter in an owned scratch context to verify readiness, inherited descriptor lifetime, detach/EOF and actual resize behavior, with the approved 45.9 disclosure visible. An agent does not acquire a real human lease even without typing. Passive Watch and fake controllers alone do not verify that adapter. Agents never type into a real blocked agent or answer its dialog. The owner's own approval-answer interaction, if needed to establish the final human flow, is a concrete manual acceptance step after all automated checks and independent review; document it honestly rather than automate it. A provider quota does not justify switching billing, answering a menu or claiming a fresh answer. Record actual native behavior/limits and cleanup before the final commit. No push/install/production watcher refresh is authorised by implementation or test completion; release publication still needs the owner's explicit word.

### 45.9 Native restored-session attachment — separately approved

Independent review verified a further limitation in the fixed native CLI: attaching Control calls the server's pending-agent-resume helper. The helper skips resume when a runtime exists at that instant, but a server replacement between the last read and attachment can restore the terminal with a pending plan. The new connection can then resume that saved agent before browser input. Neither plugin locks, a process-info read, a first frame nor a later identity check can prevent or undo that native effect. Primary implementation: [attach calls resume](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/server/headless.rs#L1834), [runtime-present condition](https://github.com/herdrdev/herdr/blob/2ecf19a92fc3a7fff75b6ce3655ef04f4625ec01/src/app/agent_resume.rs#L179). This is source-level evidence, not a live resume experiment.

On 11 November the owner separately approved allowing this disclosed native restore behavior. This replaces the earlier no-hidden-resume prerequisite only with the explicit Take control confirmation: **taking control may start/resume the saved agent process if Herdr restores this terminal between validation and attachment, even before any input. A failed acquisition or Release does not undo a resume already performed.** The plugin still refuses a target already observed missing/pending, never invokes a separate resume/start command, never retries acquisition or takes control automatically, and stops on subsequently observed identity change. This permits only the native attach effect following the owner's deliberate confirmation, including its unavoidable validation/attach race; it grants agents no new lifecycle, dialog-answer or human-lease authority. All permit, authentication, input, cleanup and manual native acceptance gates remain. Invariant 7.5 names only that exception, invariant 23 records its effect, and 45.2/45.5/45.7/45.8 implement it consistently. This authorises no separate plugin start/resume command and no agent-operated lease. The earlier approvals for human input and terminal-level shell/resize semantics remain valid; no further scope decision is outstanding for these native semantics.

### 45.10 Stage-one verification checkpoint

The coordination stage was independently approved at a frozen 37-path candidate before any controller, human permit or Control route was implemented. Module review independently passed 18 process/resolver cases and reproduced meaningful negative controls for thread-identifier reuse, cumulative descriptor growth and child descriptor inheritance. Whole-stage review found and corrected three integration defects: coordination-held `--now` requests were disposed instead of kept queued, selected-ID reads created gate files before pure validation, and a client-pair refusal escaped the ordinary Watch error stream. The final review passed 202 focused checks on Python 3.9.6, rechecked the original independent CLI/Watch probes and verified all frozen hashes. No remaining stage-one blocker was reported; this was not final product approval.

The builder's final focused runs passed 231 section-40–45 checks and 337 affected-operation checks on Python 3.9.6, all 59 section-45 checks on Python 3.12.13 and the existing 48 JavaScript cases. These overlapping groups are not complete-suite counts. The complete two-Python gates were deliberately deferred to the entire section's final freeze. Separate regressions exercised actual isolated processes for the routed root/mailbox cycle and hook-output flush boundary: the earlier implementation wedged the controlled lock-cycle case and released the name hold before flush; the corrections complete without duplicate queue/history effects and retain exclusion through output. Current candidate audit covered 139 public files, 107 Python/launcher grammar parses targeting Python 3.8, zero selected private-pattern findings and five unchanged vendor hashes. Grammar parsing is not a Python 3.8 runtime test; pattern checks are not a proof of all possible privacy issues.

The designer then used the frozen candidate with Herdr 0.9.5 and one disposable cheap Claude seat in an isolated scratch fleet, with no watcher or kickoff. Bring-up exercised its permitted second start after a fresh bare-shell read. Five checks passed: native bring-up without a work prompt, exact candidate observation, a real passive full ANSI-frame envelope, retained native identity, and no human lease, dialog answer or agent work prompt. The owned observer stopped; candidate shutdown quit that idle scratch agent and closed only its recorded created workspace after fresh ownership/single-pane checks. The agent was verified absent. All 37 reviewed hashes remained unchanged after the native check. No owner fleet/job was used, and no controller was acquired or input feature accepted by these checks. Stage two and the owner-only native adapter test remain required before the section's final commit; no publication is authorised by this checkpoint.

### 45.11 Rendering lifetime and passive Watch continuity

Independent frontend review reproduced that xterm's asynchronous write queue survives `reset()`: an earlier target's pending bytes can render after the screen is reset for a new selection. A guarded completion callback prevents stale input activation but does not discard bytes already handed to the renderer. The same review queued 100 valid frames before any parse callback, beyond the application's stated output-buffer bound. These are display-integrity and backpressure defects, not native input injection. The following refines 41.6/45.5/45.6 before the correction is implemented; it grants no input or lifecycle authority.

Watch and Control must share one generation-owned rendering boundary for writes, resets and frame sizing. Permit at most one outstanding vendor parse, with each validated payload at most 196,608 bytes. Bound any application-side waiting work to at most 32 frames and 1 MiB in total; a stricter no-extra-queue policy is acceptable. Await parsing rather than continuing to drain an arbitrary number of frames into xterm's internal buffer. An unavailable/stalled renderer ends the affected stream finitely and keeps input disabled; do not silently drop output and continue input as though the displayed target were current. Keep the pinned vendor bundles unchanged and use their public completion interface.

Release, selection/stream changes and authority loss invalidate the old render generation synchronously. They disable input and signal controller release immediately, independently of waiting for output to drain. Prevent an old queued write from appearing beneath a newly selected target: hide or fence the terminal surface while an outstanding old parse completes, then reset before exposing the next generation. The exact Control target/status and Release remain available during this transition. Every full-frame reset must follow prior parse completion. New stream frames and input readiness belong to the current render generation as well as the existing lease/selection/controller guards; a late callback cannot expose old content or enable a newer lease. Do not claim `reset()` cancels writes already queued inside xterm.

While that surface is fenced, disable Copy screen and keep its previous-generation buffer unavailable to the terminal's accessible presentation. Copy/readiness is restored only for a parsed current-generation frame. Cancellation of a Control attempt that never owned rendering must not invalidate the healthy Watch generation it was preserving. The renderer's ownership boundary applies to copy/presentation as well as input readiness; it does not grant a new clipboard or native-input path.

Hiding the document revokes pending or active human Control. With Control idle, it preserves an otherwise healthy passive Watch under section 41's existing authority/target/disconnect guards and hidden-page polling behavior. Cancellation before successful acquisition also preserves an existing Watch. Actual page departure, explicit disconnect and target changes retain their existing teardown behavior; ending Control never automatically reconnects Watch. No separate passive-Watch policy change is intended.

Regression coverage must include the actual shipped vendor's delayed old-write/reset/new-target behavior, bounded callback backpressure, Watch/Control transitions, immediate input disable/release while parsing is delayed, stale callbacks that cannot reactivate, and hidden/return with active Watch but no Control. Record which probes use the actual vendor and which use logical fixtures. Recheck integrated Chrome/Firefox transitions after this shared rendering correction; earlier keyboard, layout and backend evidence remains scoped to its recorded bytes.

### 45.12 Usable terminal expansion in short windows

The owner-only native pilot established Control and Release, but the owner reported that Expand / Restore did not change the terminal area. A designer reproduction with the shipped Live page and fake Control found the same visible failure at a 1,024 × 600 pixel viewport: the trusted click reached Expand, the expanded state and Restore label changed, but the terminal remained 722 × 360 pixels. At the previously tested 900-pixel height, the terminal did grow. A native resize request or an expanded class alone therefore does not establish usable expansion. This refinement precedes the layout correction and grants no additional native authority.

Expand must provide a visibly larger, focused terminal presentation for the default 360-pixel terminal in a short window, including 1,024 × 600 pixels. Make room by stepping surrounding content aside rather than leaving the normal terminal's minimum-height clamp as the expanded result. Keep the exact target, Watch/Control status, Restore and active Control's Release visible and reachable without overlap at narrow and short sizes. Restore must return to the previous layout and chosen terminal height. For a chosen normal height already larger than the available viewport, the focused expanded view may fit the viewport instead of growing beyond it; it must still be a distinct usable presentation with an explicit way back. Expansion remains temporary, preserves drafts and selection, and must not silently change the saved normal height or fleet-rail preference.

Measure usable visible terminal content, not just the outer rectangle. At the short-window default-height case, expansion must increase the drawable terminal content height actually visible within the viewport, excluding toolbar/padding and Control-bar occlusion. A larger off-screen box or wrapper alone fails. Restore recovers the prior normal geometry at the unchanged viewport.

Keep active-Control rejection and error feedback visibly rendered and available to assistive technology in the focused view, including a paste rejected by the existing 8 KiB limit. Reclaiming space from normal instructions must not hide every active status message. Rejection must remain all-or-nothing with no native input, and rendering feedback must not itself submit a native resize. Verify both the script's rejection and the actual stylesheet's visible feedback; a text-content assertion alone is insufficient.

Retain the existing authority boundary: Watch expansion changes only its read-only presentation/observer viewport; only an explicit human layout interaction during active Control may request native resize. Do not reconnect or acquire Control as a side effect, change target, submit input or introduce a resize feedback loop. Preserve the shared renderer, stale-output fence and persistent same-node Control/Release controls.

Expand/Restore are presentation changes, not closing/collapsing the terminal under 45.6. They retain the selected target and active Control lease; passive Watch retains its existing read-only viewport/reconnect policy. Keep the focused control available and keyboard-reachable. Surrounding controls hidden by expansion must also leave the tab/accessibility order while hidden. Restore recovers the prior reading/scroll position without discarding drafts or moving selection. Ordinary Escape from the focused controlled terminal remains native, and Ctrl+Shift+X remains local Release. Cover keyboard activation and Tab reachability as well as pointer hit testing.

Regression acceptance must measure the actual rendered terminal rectangle before Expand, after Expand and after Restore, and use real browser hit testing for both buttons. Cover Watch and fake Control, widths 375, 1,024 and 1,440 pixels, a 600-pixel short window as well as the existing 900-pixel height, both fleet-rail states and representative chosen normal heights. Verify native resize only in Control and current-frame fitting after it, target/Release/Restore reachability, preserved drafts/preferences and restored geometry. The designer's isolated fake-Live reproduction is a failing baseline; earlier 40/38 browser passes remain evidence for their tested bytes and scenarios, not acceptance of this correction. Recheck the corrected layout in Chrome and Firefox, obtain independent review, and have the owner verify the corrected native expansion before the final commit.

### 45.13 Final verification and native evidence

The final corrected candidate passed the complete suites serially on Python 3.9.6 and Python 3.12.13: 1,640 tests in 159.594 s and 98.680 s respectively. Each suite increased from 1,542 to 1,640 tests. All 66 JavaScript tests passed on Node 26.3.0 in 264.162 ms, compared with 48 cases before this section. Compilation, five production JavaScript syntax checks and whitespace checks passed. These full-suite results are builder executions. Independent review separately exercised the coordination/controller groups, the JavaScript cases and the source comparisons; its final current-source JavaScript run passed all 66 cases. Focused groups overlap the complete suites and are not additional unique-test totals. The public-tree audit covered 146 files, 112 Python/launcher grammar parses targeting Python 3.8, zero findings for the selected private/credential patterns and five unchanged vendor bundles/licences. Grammar parsing is not a Python 3.8 runtime run; pattern checks neither prove the absence of every private detail nor sanitize Git history.

Integrated acceptance used the actual candidate Live server, page, controller and CLI with isolated fake Herdr/agents. The corrected expansion matrix passed 76 checks in Chrome 157.0.8103.39 and 76 in Firefox 159.0.1 on an immutable 146-file snapshot whose hashes matched before and after. It measured viewport-visible drawable content, real trusted Expand clicks and keyboard Restore, actual click recipients, target/Release reachability, native resize only during Control, retention of the same controller, drafts/preferences, rail states, saved heights and restored reading position. Widths 375, 1,024 and 1,440 pixels and heights 600 and 900 pixels were represented. The fit comparison allows five CSS pixels for borders/subpixel discrepancies; default wide-window growth is asserted separately. Cosmetic setup invokes existing handlers with explicit preconditions and is not a pointer-access claim. Screenshots were inspected at desktop, short and narrow sizes. The complete prior Control acceptance was then rerun on the matching final working-tree revision: 40 Chrome and 38 Firefox checks passed, with all 51 reviewed source hashes unchanged before/after. These browser groups overlap and are not presented as unique-case totals. All owned fake terminal children and services stopped.

The broader browser checks cover trusted keyboard input, rejection of synthetic events and output-induced device replies, queued CLI delivery during Control followed by normal guarded recovery, explicit resize without output feedback, response loss without replay/reacquisition, target/navigation/hidden-page teardown, healthy passive Watch continuity and the shared renderer's stale-output/Copy fence. Chrome's two additional cases intercept successful acquire/input responses; Firefox does not provide that interception. The delayed old-target rendering case holds a write at the public vendor interface before later flushing it through the actual vendor; its timing is simulated, while independent review's unwrapped vendor experiment directly established that queued writes survive reset. Hidden-page state is simulated. Positive paste/composition checks use logical or supplied-origin fixtures, not physical clipboard/IME interaction. Rejection visibility is checked against the actual stylesheet with supplied rendered status; the shipped handler's whole-event rejection and zero submitted input are exercised separately. Firefox Tab traversal is performed last because exiting page content for browser chrome is valid; an earlier fixture incorrectly assumed content focus afterward. The corrected focused keyboard-Restore case passes without a product workaround. Firefox used an owned application copy and fresh private profiles after the installed application failed before browser startup; the installed application and owner profiles were not repaired or changed.

Meaningful red evidence preceded the corrections: isolated negative controls catch duplicate input, descriptor inheritance/lifetime, parent-death delivery exclusion, output-induced replies and removal of the actual renderer's drain boundary. For 45.12, the owner's initial expansion failure was reproduced with the shipped page/CSS/click path, including four independently failing Watch/Control and native-frame-size cases. A new shipped-script regression failed with the old authority-node/scroll behavior and passed after the correction. Further actual-CSS checks rejected oversized input with zero native submission but exposed its hidden status; trusted-pointer checks showed the fixed status bar intercepting Expand in a narrow normal layout. Both defects were corrected and independently reviewed before final acceptance. These controlled negative copies did not mutate the shared candidate. Earlier weak geometry checks, moving-source runs and failed fixture preconditions are not counted as final frozen acceptance.

Native evidence used disposable cheap Claude seats in isolated scratch fleets on Herdr 0.9.5, without watchers, kickoffs or agent work prompts. The first owner-operated Control cycle was observed holding ordinary producer/topology gates with one exact owned native controller; the observer then established controller disappearance and free gates. That pilot exposed the original expansion failure, and its scratch agent/workspace were cleaned. The final backend, native adapter and permit bytes are identical to that observed cycle. After the frontend correction, the owner manually confirmed that Control activated, Expand enlarged the terminal with content fitting, Restore returned its previous size and Release completed. No physical typing/paste result was reported. Agents did not operate a real human lease or answer a native dialog.

The corrected pilot's additional descriptor observer stopped before capturing its held phase because its private launcher helper recorded an identity before Apple's Python launcher completed its executable handoff. The recorded hash was reconstructed exactly from the unchanged UID/start prefix and fixed launch arguments with the launcher executable; the ready framework executable had a different hash. A narrowly reviewed private repair records identity after the ready launch message and validates only that exact transition, preserving the old evidence. It does not relax the public process-identity guard or certify a lease. After the owner requested autonomous completion, no further owner cycle was required for the already completed functional UI check. Supplementary autonomous observation launched only a real read-only Herdr agent-wait client with closed stdin and four ordinary temporary files: the native client retained the inherited file descriptors and the owned reader was terminated/reaped. This verifies native executable inheritance mechanics, not the Control branch's permit-file descriptors. A direct native Control four-descriptor snapshot remains **uncaptured**. Actual parent-crash survival and open-file-description/lock-mode identity are not claimed by this native observation; the crash/lifetime evidence remains the isolated process fixtures. The earlier captured native hold/free/teardown cycle, the owner's corrected UI pass and these supplementary checks retain their separate scopes.

Autonomous final cleanup required the owner's recorded Release report, fresh exact scratch identity, an ordinary producer/topology exclusion that fails closed for an active human lease, no owned native controller, and the exact dashboard fingerprint. The owned service was stopped and its positive PID disappearance plus launcher acknowledgement established before exclusion was released. Only post-report free-gate/controller-absence evidence was recorded; no missing held-cycle or descriptor success was fabricated. Ordinary guarded candidate shutdown then rechecked the created single-pane scratch workspace, quit its idle agent through the existing safe path and established structured agent-not-found and workspace-not-found results. Both owner-pilot scratch configurations, their dashboard services and their owned observer clients are cleaned. No owner fleet/job or fleet-file edit was used as an acceptance fixture. The terminal-level scope, cooperative-writer limits, inability to recall accepted work/bytes, best-effort process observation and explicitly approved native restore effect of 45.9 remain unchanged. Publication and production watcher refresh still require the owner's separate push authorization.

## 46. Deterministic watcher process identity (0.23.1)

The owner asked to continue improving after the release status identified a confirmed cross-locale Control availability defect. This section scopes that correction only. The existing fingerprint hashes the locale-sensitive `ps` start-time display: the same process can produce different digests under different date locales. A changed timezone or output-width context can likewise affect a formatted representation. A valid participating watcher can consequently be rejected as unverified. The correction must preserve the fail-closed identity check, not substitute a PID-only check or accept guessed legacy representations. Unresolved provider menus, native agent attribution, focus-loss policy and terminal authority remain outside this change.

### 46.1 One deterministic, bounded representation

`process_identity.identity` remains the single shared implementation for capture and verification. For a positive integer PID owned by the effective user, run the fixed read-only `/bin/ps -ww -p PID -o uid=,lstart=,command=` query with closed stdin, captured stdout, suppressed stderr and the existing one-second timeout. Use a child-only environment copied from the caller with `LC_ALL=C`, `LC_TIME=C`, `LANG=C` and `TZ=UTC0`; remove inherited `COLUMNS` and explicitly request unlimited width with `-ww`. Do not mutate the caller's environment or locale. The POSIX UTC0 timezone does not depend on a caller-selected local timezone. Retain the output-size limit of 65,536 bytes, nonempty-output and effective-UID validation, unknown/error/timeout refusal and the existing best-effort process-generation semantics.

Define the identity scheme as the exact string `ps-c-utc-v1`. Hash `b"ps-c-utc-v1\0"` followed by the successful output stripped only at its outer boundary. The retained output includes UID, start time and complete command arguments; no raw command, environment or process output is persisted or exposed by the helper. The explicit domain prefix distinguishes this recipe from the previous unversioned digest. Do not normalize arbitrary localized timestamps, search candidate locales, use process titles alone or fall back to an old recipe.

### 46.2 Explicit watcher compatibility

New watcher lock metadata records `process_identity_scheme` equal to that shared scheme alongside its existing PID, launch identity, protocol and digest. Keep `control_gate_protocol` at 1 because the producer/lease coordination protocol is unchanged. A current dashboard requires the exact recognized scheme before accepting a live enabled watcher's digest, then retains all existing metadata ownership, file bounds, liveness, launch, fleet and exact-digest checks. A missing, malformed or unknown scheme refuses Control with actionable guidance to restart the watcher using the current plugin. An unknown current digest still refuses; an already positively dead watcher retains its existing distinct treatment.

Do not rewrite a running watcher's lock, infer its scheme from a coincidentally matching hash, restart watchers or dashboards automatically, or acquire a human lease to test this correction. A deployment must refresh legacy watcher processes through the existing owner-authorized guarded release routine; an existing dashboard process also retains its original running build until explicitly restarted by its owner. Document that legacy metadata can remain visible/running for ordinary fleet operations while Control is unavailable. Watch and ordinary queue/lifecycle semantics do not change. There is no new key, prompt, native control or caller-identity path.

The restart guidance must reach the authenticated browser's existing Control-prepare error presentation, not be discarded by the asynchronous identity probe or replaced by the generic action error. Preserve only a dedicated fixed, body-free upgrade-required condition through the read-only probe and authenticated prepare refusal. The HTTP error carries one hard-coded instruction to restart the legacy/unsupported watcher with the current plugin; never serialize arbitrary exception text, metadata, paths, commands or provider content. Keep all other errors generic and preserve authentication, origin, request-schema, cancellation/expiry and zero-controller behavior. An unauthenticated, invalid or cancelled request must not gain watcher details. This is a narrow existing-error-response change, with no new route, request authority or frontend asset change. Add direct probe and actual authenticated HTTP regressions, including unrelated sensitive exceptions remaining generic.

### 46.3 Regression and acceptance gates

Add regressions against the real shared identity function that first fail on the released implementation: capture and verify under different inherited locale/timezone/column environments must yield one digest for one unchanged process, while different UID/start/command evidence and unknown/error/timeout/oversized output are refused or distinguished as appropriate. Assert exact fixed argv and child environment, untouched parent environment, the domain prefix, and positive integer PID validation. Use controlled fake process output for deterministic environment-sensitive negative controls and a real isolated child where available; record a platform limitation rather than inventing a pass when process inspection is unavailable.

Exercise watcher capture and dashboard eligibility together. Current scheme plus matching current digest passes; missing, unknown and incorrectly typed schemes fail even if the supplied digest would otherwise match. Different process evidence, wrong fleet, invalid launch/protocol, unreadable metadata and existing dead-process handling retain their coverage. A legacy running watcher must not be silently upgraded by either observation or a failed Control check. Update relevant existing fixtures to explicitly represent current or legacy metadata, not weaken assertions to accommodate the new field.

The builder implements this section, version declarations, focused regressions and the two complete Python suites. Independent review checks source and specification. The designer then verifies the frozen candidate in a disposable scratch fleet with a cheap Claude seat and the actual candidate watcher: capture under one locale/timezone/column context and call the read-only eligibility check under another, preserving the exact native seat identity and watcher metadata bytes. No real human lease, input, dialog answer or production fleet is used. Record actual results and limits in a verification amendment before committing. No frontend behavior changes are intended, so reuse the earlier browser evidence only for unchanged assets; do not claim new browser or physical-input coverage. Publication and production rollout require a new explicit owner push authorization for this release.

### 46.4 Verification

The frozen correction passed both complete suites serially: 1,658 tests on Python 3.9.6 in 290.860 s and 1,658 on Python 3.12.13 in 257.662 s, increasing coverage from 1,640 to 1,658 methods per runtime. All 66 existing Node cases passed in 603.762 ms; the count is unchanged. These complete-suite results are builder executions. Independent source review separately passed 38 focused cases on Python 3.9.6 in 21.489 s, including the new identity/guidance tests, existing authenticated HTTP checks and process-evidence cases. These groups overlap the full suites and are not additional unique-test totals.

Meaningful failing baselines preceded the fixes. The released identity function failed the cross-environment regression. Scheme capture and legacy compatibility produced two failures across three tests before the metadata/eligibility correction. The read-only probe and actual local HTTP guidance tests produced two clean failures across three cases before the dedicated fixed condition was propagated. Independent review also ran the released identity in memory and observed assertion failures in both environment invariance and exact-recipe cases without modifying the shared source. Other negative controls were exercised green, rather than each individually proved red.

The final regressions cover exact fixed argv and child environment, parent-environment preservation, a literal domain-prefixed digest, positive integer PIDs, UID/start/complete-command evidence, internal spacing, empty/failed/timed-out/oversized output, strict scheme values, watcher capture, unchanged lock bytes and ordinary legacy monitoring. An isolated owned Python child with a readiness barrier and long synthetic arguments supplies real process evidence across inherited contexts without a Herdr endpoint. Actual loopback HTTP with fake Herdr proves that authenticated prepare returns the fixed restart instruction without a nonce, controller or gate effects, while unrelated sensitive exceptions, authentication/origin/schema failures, cancellation and authority expiry retain their refusal/privacy behavior. These are process and HTTP checks, not new physical browser or native-input coverage.

The designer then ran the pinned candidate with one disposable cheap Claude seat and an actual candidate watcher in an isolated scratch fleet. Candidate bring-up used no kickoff, work prompt or registered production watcher. A passive native ANSI frame and the exact native seat mapping were observed. The owned candidate watcher captured its current scheme while inheriting an Australian locale, a Pacific timezone and 40-column preference. Three fresh read-only eligibility subprocesses passed under a C locale/UTC/300-column context, a British locale/Asian timezone/60-column context and the capture context. The owned watcher remained alive before and after every query and immediately before certification, so success did not use the positively-dead-watcher branch. Watcher lock bytes and modification time were unchanged, and the exact scratch native identity was retained. No human lease, controller, agent work prompt or dialog answer was used. This establishes native watcher capture/read-only eligibility across those contexts; it does not claim a new real Control cycle.

The watcher child was signalled, waited and positively absent before scratch cleanup. Cleanup held the ordinary workspace exclusion, rechecked the recorded created single-pane workspace and native identity, closed only that owned workspace without a quit prompt or dialog answer, and established structured agent-not-found and workspace-not-found results. All 147 public candidate file hashes matched before and after the native check. No owner fleet, production job, fleet configuration or installed registration was used as a test target or changed.

Compilation and whitespace checks passed. Compilation initially met a sandbox denial at the configured global bytecode cache, then passed with the approved cache access; no cache-routing or runtime change was used as a workaround. The public audit covered 147 files and 113 Python/launcher grammar parses targeting Python 3.8, with zero findings for the selected private/credential patterns, unchanged first three repository rules and section 7, and all five vendor hashes verified. All 13 frontend files are byte-identical to the base release, so no new browser run or frontend acceptance is claimed. Grammar parsing is not a real Python 3.8 runtime run; selected-pattern checks do not prove absence of every private detail or sanitize history.

The scheme is deliberately incompatible with unversioned live watcher metadata. Legacy or unknown formats still require an explicit current-plugin watcher restart, and an already-running dashboard retains its original code until its owner restarts it. No automatic metadata migration, locale search, lock rewrite, watcher refresh or permission relaxation was introduced. Process evidence remains best effort rather than an atomic agent-generation guarantee. The one-second query timeout and 65,536-byte refusal bound remain, with no new runtime dependency. Independent final source and evidence review returned MERGE. Publishing, installation and production rollout still require the owner's separate explicit push instruction for this release.
